## Spec (what & why)

The owner's requirements (2026-10-10), in product terms:

- **R1 — The host is the proxy.** Cross-app data flows (read AND write) go through the runner/host, never app-to-app, so apps stay sandboxed while the host facilitates everything.
- **R2 — Scheduled work runs where the app lives.** When an app is open, the host asks THAT iframe to run the scheduled job; when it is closed, the host executes the job itself where it can (without instantiating the app), or in the background when app code is required. Never a second copy beside the open one.
- **R3 — The host acts on the user's behalf.** The runner/hub controls many apps, executes, reads and writes for the user, within every policy and permission the user granted.
- **R4 — Fresh data on demand.** The host can make a source app PULL new data (through that app's own connections), not only serve what it already stores.
- **R5 — All of Group 1**, the fourteen deferrals ADR-0075 §10 named; the result must be world-class and intuitive, with the complexity and the security abstracted from the user.

What is true today, so the plan corrects the right thing (read from the code 2026-10-10 — anchors in "Found while planning"):

1. **The host already IS the proxy for reads.** A reader never touches a source's bytes: the frame goes to the runner's value-blind seat, the host handler exports the source, a dedicated Worker makes a scoped copy, runs the one SELECT and the rows come back capped. R1's read half is built; what is missing is that **only app code can use it** — the chat beside an app and the scheduler cannot (deferred by name in ADR-0075 §10 and SPEC §25), and nothing writes.
2. **An unattended scheduled run ALWAYS mounts a hidden frame, even when the app is open** (`appRun.ts:311-319` decides on the trigger alone). That is the two-copy behaviour the owner saw; it is threat-model R-70 and the "open copy" items in next-steps. Only the user's own *Run now* rides the live frame.
3. **Scheduled writes are refused by construction** (ADR-0074 §6): a mutating connected call answers `NET_CONFIRM_DENIED`, the AI's proposals wait for the user. Standing approvals exist as a gate with no arming surface (ADR-0033).
4. **Nothing pulls fresh data on another app's behalf.**

**Goals**

- **G1 — One broker, three doors.** ONE host-side Access Service with ONE policy function serves app code (frames), the chat beside an app (the data lane) and the scheduler (steps). The same grant, the same log, the same disclosure, whichever door asks. The user never learns there are doors.
- **G2 — Writes are brokered, bounded, journaled, undoable.** A write grant is the same consent sheet with one more toggle; every brokered write passes one path (strict parse → dry run → cap → busy check → undo snapshot → transaction → history line → doorbell) and the source's history offers *undo* for seven days. Whole-table writes and writes too large to undo are refused, by name.
- **G3 — One instance per app.** A run executes where the app lives: in the live frame when the app is open (under a run-scoped gate that asks the PRESENT user, never a remembered approval), headless when it is closed, and a run follows the app when that changes mid-flight. Host-native work (think, read, fetch, bounded write) needs no frame at all.
- **G4 — Fresh on demand.** A reader (app, chat or schedule) may ask the host to have the source pull new data first; the host runs the source where it lives, under the unattended posture, once per cooldown, counted and logged.
- **G5 — Discovery with permission.** Apps learn a count class, never names; the user sees ranked candidates in the sheet; the Hub and Settings surface "apps that could work together"; a source can offer itself; an app can DECLARE at build time what it would like to read, and the host turns that into a one-tap allow.
- **G6 — Everything in the file, nothing new on the wire beyond one minor.** Grants, history, undo and declarations are namespaced settings rows; spec 1.2 is additive; PR-1, PR-2, PR-4, PR-5 change no schema bytes.
- **G7 — Honest and plain.** Every surface says what will happen in words a non-engineer reads in one breath; every unattended act is refused, asked or journaled — never silent.
- **G8 — Shippable in six reviewable PRs**, each walkable on its own, the starter wave owner-timed.

### The model, in Chrome's terms (the owner asked for the Chrome architect's view)

| Chrome | Snug | Where it lands here |
|---|---|---|
| The browser process brokers every privileged operation; renderers hold nothing | The host page brokers every cross-app read/write/refresh; iframes hold their own data only | Access Service (`access/service.ts`), one policy (`access/policy.ts`) |
| Mojo IPC | Frames | `snug:access-request` gains `write` · `refresh` · `discover` |
| Site permissions: asked in context, shown in the omnibox chip, managed in chrome://settings | Access grants: the strip + sheet, the ⋈ header, the Settings card | Consent sheet v2 (one more toggle per table) |
| Extensions declare permissions at install, request optional ones at runtime | An app DECLARES what it would like to read (install-time), ASKS at runtime after a user act | `<meta name="snug:access-wants">` → `accessWants:<appId>` |
| One renderer per tab; the browser decides where code runs | One instance per app; the host decides where a run executes | Run placement (`schedule/runPlacement.ts`) |
| A service worker runs without a tab, woken by the browser | The headless frame — used ONLY when the app is closed | `ScheduledRunHost` stays; coexistence ends |
| Background Sync / Periodic Background Sync | The scheduler, plus refresh-on-demand | `access/refresh.ts` |
| Undo close tab / history | The source's history with *undo* on every brokered write | `accessUndo:<sourceAppId>` |

### Principles the plan stands on

- **P1 One broker, three doors.** Nothing cross-app happens outside `access/service.ts`; the frame handler, the data-lane tools and the scheduler steps are CALLERS with a declared attendance and egress, not engines.
- **P2 Declarations propose; only the user grants** (ADR-0016). Declarations travel in bundles; grants never do.
- **P3 One instance per app; the host chooses the executor.** Live frame if open and announced; headless if closed; handover when that changes; host-native work needs neither.
- **P4 Host-native where possible.** Read, think, fetch (GET) and bounded write are host-native; app code runs only for app-authored handlers.
- **P5 One write path.** Every write not typed by the user's own hands inside the app goes through the Write Broker: strict, dry-run, capped, busy-checked, journaled with an undo plan, doorbelled.
- **P6 Caps sit at the egress, not at the grant.** The frame's 500 rows / 192 KiB exist because of the bridge; an in-host materialisation into the chat's scratch copy gets its own, larger cap; the brain only ever sees `data_query`'s capped result.
- **P7 Presence is not consent — but a present user can be asked.** A delegated run never consults a remembered or standing approval; it asks the user who is there, with a timeout to *needs you*.
- **P8 Hints, never content** (ADR-0034). Every new host-event carries ids and names, never rows.
- **P9 Disclosure is derived.** "Where this can go" is computed from the transports that will carry the data, per door.

- The hidden frame is `ScheduledRunHost.tsx:37-67`, mounted once at `App.tsx:360`; its db binding `scheduledDbDriver.ts:35` refuses BEGIN/COMMIT/…/`import`; its gate `scheduledConfirmGate.ts:43` always refuses; its transport `scheduledTransport.ts:48` counts and caps; `onNetCall` is `state/net.ts:459`.

### Owner questions — recommended defaults (the owner delegated the architecture 2026-10-10: "I want you to be the judge"; each default is reversible by one word and is recorded in ADR-0076 / ADR-0077)

| # | Decision | Default taken | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Where cross-app value is delivered | **One Access Service with three doors** — app frames, the chat beside an app, the scheduler — over ONE policy function; today's frame handler becomes the first caller | R1/R3: the host is the agent of record, not only a router; the user never learns there are doors |
| Q2 | How the chat's brain sees another app's data | **Materialise the granted tables THROUGH the Worker into the data lane's scratch copy** as `<alias>__<table>` (JOINs work: "spending vs budget"); not a separate query tool | The product value is the join; every protection (drops, withheld, masked, drift, `query_only`, the clock) still runs in the Worker before a row exists on the main thread; the scratch is already a throwaway (ADR-0019) |
| Q3 | Writes | **A standing write grant per table** — one toggle *can also change rows* (add, change, remove as one); ≤ 200 rows per statement; whole-table writes and writes too large to undo are refused by name; every write journaled with an undo plan, undo offered for 7 days | Frictionless after consent (the owner's bar) with ADR-0033's shape: explicit, frozen scope, host guardrails, disclosed, journaled, instantly revocable — and reversible, which ADR-0033 never had |
| Q4 | Who may write unattended | **The app's `write` op only when ATTENDED** (the visible frame of its generation) **or inside a scheduled run whose task carries an armed write authority**; chat writes stay per-approval cards (the user is there); the scheduler applies the AI's proposals only under the task's authority, else they wait as today | "No new powers" (ADR-0074 §6) is kept as the default and widened only by one explicit arming act the user can see and kill |
| Q5 | Execution model | **One instance per app.** A run is delegated to the live frame when the app is open and announced; headless when closed; a run FOLLOWS the app (handover both ways, same `runId`, at most once) | R2; R-70 retires for the delegated case; the KB already requires handlers idempotent by `runId` |
| Q6 | The delegated run's net gate | **A run-scoped ask gate**: while a run is in flight on the live frame, a mutating connected call never consults the session-remember or standing gates; it asks the PRESENT user in the ordinary dialog, naming the schedule; no answer in 60 s → `NET_CONFIRM_DENIED` → *needs you* | Honors "presence is not consent" (lesson `:211`) without the hidden frame: a human clicks, or nothing happens |
| Q7 | Counting on the live frame | **Count, never refuse mid-run**; the daily ceiling is enforced BEFORE dispatch (`capped` without dispatch); over-attribution of a user's own click inside the ≤ 90 s window is accepted and disclosed | Refusing a user's own click because a scheduled run is in flight is the wrong failure |
| Q8 | Host-native jobs ("without instantiating the app") | **`app-think` gains `tools: true`** — the data lane's `data_query` + `data_propose_write`, a GET-only `fetch_connection`, the shared tables; ≤ 8 tool calls a run; proposals apply only under the task's write authority. Available where the data lane is (BYOK direct brains); elsewhere the step runs tool-free with the shared tables and says so | Reuses the one data lane rather than inventing a declarative job language; ADR-0074 rejected the lift as "a refactor nobody needs" — R2/R3 need it now |
| Q9 | Fresh data on demand (R4) | **`op: 'refresh'`** + a `refresh` tick on the grant (*may ask <Source> to fetch new data first*): the host runs the SOURCE where it lives with `input {kind:'refresh', requestedBy}` over the schedule handshake; one refresh per source per 5 min (else `ok, fresh:false`); GET-only under the unattended gate; counted; logged `refreshed`; the chat and the scheduler get *fetch new data first* | R4 verbatim; a refresh is a run, so Q5's placement is what makes it safe while the source is open |
| Q10 | Discovery to apps | **`op: 'discover'` → `candidates: 'none' \| 'one' \| 'several'`**; never names, ids or counts | The owner's auto-discovery, bounded to the one fact an app needs to show or hide its *use my ledger* button |
| Q11 | Builder-time declaration carrier | **`<meta name="snug:access-wants" content="<json ≤ 2 KiB>">` inside the app HTML**, strict-parsed at install/update/hand-in into `accessWants:<appId>`; never a bundle field | `snug-app-bundle/1` is strict at every level — a new field makes every 1.1 hub refuse the whole bundle; HTML travels inside every bundle unchanged; a declaration proposes, never grants (ADR-0016) |
| Q12 | User-chosen columns | **`withheld?: string[]` per table**; withheld columns NULLed on the copy exactly like credential columns; `driftOf` ignores them; the sheet shows per-column ticks, pre-ticked | Reuses D27's fail-closed withhold; no new mechanism |
| Q13 | Sharing the key-value store | **`kv: true` on the grant** keeps a FILTERED `snug_kv` on the copy (credential-named keys, credential-shaped values and `snug:`-prefixed host keys dropped); appears as `snug_kv(key, value)`; the sheet's tick reads *settings & notes* | Apps keep real state there (`usePersistedState`); the filter removes the two things that must never cross |
| Q14 | Doorbells | **Three host-events, ids and names only**: `access-source-changed {grantId}` → readers (coalesced ≥ 2 s, from a real write signal in `runExec`); `access-read {grantId}` → the source's live frame; `data-changed {table}` → the target's live frame after a brokered write | ADR-0034; the deferred "doorbell" and "hint to the source" of ADR-0075 §10 |
| Q15 | Host-side discovery surfaces | **Yes**: Settings *apps that could work together* (ranked pairs, one tap), the source's ⋈ sheet *let another app read this…*, a Hub tile ⋈ badge | The owner's "auto-discovery with permission" belongs to the host, which can see the inventory |
| Q16 | Two-device history | **Union-merge for append-only rows** (`accessLog:*`, `accessUndo:*`) in the divergence resolver, bounded to those prefixes and the existing caps | A log has no "right" version; union is the honest merge |
| Q17 | Sidecar-fact sources (WhatsApp) | **Defer, with the design named**: a reader holding a live grant from a restricted source would inherit the source's egress restriction — scrubbed brain AND refused connected calls while the grant is live ("taint follows data") | A GET URL can carry rows and cannot be scrubbed; the restriction would surprise users of the reader; it needs its own ADR and copy. One word moves it into PR-5 |
| Q18 | Delivery | **Six PRs on one task** — PR-1 execution model · PR-2 the doors (read) · PR-3 the wire (spec 1.2: writes, refresh, discover, columns, kv, doorbells) · PR-4 host-native jobs + task write authority · PR-5 discovery, declarations, belts · PR-6 the starter wave (owner-timed). Each PR: fresh-context plan review, tests first, diff review, threat delta, Gate 6 | The scheduling task's lineage; wire changes in ONE PR give ONE spec minor and ONE owner push |
| Q19 | Grant v2 rows on a 1.1 hub | **Accepted and said**: a strict 1.1 reader treats a v2 grant row as unreadable → no access (fail closed); the changelog states it | Fail closed is the right failure for an authority record |

### Acceptance criteria (each becomes at least one test; `→` names the test's home; ranges are per PR and in dependency order)

*PR-1 — one instance per app (host only; no schema bytes)*
1. **AC1** `due`/`late`/`catch-up` with the app LIVE and announced → the run is DELEGATED to the live frame (`notifyAppHost` hint, no hidden mount) and its result is recorded → `apps/playground/src/__tests__/appRunHandshake.test.tsx` (rewritten: the pin "the live frame is never hinted" is inverted, not deleted).
2. **AC2** A mutating connected call inside a delegated run reaches the run-scoped ask gate: the ordinary dialog opens with the schedule's name in its copy; **negative**: an ARMED standing approval and a REMEMBERED session grant for the same host are not consulted (the dialog still opens); no answer within 60 s → `NET_CONFIRM_DENIED` → the step is `refused` with the *needs you* sentence → `runScopedGate.test.ts`, `appRunHandshake.test.tsx`.
3. **AC3** App closed → the headless frame exactly as today (refusing gate, counting transport, `scheduledDbDriver`) → `appRunHandshake.test.tsx`, `scheduledDbDriver.test.ts` (unchanged, green).
4. **AC4** Handover: opening the app while a headless run is in flight cuts it `handed-over` and re-dispatches the SAME `runId` to the live frame once announced; closing the app mid-delegated-run re-dispatches headless; at most one handover per run; the kv key is written once per dispatch and cleared once at the end → `runPlacement.test.ts`.
5. **AC5** Readiness: `state/appHosts.ts` entries carry `{generation, announced}`; a delegated hint waits for `announced` ≤ 10 s else `no-handler`; the *run now* race (hint before the listener mounts) is closed → `appHosts.test.ts`, `appRunHandshake.test.tsx`.
6. **AC6** AI and net calls inside a delegated run are COUNTED on the run row and never refused mid-run; the ceiling is enforced before dispatch (`capped` without dispatch) → `scheduledTransport.test.ts`, `queue.test.ts`.
7. **AC7** An auth-shaped failure inside a delegated or headless run is attributed to the run; the reconnect chip's copy says a scheduled run found it (R-b) → `net.test.ts`.
8. **AC8** Docs: KB 85 rules + the SDK's header say "one instance — a run may be handed over, stay idempotent by `runId`"; lesson `:211` amended; ADR-0074 status → `accepted (amended by 0075, 0077)`; ADR-0077 `accepted`; threat delta + R-62/R-70 rewritten; architecture + code-map + glossary → `kb` tests, `check-threat-model`.

*PR-2 — the Access Service and the chat + scheduler doors (read; host only)*
9. **AC9** `access/service.ts` + `access/policy.ts`: the frame handler, the chat door and the scheduler door call ONE `authorise(caller, act)`; a table-driven test over (caller kind × grant status × attendance × duration × session generation) reproduces today's handler answers byte-for-byte (characterisation written FIRST — the table's frame cells driven through the real handler, green today; mute, decline and rate stay the handler's ladder, pinned by `accessHandler.test.ts`) → `accessPolicyTable.ts` + `accessHandlerTable.test.ts` + `accessPolicy.test.ts`; `accessHandler.test.ts` unchanged and green. *(Amended at PR-2 Gate 2: the one named invert — a session grant is never read without someone present, whatever `unattended` says.)*
10. **AC10** Chat door: with a live read grant, the data lane's scratch copy holds `<alias>__<table>` tables materialised THROUGH the Worker (drops, withheld, masked, drift applied in the Worker); the DDL block lists them under *From <Source> (read-only · access until …)*; `data_query` can JOIN across them; a `read` line (`attended: true`, the statement's SQL — no `via`: that seat lands with PR-3's record change, D3) is logged BEFORE any row reaches the brain; the in-host caps `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_ROWS` (5,000) / `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_BYTES` (2 MiB) per table and `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_SET_BYTES` (8 MiB) per set apply, truncation stated in the DDL block → `dataLaneAccess.test.ts`, `materialise.test.ts`.
11. **AC11** `data_propose_write` naming an alias table is refused by name in PR-2 ("<Source>'s data is read-only here") → `dataTools.test.ts`.
12. **AC12** `access_propose` tool (data + answer lanes, and the builder set when an app is attached): ONE proposal per turn staged on `meta.access`; the card's *review* calls `startUserAsk(reader, {purpose, hints, provenance:'chat'})`; the resolution persists; re-validated on read → `accessProposeTool.test.ts`, `chatLogAccessCard.test.tsx`.
13. **AC13** Scheduler door: *Ask [app]'s AI* context includes the materialised shared tables (tool-free, every brain) only for persisted grants ticked *also while I'm away*; the `read` line carries `attended: false` (`via` waits for PR-3) → `scheduleAppThink.test.ts`.
14. **AC14** Disclosure: the consent sheet's "where this can go" gains the chat line wherever the chat door can open, and the away line says a scheduled *ask <Reader>'s AI* sees the tables too; `chatBrainRoute` is pinned equal to the real `createDirectBuilder` route across every brain arm (one derivation by test — the chooser's memo is not rewired; the chat routes identically to the app transport today, measured at PR-2 Gate 2) → `accessEgress.test.ts`.
15. **AC15** Shared rows in a chat turn are wrapped in `<query_result>` with the not-instructions trailer; the delta extends R-71 to the chat door → `dataLaneAccess.test.ts`.
16. **AC16** ADR-0076 §1–§3 `accepted`; threat delta; KB 00/10/87 + the skill say "the chat beside your app can read what you allowed"; code-map, architecture, glossary (*door*, *materialised table*).

*PR-3 — the wire: spec 1.1 → 1.2 (High: protocol + runner)*
17. **AC17** Protocol: request ops `write {grantId, sql, params?}`, `refresh {grantId}`, `discover {hints?}` (strict); grant RECORD v2 `scope.tables[].{name, columns, withheld?, write?}` + `kv?` + `refresh?` (strict, additive, defaults off); VIEW v2 (tolerant); codes `ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED` · `ACCESS_BUSY` · `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED`; constants; `schemas-stable` (still sixteen files) regenerated and `review-regressions` unchanged and green; **negative**: a 1.1-shaped `request`/`query`/`list`/`release` still parses; `write` with two statements → `ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED`; `write` to a table without `write` → `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED`; `canonicalAccessGrantIntent` includes the new fields (an import of a widened grant is NOT intent-identical) → `packages/protocol/src/__tests__/access.test.ts`, `frames.test.ts`.
18. **AC18** Runner: the access seat's `ok` mapper covers the three ops; value-blind posture unchanged (characterisation) → `packages/runner/src/__tests__/host-access.test.ts`.
19. **AC19** Write Broker (`packages/db/src/brokered-write.ts` pure + `apps/playground/src/access/writeBroker.ts`): one plain DML only; table ∈ write scope, every named column ⊆ the granted columns (a withheld, credential-named, generated or masked column anywhere → refused by name); literal-valued (literals and placeholders; a WHERE of simple comparisons), no subquery/CTE/function; UPDATE/DELETE without WHERE refused; a triggered, virtual, WITHOUT ROWID, `ON CONFLICT REPLACE`, foreign-key-action or CHECK-over-unshared table refused; a DELETE only on a fully shared table; dry-run count ≤ `ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS` (200); the target mid-transaction → `ACCESS_BUSY` (retryable, from the driver's per-namespace flag); the undo captured INSIDE the transaction by TEMP trigger (UPDATE/DELETE: the OLD rows by rowid; INSERT: the new rowids) as structured rows ≤ 64 KiB else refused "too large to undo"; `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`/exec/`COMMIT` through the driver's SYNCHRONOUS `brokeredWrite` seat (no frame request can interleave); the `wrote` history line is written with the journal entry before the answer; `undo` rebuilds the inverse from the journal's rows, applies it through the SAME seat guarded by the values the write left, writes `undone`, is itself not undoable; ≤ 20 live undo entries per (target, reader) — the 21st write is refused — 7 days; **negative**: a credential-named column in SET → refused; a credential-shaped value → refused; a planted journal row carrying statement text has no seat to carry it → `brokered-write.test.ts`, `writeBroker.test.ts`. *(Amended at PR-3 Gate 2 — SC6/F21: the seat, the capture mechanism, the column and table rules.)*
20. **AC20** `executeApprovedWrite` is a thin caller of the broker's own mode (authority `approval`; atomic through the same synchronous seat, no journal, no line — the own-app undo surface is PR-4's AC31); its characterisation tests stay green → `dataTools.test.ts`. *(Amended at PR-3 Gate 2 — SC1/F21: the authority spelling; own mode never journals.)*
21. **AC21** Consent sheet v2: per table *can also change rows* (off; offered when the ask carried `access:'write'` or behind *more…*), per-column ticks, *settings & notes*, *may ask <Source> to fetch new data first*; one-breath copy; "every change can be undone from <Source>'s history" → `accessConsentSheet.test.tsx`, `accessCopy.test.ts`.
22. **AC22** Refresh engine: `refresh` under `grant.refresh` runs the source where it lives (PR-1 placement) with `input {kind:'refresh', requestedBy}` and `taskId: 'access-refresh'`; cooldown 5 min per source (`ok, fresh:false, reason:'recent'`, nothing run); GET-only under the unattended gate; counted; `refreshed` line; no handler → `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` (not retryable); source busy → retryable → `refresh.test.ts`.
23. **AC23** `discover` → the class from `collectSources`; nothing recorded; bounded like `request` — a per-reader cache of the class for one minute, so the library is gathered at most once a minute per reader and discover → the user's click → `request` is never refused → `accessHandler.test.ts`. *(Amended at PR-3 Gate 2 — SC6/S16: a shared window would refuse the user's click that follows a discover.)*
24. **AC24** Doorbells: a successful mutating exec on namespace N (from `getRowsModified`/statement class, raised in the driver's `handle()`, never `markDirty`, never the host's own kv writes) rings every live reader of N `access-source-changed {grantId}` coalesced ≥ 2 s; a read rings the source `access-read {grantId}`; a brokered write rings the target `data-changed {table}`; **negative**: no event carries a row, a value, a name or a count → `doorbell.test.ts`, `driver.test.ts` (the runner's `notifyEvent` is unchanged). *(Amended at PR-3 Gate 2 — SC6: the test home.)*
25. **AC25** kv: `kv:true` keeps a FILTERED `snug_kv` on the copy; `accessTableNameSchema` still refuses `snug_kv` in `tables`; **negative**: a `snug:schedule:*` key and a credential-shaped value never cross → `scoped-read.test.ts`.
26. **AC26** Columns: `withheld` NULLed on the copy; `driftOf` treats them as part of the known set (a withheld column that vanishes still drifts); the view's `columns` excludes them → `scoped-read.test.ts`, `accessGrants.test.ts`. *(Amended at PR-3 Gate 2 — SC6: the drift wording.)*
27. **AC27** SDK `useSnugAccess` v2 (`write`, `refresh`, `discover`, `onSourceChanged`); KB 87 v2; skill; spec 1.2 draft + `spec-changelog` entry (with Q19's note) + regenerated schemas + website sync + whitepaper edition 5 (`check-whitepaper` AC1–AC8 green); the kit page measured under `KIT_SIZE_CEILING_BYTES` or the ceiling conversation held BEFORE merge.
28. **AC28** e2e: `apps/playground/e2e/access.spec.ts` + `apps/host/e2e/kit-access.spec.ts` gain a write + undo walk and a refresh walk.

*PR-4 — host-native jobs and the task write authority (host only)*
29. **AC29** `app-think` with `tools: true` runs on the data-lane machinery (`runAgentTurn` + `data_query` + `data_propose_write` + `fetch_connection` + the shared tables) over the app's model pin; ≤ 8 tool calls a run; replies through `scrubOrWithhold`; under host/subscription brains it degrades to tool-free with the shared tables and the result SAYS so → `appAgent.test.ts`.
30. **AC30** `fetch_connection` is GET/HEAD only through the app's APPROVED connections (connection-relative, ADR-0026), ≤ 64 KiB, counted on the net ceiling, scrubbed; **negative**: no method parameter exists, a POST cannot be expressed → `fetchConnectionTool.test.ts`.
31. **AC31** Task write authority (ADR-0033's first arming surface): `EnableConsent` gains *let it apply its changes (up to 200 rows a run · you can undo any run)*; stored on the task as `writeAuthority {rowsPerRun, armedAt}`; proposals in an armed run execute through the Write Broker (`authority: {kind:'task', taskId}`) and the result shows *undo*; unarmed → pending as today; kill switches: global (Settings) and per task; an import DISARMS (canonical intent excludes engine state but includes the authority); **negative**: an imported armed task never applies → `taskWriteAuthority.test.ts`, `schedules.test.ts` (reconcile).
32. **AC32** The editor and the chat offer the choice in words — *Ask <App>'s AI* (host-native) vs *Run <App>'s own routine* (app code) — and the result page says where it ran (`host.kind`) → `editorModel.test.ts`, `ResultDetail` test.

*PR-5 — discovery, declarations, belts (host only)*
33. **AC33** `<meta name="snug:access-wants">` parsed at install/update/hand-in (≤ 2 KiB; strict `[{purpose, hints}]`, ≤ 4 entries) into `accessWants:<appId>`; shown on the install preview, on the Hub tile when a match exists, and pre-fills the sheet; **never** writes a grant → `accessWants.test.ts`, `installShared.test.ts`.
34. **AC34** Settings *apps that could work together*: ranked pairs from `relevance.ts` over owned apps with tables; one tap → `startUserAsk(reader, {source})` → `accessSettingsCard.test.tsx`.
35. **AC35** Source-initiated: the source's ⋈ sheet gains *let another app read this…* with a reader picker → `accessSheet.test.tsx`.
36. **AC36** Hub tile ⋈ badge with the live-grant count either direction → `hubTile.test.tsx`.
37. **AC37** Two-device merge: `accessLog:*`/`accessUndo:*` divergences union-merge by entry id under the caps → `sync` test in `packages/db`.
38. **AC38** Belts: `DELETE FROM sqlite_sequence WHERE name NOT IN (granted)` on the copy (R-76); an app-level imported marker written by the untrusted import path feeds the provenance line (R-77) → `scoped-read.test.ts`, `provenance.test.ts`.
39. **AC39** KB template + skill: when the user's request implies another app's data, the builder adds BOTH the declaration and the runtime ask → `cross-app-access-kb.test.ts`.
39a. **AC39a** *(moved from PR-4 by its Gate-2 scope fold, D-PR4-1)* The chat's cross-app write through its card (D-PR3-16 reversed): a separate `meta.accessWrite` card key; an invalid `target` drops the whole card; `applyApprovedWrite` refuses a `target`; a host-only `service.writeApproved` with a pre-seat `drifted`; the DDL mark for the chat caller only and `CHAT_DOOR.changes`/`oneChange`/`readOnlyTable`; the one-app rule; the rename's home re-decided at Gate 2; `planWrite` after `recordRead` with `inTransaction` first; `access_propose` + `access` and `userTitle(readerName, wantsWrite)`; the host line first and the *open <Source>’s access* deep link — the PR-4 contract v2's "PR-5 additions" paragraph lists every input → `chatCrossAppWrite.test.ts`, `chatCrossAppCard.test.tsx`, `sharedDdl.test.ts`, `accessProposeTool.test.ts`, knowledge `access-doors-prompts.test.ts`.
39b. **AC39b** *(moved from PR-3 via PR-4)* *fetch new data first* — the chat's (a user act, never a brain tool) and the scheduler's (`refreshFirst` on the step; the refresh engine counting the run it sits in) → `refresh.test.ts`, `scheduleExecutors.test.ts`, `useBuilderChatLanes.test.tsx`.
39c. **AC39c** *(moved)* the source's ⋈ *fetch now*, with the decisions on SPEC MUST 17 (one act per grant row) and a grant-less `refreshed` line → `accessSheet.test.tsx`.
39d. **AC39d** *(moved)* a *cancel* on the refresh chip (the engine's abort seam) → `runningChip.test.tsx`, `refresh.test.ts`.
39e. **AC39e** *(moved)* the owner's ONE push of the staged spec revision (PR-4's §24.5/§25/§8.1 edits + PR-5's) and whitepaper edition 6 — one line, wait for a yes (SPEC_SYNC step 8).

*PR-6 — the starter wave (owner-timed; publish is the owner's act)*
40. **AC40** The embedded hooks block gains section 6 (`useHostEvents` + `useSnugSchedule`) and section 7 (the access helper, v2 ops); `kb-sync.test.ts` and all twelve `examples/*/app.html` byte-identical; `87-cross-app-access.md:151-222` retired; `@snugprotocol/starters` 0.2.0 staged by `publish-starters.mjs --stage`; merged only after the owner publishes; the lock marks it.

**Out of scope** (each named so a fresh session does not drift into it): desktop background mode (its own High task, next-steps 2026-10-09); write access on a source-initiated grant (the source offers READ); sidecar-fact sources (Q17); step chaining across apps inside one schedule; raising `KIT_SIZE_CEILING_BYTES` (a conversation with the owner if PR-3 needs it); the data lane under host/subscription brains (pre-existing bound); the reader's own sidecar data reaching its chat unscrubbed (pre-existing; PR-2 records it in next-steps); a per-op (insert/update/delete) write toggle (one toggle, Q3); the sibling confirms' arming delay (a rider a PR may take if cheap).

## Plan

### The architecture in one picture

```
                         ┌────────────────────── the host page (Chrome's "browser process") ──────────────────────┐
 app iframe (renderer) ──frames──▶ runner access seat (value-blind) ──▶ accessHandler.ts (thin mapper) ──┐
                                                                                                          ▼
 chat beside the app ──▶ agent/dataTools.ts  (chat door) ───────────────────────────────────▶  access/service.ts
 scheduler step      ──▶ schedule/appThink.ts | appAgent.ts (scheduler door) ───────────────▶      │  calls
                                                                                                   ▼
                                                                                   access/policy.ts  authorise(caller, act)
                                                                                                   │
                      ┌──────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┴───────────┐
          access/materialise.ts        access/writeBroker.ts        access/refresh.ts                 access/doorbell.ts
          Worker: scoped copy → rows   dry-run → cap → busy →        run the SOURCE where it lives     ids-only hints to
          (drops, withheld, masked,    undo plan → txn → history     (ADR-0077 placement) → read      readers / source / target
          drift — all in the Worker)   → doorbell                    cooldown · counted · logged
                                                                             │
                                                            schedule/runPlacement.ts  live frame (delegated, run-scoped ask gate)
                                                                                      | headless frame (today's hidden path)
                                                                                      | host-native (no frame at all)
```

Everything below the dotted line already exists in some form; the plan factors it into these seven modules rather than adding a second engine beside the first.

### Components (new or changed modules — the vocabulary every PR uses)

| Module | Role | Public surface | Callers |
|---|---|---|---|
| `access/policy.ts` (new, pure) | THE one authorisation function | `authorise(ctx, caller, act) → Allow \| Refuse{code,message,retryable,log?}`; `caller ∈ frame \| chat \| schedule \| user`; `act ∈ read \| materialise \| write \| refresh \| discover \| list \| release` | service |
| `access/service.ts` (new) | THE one engine | `read`, `materialise`, `write`, `undo`, `refresh`, `discover`, `list`, `release` — each takes a `caller` | handler, dataTools, appThink/appAgent, UI acts |
| `access/materialise.ts` (new) | granted tables → rows through the Worker | `materialiseFor(caller, readerAppId) → MaterialisedTable[] {alias, name, columns, rows, truncated}`; 10 s cache per grant | service |
| `packages/db/src/scoped-read.ts` (+`scopedScratchDump`) | the pure dump beside the pure read — same drops/withheld/mask/drift core | `scopedScratchDump(SQL, bytes, scope, caps)` | the Worker |
| `packages/db/src/brokered-write.ts` (new, pure) | plan a bounded, undoable write on a scratch copy | `planBrokeredWrite(SQL, bytes, scope, statement, caps) → Plan \| Refusal`; `inverseOf(...)` | the Worker (`plan-write` job) |
| `access/writeBroker.ts` (new) | execute a planned write on the REAL target through the driver, journal it, ring | `brokeredWrite(db, {targetAppId, statement, authority})`, `undoBrokeredWrite(db, sourceAppId, undoId)` | service, `executeApprovedWrite` (thin), appAgent |
| `access/refresh.ts` (new) | have the source pull | `refreshSource(db, {grantId, requestedBy, caller})` | service, the ⋈ sheet's *fetch now* |
| `access/doorbell.ts` (new) | ids-only hints from a real write signal | subscribes `driver.onNamespaceWrite`; rings via `notifyAppHost` | boot |
| `schedule/runPlacement.ts` (new) | where a run executes; handover | `placeRun(appId) → 'live' \| 'headless'`; `delegatedRunStore`; `watchHandover(run)` | appRun, refresh |
| `schedule/runScopedGate.ts` (new) | the live frame's gate during a run | `createRunScopedGate({inner, store, dialog, timeoutMs})` | `state/net.ts` default gate |
| `schedule/dispatchAppRun.ts` (extracted) | ONE dispatch (kv input → hint → await result) for live and headless | `dispatchLive(...)`, `dispatchHeadless(...)` | appRun (scheduler), refresh |
| `schedule/appAgent.ts` (new) | *Ask [app]'s AI* with tools (host-native job) | `executeAppAgent(ctx, step)` | executors |
| `agent/dataTurn.ts` (extracted) | the data-lane turn body, out of `useBuilderChat.send` | `runDataTurn({db, appId, prompt, tools, transport, maxToolCalls, onProposal})` | useBuilderChat, appAgent |
| `agent/fetchConnectionTool.ts` (new) | GET/HEAD through approved connections | `fetch_connection {connection, path, query?}` | appAgent (PR-4); the chat's data lane may adopt it later |
| `agent/accessProposeTool.ts` + `agent/accessCard.ts` (new) | the brain asks; the card's *review* opens the sheet | mirrors `scheduleProposeTool.ts` / `scheduleCard.ts` | laneTools, ChatLog |
| `access/wants.ts` + `packages/db/src/access-wants.ts` (new) | the install-time declaration | `parseAccessWants(html)`; `getAccessWants/setAccessWants` | install/update/hand-in seams, Hub, Settings, the sheet |

### PR-1 — One instance per app (ADR-0077) · host only · ~1.5k lines

**Goal.** A due/late/catch-up run of an OPEN app runs in the open app's frame under a gate that asks the present user; a closed app runs headless; a run follows the app. R-70 retires for the delegated case; R-b closes.

**Order (each range its own commit range and verifier round; tests first in every range):**
- **R0 characterisation.** Pin today's `executeAppRun` behaviour (trigger-only branch, manual → live, unattended → hidden) in `appRunHandshake.test.tsx` as the baseline the rewrite inverts deliberately.
- **R1 readiness.** `state/appHosts.ts`: entries `{notify, token, generation, announced}`; `markAppHostAnnounced(appId, generation)`, `isAppHostReady`, `awaitAppHostReady(appId, ms)`. `RunView.tsx`: register with `generation: frameEpoch` (`:288-290`), mark announced in `onAnnounce` (`:709-724`). Test: the *run now* race (hint only after announce).
- **R2 placement + delegation under TODAY's semantics.** `schedule/runPlacement.ts` (`placeRun`, `delegatedRunStore`); `schedule/dispatchAppRun.ts` extracted from `appRun.ts` (`:343-381` and `:384-484` share one kv-write → hint → `awaitResult` body); `executeAppRun` branches on placement; the delegated path FIRST ships with the refusing gate (`createScheduledConfirmGate`) wired through the run-scoped gate so behaviour on the live frame equals today's hidden-frame behaviour — the mechanism changes before the policy does, so each is reviewable alone.

### PR-2 — The Access Service and the chat + scheduler doors, read (ADR-0076 §1–§3) · host only · ~2k lines

**Order.**
- **R0 characterisation.** `accessPolicy.test.ts` is written FIRST against today's `accessHandler.ts:170-376` answers (a table over caller × status × attendance × duration × mute/decline × rate) — then the policy is extracted and the table must still pass; `accessHandler.test.ts` stays untouched and green.
- **R1 policy + service.** `access/policy.ts`, `access/service.ts`; `accessHandler.ts` becomes op → service with the frame caller; `logRead` moves into the service (the `read` line still lands BEFORE rows leave).
- **R2 the dump.** `packages/db/src/scoped-read.ts` gains `scopedScratchDump` sharing `dropOutsideScope`/`strayObject`/`withholdCredentialColumns`/`maskCredentialValues`/`driftOf`; `access/scopedRead.worker.ts` gains the `dump` job; `access/materialise.ts` (per live grant → `MaterialisedTable[]`, caps `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_ROWS` 5,000 / `_BYTES` 2 MiB per table in `access/limits.ts` — moved to protocol Appendix B in PR-3; 10 s cache keyed by grant).
- **R3 the chat door.** `packages/db/src/userdb/userdb.ts` `scratchRun(appId, statements, {attach?})`: after opening the copy, `CREATE TABLE <alias>__<name>(…)` + inserts, THEN the statements (the copy stays throwaway). `agent/dataTools.ts`: `buildDataTools({shared?})` — `data_query` passes `attach`; `data_propose_write` refuses an alias table by name. `agent/intentContext.ts:96-102`: the DDL block appends *From <Source> (read-only · access until …)* lines + truncation notes. `agent/laneTools.ts` and `useBuilderChat.ts`: `sharedForApp(appId)` lists live read grants (`grantsForApp(...).reads`) → `service.materialise({kind:'chat', appId, threadId})` once per turn per grant (the `read` line is written once per turn, `tables` = the materialised set, `rows` = the total, `sql` absent — the `via` field waits for PR-3's record change; PR-2's history line therefore reads like an app read, and the task says so).
- **R4 the brain asks.** `agent/accessProposeTool.ts` + `packages/knowledge/prompts/tools/access-propose.md` (`{purpose, hints?}`; refuses a purpose failing the display rule; one per turn); `useBuilderChat.ts`: `stagedAccess`, `meta.access`, rehydrate (mirrors `:598-602`, `:743-749`, `:1021`, `:1046-1051`, `:503-506`); `agent/accessCard.ts`; `views/ChatLog.tsx` renders the card beside `:291`; *review* → `startUserAsk(reader, {purpose, hints})` — `userAsk.ts` gains `purpose`/`hints` options; the grant's `provenance` stays `'user'` (the user's *review* is the act; `'chat'` as a recorded provenance is a strict-record change and lands in PR-3) and the sheet's quote line reads "asked in <Reader>'s chat". `laneTools.ts`: the tool joins the `data` and `answer` lanes and the builder set when an app is attached.
- **R5 the scheduler door.** `schedule/appThink.ts` `renderThinkContext` appends the materialised tables through `service.materialise({kind:'schedule', taskId, runId, appId, attended:false})` — policy admits only grants whose `unattended` rule allows it; `runContextQuery` passes `attach`.
- **R6 disclosure.** `access/egress.ts` gains `door` and `chatBrainRoute(db, appId)`; `useBuilderChat.ts:430-470` reads the route from there (one derivation); `EgressNote`/`AccessConsentSheet` show the chat's brain when it differs.
- **R7 docs.** ADR-0076 §1–§3 → accepted; `threat-model-delta-access-doors.md` (R-71 at the chat door; the chat path's missing R-9 scrub recorded as pre-existing) + fold (v3.9); KB `00-summary.md`, `10-overview-and-contract.md`, `87` ("your app need not read another app itself: the chat beside it can, once the user allows"), the skill's section; architecture `:851-862`; code-map `:122-123`; glossary (*door*, *materialised table*); product-vision `:11`.

**Files.** create: `access/policy.ts`, `access/service.ts`, `access/materialise.ts`, `access/limits.ts`, `agent/accessProposeTool.ts`, `agent/accessCard.ts`, `prompts/tools/access-propose.md`; change: `access/accessHandler.ts`, `access/scopedRead.worker.ts`, `access/scopedRead.ts`, `access/userAsk.ts`, `access/egress.ts`, `access/EgressNote.tsx`, `packages/db/src/scoped-read.ts`, `packages/db/src/userdb/userdb.ts`, `agent/dataTools.ts`, `agent/intentContext.ts`, `agent/laneTools.ts`, `agent/useBuilderChat.ts`, `views/ChatLog.tsx`, `schedule/appThink.ts`, KB + skill; tests as AC9–AC16 name them.

### PR-3 — The wire: spec 1.1 → 1.2 (ADR-0076 §4–§8, §10) · High · ~4k lines (the largest; its Gate-2 review may split it into 3a protocol+db pure / 3b host+UI+spec, provided BOTH merge before the spec push — one minor)

**Order.**
- **R0 protocol (tests first).** `packages/protocol/src/access.ts`: `ACCESS_OPS` + `write` · `refresh` · `discover`; `ACCESS_LOG_KINDS` + `wrote` · `undone` · `refreshed`; the log entry gains `via?` (`app` · `chat` · `schedule` · `user`) and `authority?`; `ACCESS_PROVENANCES` + `chat`; `accessScopeTableSchema` + `withheld?` · `write?`; the grant RECORD + `kv?` · `refresh?` (strict, additive, defaults off); the VIEW mirrors them; `canonicalAccessGrantIntent` includes every new field; `accessUndoEntrySchema` (new record: `{id, grantId, readerAppId, table, op, forward{sql,params}, inverse{sql,params}[], rows, at, authority, expiresAt}` ≤ 64 KiB); `accessWantsSchema` (PR-5's consumer; defined here so the spec lists it once); constants `ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS` 200 · `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES` 64 KiB · `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_ENTRIES` 20 · `ACCESS_UNDO_TTL_MS` 7 d · `ACCESS_REFRESH_COOLDOWN_MS` 5 min · `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_ROWS/BYTES` · `ACCESS_WANTS_MAX_BYTES` 2 KiB · `ACCESS_DOORBELL_COALESCE_MS` 2 s; events `ACCESS_SOURCE_CHANGED_EVENT` · `ACCESS_READ_EVENT` · `DATA_CHANGED_EVENT`; codes `ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED` · `ACCESS_BUSY` · `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED`. `frames.ts:242-301`: request variants `write {grantId, sql, params?}` · `refresh {grantId}` · `discover {hints?}` (strict); response variants `{ok, op:'write', changes, undoId}` · `{ok, op:'refresh', fresh, reason?, summary?}` · `{ok, op:'discover', candidates}`. `json-schemas.ts` regenerates `schemas/access-request.json` (7 variants) and `access-response.json` (8); `index.ts` exports; tests `access.test.ts`, `frames.test.ts`, `schemas-stable.test.ts` (sixteen files, new variant counts), `review-regressions.test.ts` (the STRICT set unchanged). **Negative**: every 1.1-shaped frame still parses; `write` with two statements → refused; `write` to a non-write table → `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED`; a widened imported grant is not intent-identical.
- **R1 runner.** `packages/runner/src/host.ts:579-616` `accessSeat.ok` + three ops; `transport.ts:112-129` `AccessHandlerResult` + three; `host-access.test.ts` (value-blind characterisation stays green).
- **R2 db.** `packages/db/src/brokered-write.ts` (pure): `isLiteralDml` (single DML, no subquery/CTE, literal values or `?`), `tableAndColumnsOf`, `whereClauseOf` (tokenizer-based; refuses what it cannot bound — an UPDATE/DELETE without WHERE is refused), `planBrokeredWrite(SQL, bytes, scope, statement, caps)` (scope check incl. withheld + credential-named columns and credential-shaped values → refused; dry run on the copy → `changes ≤ ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS`; undo capture — UPDATE/DELETE `SELECT rowid, * FROM t WHERE <pred>`, INSERT the new rowids after the dry run — `≤ ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES` else refused "too large to undo"; `inverseOf`). `scoped-read.ts`: `withheld` NULLed like credential columns and ignored by `driftOf`; `kv:true` keeps a FILTERED `snug_kv` (credential-named keys, credential-shaped values, `snug:`-prefixed keys dropped); the `sqlite_sequence` belt (R-76); `accessTableNameSchema` still refuses `snug_kv` in `tables`. `driver.ts`: `onNamespaceWrite(namespace, table?)` from `runExec` via `getRowsModified() > 0` + statement class (never `markDirty`); `inTransaction(namespace)` (BEGIN depth per connection) for the busy check. `userdb/access.ts`: `listAccessUndo` · `putAccessUndo` · `pruneAccessUndo`; `app-settings-keys.ts` + `accessUndo:<sourceAppId>` · `accessWants:<appId>`; `deleteApp` sweeps undo; the untrusted import DROPS undo rows (a planted inverse could rewrite data) and keeps a trusted pull's. Tests: `brokered-write.test.ts` (every refusal by name), `scoped-read.test.ts`, `driver.test.ts`, `access.test.ts` (db).
- **R3 the engine.** `access/writeBroker.ts`: `exportAppRuntime(target)` → Worker job `plan-write` (the 2 s clock) → `ACCESS_BUSY` if `inTransaction(target)` → `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` / exec / `COMMIT` through `db.driver.handle(targetAppId, {op:'exec', instanceId:'host-brokered-write'})` → `wrote` line (before the answer) → `putAccessUndo` → `doorbell`; `undoBrokeredWrite` runs the inverse through the SAME path (`authority:{kind:'undo'}`), writes `undone`, is not itself undoable. `access/refresh.ts`: cooldown store (in-memory per host; a cost control, not a security record) → `placeRun(source)` → `dispatchLive/Headless({taskId:'access-refresh', runId, input:{kind:'refresh', requestedBy}})` under the refusing gate + counting → `refreshed` line → the read proceeds; no handler → `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` (not retryable); busy → retryable. `access/doorbell.ts`. `access/policy.ts` + write (attended frame of its generation, or `authority.kind === 'task'`), refresh (`grant.refresh`, cooldown, ceilings), discover (shares `request`'s limit). `access/service.ts` + `write` · `undo` · `refresh` · `discover`. `accessHandler.ts` maps the three ops. `agent/dataTools.ts`: `data_propose_write` on an alias table under a write grant → the proposal carries `targetAppId` + `grantId`; `executeApprovedWrite` → `brokeredWrite(..., {kind:'user-approval'})` (characterisation stays green); `views/ChatLog.tsx` card copy "changes <Source>'s data · you can undo it from <Source>'s history".
- **R4 UI.** `TableChooser.tsx` (per-column ticks pre-ticked; the *can also change rows* toggle, off, offered when the ask carried `access:'write'` or behind *more…*); a `SourceOptions` row (*settings & notes*, *may ask <Source> to fetch new data first*); `copy.ts` one-breath sentences; `EgressNote.tsx` (the write and refresh sentences); `AccessHistory.tsx` (+`wrote` · `undone` · `refreshed` lines in words, *undo* behind an armed inline confirm); `AccessSheet.tsx` (*fetch now* on a source); `GrantRow.tsx` (a write badge); `AccessSettingsCard.tsx` (the write rows named). `createGrantFromDecision` (`grants.ts:398`) writes v2.
- **R5 SDK + KB + skill.** `packages/sdk/src/access.ts` + `write` · `refresh` · `discover` · `onSourceChanged`; `types.ts`; tests. KB `87-cross-app-access.md` v2 (ops table 4 → 7; "Write: one statement, ≤ 200 rows, undoable — never a whole table"; "Refresh: ask once, after a user act; `fresh:false` means recent"; the two events; helper snippet v2 beside the hooks block); `30-bridge-protocol.md:24` row; the skill's section; `apps/host-mcp/src/instructions.md:41-42`.

### PR-4 — Host-native jobs and the task write authority (ADR-0076 §9; ADR-0033's first arming surface) · host only · ~2k lines

**Order.**
- **R0 characterisation** of the chat's data-lane turn (`useBuilderChat.send`'s body) BEFORE the lift.
- **R1 the lift.** `agent/dataTurn.ts` `runDataTurn({db, appId, prompt, tools, transport, maxToolCalls, onProposal})`; `useBuilderChat.send` calls it (behaviour byte-identical, R0 proves it).
- **R2 the tool.** `agent/fetchConnectionTool.ts` + `prompts/tools/fetch-connection.md`: `{connection, path, query?}` → connected GET/HEAD through the executor (connection-relative, ADR-0026; GET never gates), ≤ 64 KiB, counted on the net ceiling, `scrubText`; **negative**: no method parameter exists.
- **R3 the step.** `packages/protocol/src/schedule.ts` (INTERNAL draft shapes — not published schemas, no spec bytes): `app-think` + `tools?: boolean`; the task + `writeAuthority?: {rowsPerRun, armedAt}`; `canonicalScheduleIntent` includes it. `schedule/appAgent.ts` `executeAppAgent`: `runDataTurn` with `buildDataTools({allowWrites:true, shared})` + `fetch_connection`, over the app's model pin, ≤ 8 tool calls, replies through `scrubOrWithhold`; proposals → pending as today, or — under `task.writeAuthority` — `brokeredWrite(..., {kind:'task', taskId})` per proposal with each result row carrying `undoId`; where the data lane is unavailable (host/subscription brains) → `executeAppThink` with the shared tables and a result line that says so. `executors.ts` dispatches on `tools`.
- **R4 consent + kill switches.** `EnableConsent.tsx` (*let it apply its changes · up to 200 rows a run · you can undo any run*; the exact tools listed); `enableProposedTask.ts` and the editor's writer store `writeAuthority` only from the user's act; `ScheduleSettingsCard.tsx` global kill (*never let scheduled runs change data on their own*); per-task kill on the row; `schedules.ts` `reconcileImportedSchedules` strips `writeAuthority` on an untrusted import; `ResultDetail.tsx` (*applied · undo*); `StepsEditor.tsx` offers *Ask <App>'s AI — let it look things up and act* vs *Run <App>'s own routine*; `honesty.ts` adds the brain line.
- **R5 docs.** ADR-0076 §9 → accepted; ADR-0033 status → `accepted (amended by 0076 — the first arming surface)`; `threat-model-delta-task-write-authority.md` + fold (v3.11); KB 85 ("a schedule may let the AI act — only the user arms it"); architecture; code-map `:113-115`; glossary (*write authority*); next-steps (the 2026-10-09 standing-approvals item closed).

**Suites.** protocol (internal draft shapes), db, knowledge, playground, host, desktop.

### Test plan (summary; TDD.md: tests first, one per AC, negative tests for everything near C1/C2)

- **Characterisation before every extraction** (PR-1 R0 `executeAppRun`; PR-2 R0 the access handler's answers; PR-4 R0 the chat's data turn) — the refactor is proven behaviour-preserving before the behaviour changes.
- **Negative tests that MUST exist** (High tier): a delegated run never consults a remembered or armed approval (PR-1); no event carries a row or a value (PR-3); a `write` with two statements, a whole-table UPDATE/DELETE, a credential-named column in SET, a credential-shaped value, a table outside the write scope, a withheld column — each refused by name (PR-3); a `snug:schedule:*` key or a credential-shaped kv value never crosses (PR-3); `fetch_connection` cannot express a POST (PR-4); an imported armed task never applies and an imported undo row never runs (PR-4, PR-3); a declaration never writes a grant (PR-5); a 1.1-shaped frame still parses (PR-3).
- **Worker tests** stay in `scopedReadWorker.test.ts` (the `dump` and `plan-write` jobs under the 2 s clock).
- **e2e**: `access.spec.ts` (write + undo, refresh, the chat door), `kit-access.spec.ts` (the same in the kit), a scheduling e2e for the delegated run (the weather starter's *Morning forecast* with the app OPEN — one instance, result recorded).
- **Vocabulary**: `copy.ts` sentences single-homed; the vocabulary scan (TS compiler API, lesson `:299`) covers the new words.

### Spec-sync (PR-3 only)

1. Gate 2 of PR-3 states the exact §-list above and the version bump 1.1 → 1.2. 2. Implement + regenerate `packages/protocol/schemas/`. 3. Edit `docs/spec-drafts/SPEC.md`, `docs/spec-changelog.md` (format `## YYYY-MM-DD — spec v1.2 — TASK-20261010-host-broker — <summary> — <SHA>`), run `/sync-website`, build the whitepaper (`node docs/whitepaper/build.mjs`, `pnpm run check-whitepaper`). 4. Rewrite `docs/engineering/SPEC_SYNC.md` to the 1.1 procedure (it is pre-1.0 today). 5. After merge: the owner's one-line ask → branch + PR on `/Users/jeetu/SnugProtocol/spec` → rebase-merge → changelog SHA + done line.

### Process owed (every PR)

Fresh-context plan review BEFORE implementation (the 0074/0075 precedent: four lenses — security, feasibility, design, scope — folded into the task file and the ADR); tests first; a fresh-context diff review at Gate 5 (plan and diff reviews catch disjoint defects, lesson `:265`); a threat delta pinned by `check-threat-model`; Gate 6 (journal, lessons, doc drift in-branch, ADR status lines, next-steps pruned); PR-3 adds the spec-changelog entry. Self-sign-off in this journal per PR. Done-move after PR-6 (or after PR-5 if the owner times PR-6 far out — the task file then records PR-6 as a queued owner-timed item in next-steps).

### Residual risks the deltas will name (so the fresh session does not rediscover them)

- The chat's brain sees another app's rows (R-71 at a second door); the chat path has no R-9 scrub (pre-existing; sidecar sources are excluded by construction).
- A standing write grant and a task write authority are powers exercised while nobody looks — bounded by the row cap, the undo journal, the ceilings, the kill switches; disclosed on every result and in every history.
- The undo journal holds rows at rest in the user's file — the credential walk applies to the plan; undo rows never survive an untrusted import.
- A refresh spends the source's connections and the user's brain for a reader — once per cooldown, counted, logged with who asked.
- Over-attribution of a user's own click during a delegated run's window; a handover's re-dispatch relies on idempotent handlers (the KB rule, now explained).
- A 1.1 hub cannot read a v2 grant (fail closed, said in the changelog).
- Materialisation copies up to 5,000 rows per table per turn onto the main thread (cached 10 s per grant).
- Doorbell timing is a side-channel of WHEN a source changed (ids only).

### What a fresh session does first

1. `/pickup TASK-20261010-host-broker`; read this file whole, ADR-0076, ADR-0077, ADR-0074, ADR-0075, `docs/lessons.md` 2026-10-09/10 bullets, the four "Found while planning" blocks' anchors (open each file at the line).
2. `git checkout main && git pull`; `git checkout -b feat/TASK-20261010-host-broker-pr1`. One PR at a time, in order; never start PR-n+1 before PR-n merges (each later PR's Gate 2 re-reads the merged code).
3. For the PR at hand: write its Gate-2 sub-plan INTO this file under "Decisions & surprises → PR-n", run the fresh-context plan review (four lenses), fold, self-sign-off in the journal — THEN tests first.
4. Measure before claiming: kit size (`check-host-kit`), threat-model pin (`check-threat-model`), whitepaper (PR-3), the vocabulary scan.
5. Report completion only when every AC of the PR is green and the docs owed are in the branch. The spec push (PR-3) and the starters publish (PR-6) are the owner's acts — one line each, wait for a yes.

## PR-1 — Gate-2 contract v2 (2026-10-10; FOLDED from four fresh-context plan reviews — security 16 findings · feasibility 20 · design 13 · scope 18; 5 blockers, every one folded below. The test lane writes against THIS; the implementation lane implements THIS.)

The owner approved the plan 2026-10-10 ("approved. start with PR-1") with a routing rule: tests (writing and running) on Opus 5.5 high; implementation, decisions and reviews on Fable 5.1 xhigh. Branch `feat/TASK-20261010-host-broker-pr1`, cut from the planning branch (the task file and the ADR drafts ride PR-1 to `main`). The v1 contract and the four reviews are in this branch's history (`git log -p -- docs/tasks/active/TASK-20261010-host-broker.md`).

### PR-1 decisions (each reversible by one word)

- **D-PR1-1 No persisted shape changes.** The run row, the step result and the task keep their shapes (a new field on a strict settings row makes a fielded 0.1.x hub mark the row unreadable). `handed-over` is NOT a step status — it exists only in summary text. Where a run executed is in-memory only (the placement store). The ADR-0077 line "the run row's `host.binding` records live|headless" is retracted — `binding` is the HOST binding.
- **D-PR1-2 `manual` runs are untouched except the readiness wait.** A manual run still rides the live frame under the page's ordinary gate, uncounted, and fails `the app was closed` on retraction (M18, R-62) — but it now waits for the frame to be READY before the hint (the *run now* race, AC5, is a manual run). Placement, the ask gate, counting, handover and the access-door rule apply to `due` · `late` · `catch-up` only.
- **D-PR1-3 A scheduled dialog never remembers, and a frame that hosted a scheduled run stays ask-only.** The dialog for a delegated run has no *remember for this session* box; the run-scoped gate never calls the session gate's prompt path, so nothing can be remembered (structural: `rememberSession` is only ever written inside `createSessionConfirmGate.confirm`). **Sticky generation (security blocker 1):** once a delegated run has begun on a frame generation, EVERY later mutating call from that generation — during the run and after it, until the frame retracts or remounts — is asked once, never answered by a remembered or armed grant (a handler that posts its result first and POSTs afterwards finds the same dialog). The user's own *remember for this session* is unavailable on that frame until they reopen the app; said in the delta and in the dialog ("nothing is remembered").
- **D-PR1-4 Handover: unattended runs only; hidden → live and live → hidden; at most once; never after a grant; a remount is not a handover.** Opening the app mid-hidden-run cuts the attempt and re-dispatches the SAME `runId` to the live frame once ready (after the hidden frame reports unmounted); retracting the app mid-delegated-run re-dispatches hidden. A second handover ends the step `failed` (`copy.handedOverTwice`). If the gate GRANTED any mutating call during the delegated attempt, a retraction ends the step `failed` (`copy.closedAfterChange`) — never a re-run of a handler whose POST already went out. A `frameEpoch` remount mid-run is not detected in PR-1: the attempt ends by the result bound as the manual path does today (rider in next-steps). The kv input is written per dispatch (same key) and cleared ONCE in the executor's outer `finally`.
- **D-PR1-5 Placement reads registration; readiness is awaited inside the live dispatch; one instance per TAB.** `placeRun` answers `'live'` when the registry HAS the app (registered, announced or not), else `'hidden'`; the live dispatch waits `announced` ≤ `announceTimeoutMs` and answers `no-handler` if it never comes. The leader tab runs the scheduler; an app open in a FOLLOWER tab is not "live" here (ADR-0077 §1 reads "per tab"). Presence means "RunView mounted", not "the tab is visible": in a backgrounded tab the dialog times out to *needs you* (disclosed, not gated).
- **D-PR1-6 The ceiling is asked BEFORE a delegated dispatch; inside the window calls are counted, never refused; the fold still says `capped`.** Before dispatch: if either counter has no headroom for one more call (`wouldExceedCeiling(daily+spent, {ai:1})` or `{net:1}`) → `{ status: 'refused', capped: true, summary: copy.capped(what) }`; `StepOutcome.capped` is copied by `finalizeOutcome` and folded `capped` by the queue (the fold's `reason` carries the step's summary so the card can say which limit). Tallies are SUMMED across attempts (a handover never drops what the first attempt spent). If the summed tally crossed a ceiling inside the window, the outcome carries `capped: true` too (app code is not stopped; the row says so).
- **D-PR1-7 The live frame's reply is not scrubbed by the delegated path, and its db binding is the ordinary one.** The attended frame's transport and db binding are the open app's own (unscrubbed replies, BEGIN/COMMIT allowed); `finalizeOutcome` still scrubs the step's `summary` and `alert`. Named in the delta as "the powers the open app already has". The hidden path keeps `createScheduledTransport`'s scrub and `scheduledDbDriver`.
- **D-PR1-8 The access door closes during a delegated run (security blocker 2).** While a delegated run is in flight for an app, its attended access handler answers `request` with `ACCESS_UNATTENDED`, admits `query` only under a grant's `unattended`, and logs `attended: false` — the hidden frame's posture, for the run's window only (the app's genuine user-driven ask after the run is the ordinary strip, which needs the user's act anyway).
- **D-PR1-9 One outstanding ask per run.** While a scheduled dialog is parked for a run, or after ANY refusal (denied or timed out) for that `runId`, further mutating calls in the window are refused at once and recorded — a handler cannot park N dialogs. A parked scheduled confirm is WITHDRAWN (denied by reference) on timeout and whenever the run ends (result, bound, cancel, handover).
- **D-PR1-10 One deadline per step.** The executor fixes `deadline = now + resultTimeoutMs` at the first dispatch; a re-dispatch's announce and result waits are bounded by `deadline − now`, so a handover never runs past the queue's 120 s bound.
- **D-PR1-11 The noun is "hidden frame".** `Placement = 'live' | 'hidden'`; "headless" appears nowhere in code or copy; ADR-0077 gains the line "headless = the hidden frame".

### Module contracts

**`state/appHosts.ts`** (additive; registration stays on `[id]` — a remount must NOT retract)
```ts
interface LiveHost { notify: NotifyEvent; token: symbol; generation: number; announced: boolean }
export function registerAppHost(appId: string, notify: NotifyEvent): () => void            // unchanged signature; generation 0, announced false
export function setAppHostGeneration(appId: string, generation: number): void             // sets generation, clears announced; fires NO host listener
export function markAppHostAnnounced(appId: string, generation: number): void             // announced=true only when the entry's generation matches (a stale call is ignored)
export function isAppHostReady(appId: string): boolean                                    // registered AND announced
export function liveAppHostGeneration(appId: string): number | undefined
export function awaitAppHostReady(appId: string, timeoutMs: number): Promise<boolean>     // true when ready (at once if already); false on timeout or retraction
export function publishAppEvent(appId: string, event: string, data: unknown, generation: number): void   // generation NEW
export function subscribeAppEvents(appId: string, listener: (event: string, data: unknown, generation: number) => void): () => void
```
RunView: `registerAppHost(id, notify)` stays on `[id]`; a NEW effect on `[id, frameEpoch]` calls `setAppHostGeneration(id, frameEpoch)`; `onAnnounce` (deps gain `frameEpoch`) calls `markAppHostAnnounced(id, frameEpoch)`; the frame's `onAppEvent` calls `publishAppEvent(id, event, data, frameEpoch)`. `hasLiveAppHost` keeps meaning "registered". (`access/grants.ts`'s `onAppHostChange` is untouched: no new retraction exists.)

**`schedule/runPlacement.ts`** (new; a LEAF — imports `state/store.ts` and types only; `state/net.ts`, `run/appRuntime.ts`, `access/accessHandler.ts` and `RunningChip.tsx` import it)
```ts
export type Placement = 'live' | 'hidden';
export const MAX_HANDOVERS = 1;
export interface ScheduledRefusal { host: string; method: string; why: 'declined' | 'timed-out' | 'already-asked' }
export interface DelegatedRun {            // a MUTABLE record; the store notifies on presence changes only
  appId: string; appName: string; runId: string; taskId: string; title: string; generation: number;
  calls: { ai: number; net: number }; refused: ScheduledRefusal[]; granted: number; asking: boolean;
  readonly signal: AbortSignal;            // aborted by endDelegatedRun — every parked prompt for the run listens to it
}
export const delegatedRunStore: Store<ReadonlyMap<string, DelegatedRun>>              // appId → the run in flight on the LIVE frame
export function delegatedRunFor(appId: string): DelegatedRun | undefined
export function beginDelegatedRun(input: Pick<DelegatedRun, 'appId' | 'appName' | 'runId' | 'taskId' | 'title' | 'generation'>): { ok: true; run: DelegatedRun } | { ok: false; reason: string }   // refuses a second run for the same app
export function endDelegatedRun(appId: string, runId: string): Pick<DelegatedRun, 'calls' | 'refused' | 'granted'> | undefined   // aborts the signal, removes the entry, returns the final record
export function noteDelegatedCall(appId: string, kind: 'ai' | 'net'): void            // no-op when no run is in flight
export function touchedGeneration(appId: string): number | undefined                  // the generation a delegated run last began on, until cleared
export function clearTouchedGeneration(appId: string, generation: number): void       // called on retraction and on setAppHostGeneration (a remount)
export function placeRun(live: { has(appId: string): boolean }, appId: string): Placement
```
`beginDelegatedRun` also records `touchedGeneration(appId) = generation` (D-PR1-3's sticky posture). `endDelegatedRun` does NOT clear it.

**`schedule/runScopedGate.ts`** (new)
```ts
export const DELEGATED_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000;
export interface ScheduledPrompt { request: NetConfirmRequest; run: { runId: string; title: string; appName: string } | undefined; signal: AbortSignal }   // run undefined ⇒ the sticky after-run ask
export interface RunScopedGateDeps {
  inner: NetConfirmGate;                                             // the page's standing→session chain
  delegated: (appId: string) => DelegatedRun | undefined;
  touched: (appId: string) => number | undefined;                    // the sticky generation
  liveGeneration: (appId: string) => number | undefined;
  prompt: (ask: ScheduledPrompt) => Promise<NetConfirmDecision>;    // parks on the SAME confirm queue, tagged; withdraws on signal abort
  timeoutMs?: number;
}
export function createRunScopedGate(deps: RunScopedGateDeps): NetConfirmGate
```
`confirm(request)`: (1) `run = delegated(request.appId)`; if a run is in flight: `run.asking || run.refused.length > 0` → push `{host, method, why:'already-asked'}`, return `false` (D-PR1-9); else `run.asking = true`, `prompt({request, run, signal: anySignal(run.signal, timeout)})` → granted → `run.granted += 1`, `true`; denied → push `why:'declined'`, `false`; timed out → push `why:'timed-out'`, `false`; finally `run.asking = false`. (2) No run, but `touched(appId) === liveGeneration(appId)` (the sticky posture): `prompt({request, run: undefined, signal: timeout})` → `true`/`false`, nothing recorded, nothing remembered. (3) Otherwise `inner.confirm(request)` — byte-for-byte today's chain. The gate never imports `state/net.ts`.

**`state/net.ts`** (changed)
- `PendingNetConfirm.scheduled?: { title: string; appName: string; runId: string } | { afterRun: true; appName: string }` — the dialog's tag.
- The session gate's prompt closure becomes `parkConfirm(request, tag?, signal?)`: one queue, one dialog; when `signal` aborts, the entry is withdrawn through the existing identity-keyed `denyParkedConfirmByRequest(request)`.
- `const runScopedGate = createRunScopedGate({ inner: standingGate, delegated: delegatedRunFor, touched: touchedGeneration, liveGeneration: liveAppHostGeneration, prompt: ({request, run, signal}) => parkConfirm(request, run ? { title: run.title, appName: run.appName, runId: run.runId } : { afterRun: true, appName }, signal) })`.
- **Only the frame path defaults to it (security 3, feasibility F7):** `createNetHandlerFor` uses `options.confirmGate ?? runScopedGate`; `connectedFetchDepsFor`'s default stays `standingGate` (the wizard probe, the provider tools and the sidecar pump are untouched).
- `createNetHandlerFor` options gain ONE seam, `scheduledRun?: () => boolean`; when it answers true at an auth-shaped failure the store entry carries `via: 'scheduled-run'`. `AuthShapedFailure.via?: 'scheduled-run'`.
- `net.ts` subscribes to `subscribeAppHosts`: on a retraction it calls `clearTouchedGeneration`.

**`run/NetConfirmDialog.tsx`** (changed): when `pending.scheduled` is set — the host-composed title `DELEGATED_CONFIRM.title` (never the task title), body `DELEGATED_CONFIRM.body(scheduled)` (the task title quoted inside it), the credentials sentence and the verbatim URL line unchanged (R-8), NO remember checkbox, acts `DELEGATED_CONFIRM.deny` / `DELEGATED_CONFIRM.allow`. The after-run variant uses `DELEGATED_CONFIRM.afterRunBody(appName)`.

**`schedule/copy.ts`** (new sentences, single-homed; the vocabulary scan bans "task"/"proposal" and the glossary keeps "run" a verb)
```ts
export const DELEGATED_CONFIRM = {
  title: 'a schedule wants to make a change',
  body: (title, appName, method, host) => `“${title}” is running ${appName} and wants to send a ${method} request to ${host}. You didn’t click this — allow it once, or don’t. Nothing is remembered; no answer in a minute means nothing is sent.`,
  afterRunBody: (appName, method, host) => `${appName} ran a schedule here earlier, so Snug asks every time: it wants to send a ${method} request to ${host}. Allow it once, or don’t — nothing is remembered until you reopen the app.`,
  allow: 'allow once', deny: 'don’t send',
};
export const needsYouDeclined   = (appName, verb) => ({ text: `you said don’t send — ${appName} didn’t ${verb}`, action: 'run now and review' });
export const needsYouUnanswered = (appName, verb) => ({ text: `${appName} asked to ${verb} and nobody answered in a minute — nothing was sent`, action: 'run now and review' });
export const needsYouAlreadyAsked = (appName, verb) => ({ text: `${appName} tried to ${verb} again after you answered — nothing more was sent`, action: 'run now and review' });
export const handedOverTwice  = (appName) => `${appName} opened and closed twice while this ran — nothing was recorded`;
export const closedAfterChange = (appName, host) => `${appName} was closed after a change was sent to ${host} — check there before running again`;
export const RUNNING_CHIP.inApp = (appName) => `running in ${appName}`;
export const AUTH_REPAIR_VIA_SCHEDULE = 'a schedule ran into this';
```
`outcomeOf` picks the sentence from the first refusal's `why` (the hidden path's refusals keep `why: 'declined'`-less `needsYou` as today — a hidden refusal has no dialog). `copy.result.ts`: the needs-you StateCard title becomes `needsYouTitle(appName)` ("<app> needs your OK") with `run.reason` as the body (today's fallback tail "Snug doesn't make changes while you're away" is wrong for a declined dialog).

**`schedule/scheduledTransport.ts`** (changed, additive)
```ts
export function createRunCountingTransport(inner: AgentTransport, deps: { delegated: () => DelegatedRun | undefined; onCounted: (run: DelegatedRun) => void }): AgentTransport
```
`send(wire, sendOptions)` → `inner.send(wire, sendOptions)` — **the options forwarded WHOLE** (streaming deltas and the signal must reach the inner transport; the sibling `createScheduledTransport` deliberately drops `onDelta`, this one must not); after the reply, when a run is in flight AND `countsAsAiCall(reply)`, `onCounted(run)`. No cap, no scrub.

**`run/appRuntime.ts`** (changed): for `attended: true` — `transport = createRunCountingTransport(createAppTransport(...), { delegated: () => delegatedRunFor(appId), onCounted: (run) => noteDelegatedCall(run.appId, 'ai') })`; `onNetCall ??= (id) => { noteDelegatedCall(id, 'net'); return true; }`; `scheduledRun: () => delegatedRunFor(appId) !== undefined`. For `attended: false` — `scheduledRun: () => true`; the transport untouched (the hidden path wraps it). Both call-time; the header names the two new call-time edges (`schedule/runPlacement.ts`, `schedule/scheduledTransport.ts`).

**`access/accessHandler.ts`** (changed, D-PR1-8): `createAccessHandlerFor(appId, frame)` reads `delegatedRunFor(appId)` PER OP: in flight → `request` answers `ACCESS_UNATTENDED`; `query` admits only `grant.unattended` (a session grant is not readable); the `read`/`refused` lines carry `attended: false`. Not in flight → unchanged.

**`schedule/appRun.ts`** (changed; NO new file — `dispatchLive`/`dispatchHidden` live here, PR-3 extracts them when the refresh engine becomes the second caller)
- `LiveAppHosts` gains `awaitReady(appId, ms): Promise<boolean>`, `generation(appId): number | undefined`; `subscribeEvents` listeners receive `generation`. `defaultAppRunDeps().live` maps them to the registry; the handshake test's fake DELEGATES to the real `state/appHosts.ts` (`registerAppHost`/`setAppHostGeneration`/`markAppHostAnnounced`/`publishAppEvent`/`__resetAppHostsForTest`) so it cannot drift.
- `HiddenMount.onUnmounted(): void` — `ScheduledRunHost` calls it from its effect cleanup keyed by `runId`; the hidden dispatch resolves an `unmounted` promise from it.
- `type DispatchEnd = { kind: 'result'; result } | { kind: 'timeout' } | { kind: 'aborted' } | { kind: 'failed'; message } | { kind: 'no-handler' } | { kind: 'handover'; to: Placement }`; `interface Dispatch { mode: 'manual' | 'unattended'; app; step; ctx; deps; key; payload; deadline: number }`.
- `dispatchLive(d)`: `awaitReady(announceTimeoutMs bounded by deadline)` — false + still registered → `no-handler`; false + retracted → unattended: `handover → hidden`, manual: `failed APP_CLOSED_SUMMARY`. Unattended: `beginDelegatedRun({…, appName: app.displayName, title: ctx.task.title, generation: live.generation(appId)})` (a refusal → `failed` with its reason). Subscribe events — accept a `schedule-result` ONLY from the hinted generation (security 5); kv write; `live.notify` hint; `awaitResult` bounded by `deadline` raced with retraction (unattended: `run.granted > 0 ? failed closedAfterChange : handover → hidden`; manual: `failed APP_CLOSED_SUMMARY`). `finally`: `endDelegatedRun` (returns the record; the executor keeps it).
- `dispatchHidden(d, gate)`: today's `runInHiddenFrame` body (unchanged guards and counting) PLUS, for `mode: 'unattended'`, a registry subscription that ends the attempt `handover → live` when the app REGISTERS; the `finally` clears the mount and, on a handover, awaits `onUnmounted` (≤ 1 s) before returning so the live dispatch never overlaps the hidden instance.
- `executeAppRun`: `manual` → `dispatchLive({mode:'manual'})` → `outcomeOf` (unchanged otherwise). Unattended → the D-PR1-6 pre-check → `deadline = now + resultTimeoutMs` → `placement = placeRun(deps.live, appId)` → loop: dispatch; `handover` with `handovers < MAX_HANDOVERS` → `handovers += 1; placement = end.to; continue`; a second → `failed handedOverTwice`; else `outcomeOf(app, record, end, calls)` where `record.refused` is the hidden gate's record or the delegated record's `refused`, `calls` the SUM of every attempt's tally, and `capped` set when the sum crossed a ceiling. Outer `finally`: `kvSet(appId, key, null)` once.
- `outcomeOf`: a refusal with `why` picks `needsYouDeclined` / `needsYouUnanswered` / `needsYouAlreadyAsked`; without `why` (the hidden path) today's `needsYou`. The record outranks `timeout`/`aborted` for both paths, as today.
- The doctrine headers are REWRITTEN in the same range: `appRun.ts:13-57`, `appRuntime.ts:21-24`, `net.ts:351-359` and `:434-438`, `appHosts.ts:40-43`, `engine-types.ts:23-28`, `scheduledConfirmGate.ts:4-11`, `queue.ts:334-336`.

**`schedule/engine-types.ts`**: `StepOutcome.capped?: boolean` only (no `placed` seam). **`schedule/executors.ts`** `finalizeOutcome` copies `capped`. **`schedule/queue.ts`**: `if (outcome.capped === true) capped = true;` and the capped fold's `reason` = the capped step's summary when present. No `RunningView` change.

**`schedule/RunningChip.tsx`**: reads `useStore(delegatedRunStore)`; when the running task's app has an entry, the label is `RUNNING_CHIP.inApp(appName)` and the aria-label `${title} is running in ${appName}`; `schedule-bits.css` shows the label at ≤ 830 px for that case (it is shorter than the default).

**`run/AuthRepairChip.tsx`**: when `failure.via === 'scheduled-run'`, appends ` · ${AUTH_REPAIR_VIA_SCHEDULE}` inline (one line; also in the tooltip).
