### Test lane (Opus 5.5 high) — what to write, where, FAILING FIRST (real file names)
- `__tests__/appHosts.test.ts` (extend, 8 pins today): `setAppHostGeneration` clears `announced` and fires NO host listener; `markAppHostAnnounced` ignores a stale generation; `awaitAppHostReady` — ready at once / becomes ready / retraction → false / timeout → false; `publishAppEvent` carries the generation to subscribers.
- `__tests__/runPlacement.test.ts` (new): `placeRun`; begin/end/refuse-second; `endDelegatedRun` aborts the signal and returns the record; `noteDelegatedCall` no-op without a run; `touchedGeneration` set by begin, kept by end, cleared by `clearTouchedGeneration`.
- `__tests__/runScopedGate.test.ts` (new): no run and no touch → inner consulted (and ONLY inner); a run → inner NEVER consulted (negative: an inner that would say yes); granted → true, `granted` incremented, nothing remembered; denied → false + `why:'declined'`; timeout → false + `why:'timed-out'` and the prompt's signal aborted; a second ask while one is parked → `why:'already-asked'` at once; after a refusal every later ask in the window → `already-asked`; the sticky after-run ask (touched === live generation) prompts and records nothing; a touched generation that no longer matches the live one → inner.
- `__tests__/netState.test.ts` (extend — security/scope's "right altitude" negative): remember a grant through the real `resolveNetConfirm({granted:true, rememberSession:true})`, `armStandingApproval(...)`, then `beginDelegatedRun(...)` and a POST through the REAL `createNetHandlerFor()` → a `scheduled`-tagged confirm is parked (not passed); `endDelegatedRun` → the parked entry is withdrawn (`netConfirmStore` head gone, the executor's promise answered false); after the run, a POST from the same generation parks an `afterRun`-tagged confirm; a retraction clears the sticky posture; `connectedFetchDepsFor()`'s default is STILL `standingGate` (a probe-shaped call never parks a scheduled confirm); `via: 'scheduled-run'` lands on the failure store when `scheduledRun()` answers true.
- `__tests__/confirmDialog.test.tsx` (extend; it IS the net dialog's suite): the scheduled variant renders `DELEGATED_CONFIRM.title`, the body with the task title, the URL, NO remember checkbox; *allow once* resolves `{granted: true}` with no `rememberSession`; *don’t send* resolves `{granted: false}`; the after-run variant renders `afterRunBody`.
- `__tests__/appRunHandshake.test.tsx` (the invert/keep table above, plus a NEW "delegated run" block): AC1; AC2 through the real default gate; AC3 verbatim; AC4 — hidden→live after `onUnmounted`, live→hidden on retraction, `handedOverTwice` on a second, `closedAfterChange` when a grant preceded the retraction, tallies SUMMED across attempts; AC5 readiness (both triggers); AC6 the pre-dispatch `capped` (no dispatch, `capped: true` survives `finalizeOutcome` — go through `createStepExecutor`), and a crossed ceiling inside the window marks `capped: true` without refusing; D-PR1-8 the access handler refuses `request` and a session-grant `query` while the run is in flight (`attended: false` on the line); D-PR1-10 a handover at 80 s still settles inside the queue bound (fake clock).
- `__tests__/scheduledTransport.test.ts` (extend): `createRunCountingTransport` counts only during a run, never refuses, forwards `onDelta` and `signal` (a delta reaches the caller).
- `__tests__/scheduleQueue.test.ts` (extend): `outcome.capped` folds `capped` with the step's summary as `reason`.
- `__tests__/runningChip.test.tsx` (extend): *running in <app>* for a delegated run; the aria-label.
- `__tests__/scheduleCopy.test.ts` (extend): the new sentences are pinned and the vocabulary scan passes over the three new/changed modules.
- `__tests__/scheduledRunHost.test.tsx` (extend): `onUnmounted` fires from the cleanup when the mount is cleared.
- An `AuthRepairChip` pin for the `via` line (in the suite that already renders the chip; find it by `grep -l AuthRepairChip apps/playground/src/__tests__`).
- e2e `apps/playground/e2e/schedule-flow.spec.ts` (extend, with the existing `fixtures/schedule-app.html`): the app OPEN at due time → no `[data-testid=scheduled-run-host] iframe` ever mounts and the result reads *done*. (A POST variant only if the fixture can POST; otherwise the unit pins stand.)
Run each suite with `pnpm --filter playground exec vitest run <file>`; the whole playground suite (`pnpm --filter playground test`), then `host` and `desktop`, at the end of the implementation lane. The e2e needs `SNUG_SERVER_PORT=8797` if the owner's `pnpm dev` holds the default ports (lesson 2026-10-10).

### Contract v2.1 notes — decided after test lane A (2026-10-10; binding for the implementation lane)

- **Copy signatures are the `copy.ts` block's:** `DELEGATED_CONFIRM.body(title, appName, method, host)` and `afterRunBody(appName, method, host)`; the dialog section's shorthand is corrected to those.
- **The after-run ask's `appName`:** `beginDelegatedRun` records the app's display name beside the touched generation; `runPlacement.ts` exports `touchedAppName(appId): string | undefined` (cleared with the generation); `state/net.ts`'s prompt closure reads it for the `{ afterRun: true, appName }` tag.
- **The sticky posture is matched structurally, not by a listener alone:** `beginDelegatedRun` captures the registration TOKEN (`state/appHosts.ts` exports `liveAppHostToken(appId): symbol | undefined`; `schedule/runPlacement.ts` may import that leaf) beside the generation; `touchedGeneration(appId)` answers the generation only while the captured token still equals the live one (both `undefined` counts as equal, so the unit suite without a registry passes). A retraction + re-registration mints a new token and the sticky posture dies by itself; `clearTouchedGeneration` stays as the belt `state/net.ts` calls from its `subscribeAppHosts` listener. (`__resetAppHostsForTest` wipes host listeners — a suite that resets the registry must not rely on the belt.)
- **`awaitAppHostReady` for an app not yet registered** waits for a registration AND its announce within the timeout (true), answers false on timeout, and false on the retraction of an existing registration.
- **The capped fold's reason** is the capped step's `summary` when present, else `'capped'` — one rule, whatever the step's own status.
- **Pins confirmed from lane A's inferences:** `endDelegatedRun` with a different `runId` returns `undefined` and leaves the run alone; a refused `beginDelegatedRun` does not move the touched generation; in the after-run ask a denial is not recorded and the next request asks again; `RUNNING_CHIP` gains `inApp` (its `toEqual` pin updated to `expect.any(Function)`).
- **Not pinned by a test:** the needs-you card's `needsYouTitle` rendering in `ResultDetail` — the implementation lane adds one `it` in the `ResultDetail` suite if cheap, else records it as a rider.
- **`pnpm --filter playground test` type-checks first**, so the full command stays red until every module exists; the implementation lane runs single suites with `pnpm --filter playground exec vitest run <file>` until the end, then the full command.

### Contract v2.2 notes — decided after test lane B (2026-10-10; binding for the implementation lane)

- **`outcomeOf` picks the first DECISIVE refusal** (`why: 'declined' | 'timed-out'`) over an `already-asked` one when both exist; only when every refusal is `already-asked` does the step read `needsYouAlreadyAsked`. (A second call parked behind the first would otherwise hide the user's own answer.)
- **`copy.capped(what)` takes `'AI call' | 'network call'`**, the words `ResultsList` already uses.
- **Timers:** every bound in the gate and the dispatchers is `setTimeout` + `Date.now()`/`ctx.now()` — never `AbortSignal.timeout`, never `performance.now()` (vitest's fake clock drives the suites).
- **D-PR1-8's code for a session-grant `query` during a delegated run is `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED`** with a `refused` history line carrying `attended: false` — the hidden path's own answer.
- **The sticky posture does not depend on the module-load listener** (v2.1's token match); `__resetAppHostsForTest` wiping listeners is harmless to it.
- **Lane B's `:579`/`:562` folds accepted:** the moved hidden-only pin drops its `open(appId)` line (an open app means no hidden mount now); the forged-result pin reads "a result published through the live registry is never taken for the hidden run".
- **e2e environment:** Node 22 (`better-sqlite3` fails to load under Node 24) and `SNUG_SERVER_PORT=8797` while the owner's dev server holds 8787; both specs fail today at "no hidden copy" (`Expected: 0, Received: 1`) after install, connect, schedule and reopen all pass.
- **Rider found by lane B:** `scheduledGate.test.ts:111` arms a standing grant but sends a literal URL, so its "armed grant" negative proves nothing (a standing grant applies only to symbolic `snug-connection://<slot>/…` requests). A test-lane fix (Opus) after the implementation lands.

### Docs range (R6) — the full list
ADR-0077 → `accepted` + the fold section; ADR-0074 status line → `accepted (amended by 0075, 0077)`; `docs/security/threat-model-delta-one-instance.md` (R-62 and R-70 rewritten; R-b closed; new rows: the sticky ask-only frame; over-attribution of a user's click to the run; a handover's re-dispatch; the ordinary db binding and the unscrubbed reply are the open app's own powers; the access door closed only for the window; one instance per TAB; presence ≠ visibility; a backgrounded tab's dialog times out) + the fold into `docs/threat-model.md` (version → 3.8, the "nineteen deltas" sentence → twenty, the ledger row + hash, a v3.8 note); `docs/lessons.md:211` amended; KB `85-scheduled-runs.md` — the intro (`:15-18`), the snippet comment (`:60-62`) and the rule (`:106-118`) rewritten to the design review's text ("One instance — and the same `runId` can reach a different one…", adjusted: open/close, not reload); `packages/sdk/src/schedule.ts` header + hook comment; `scheduled-runs-kb.test.ts:168` pins the new rule name; architecture `:835-850`; code-map `:113-114`; glossary (*delegated run*, *placement*, *handover*, *sticky ask*; *Handshake* and *Refusing gate* amended); next-steps (the open-copy items and R-b pruned; the remount-handover rider and the "end `capped` mid-window" rider added); the Plan's R4 `host.binding` sentence struck.

## PR-2 — Gate-2 contract v2 (2026-10-11; FOLDED from four fresh-context plan reviews on Fable xhigh — security 14 findings · design 16 · feasibility 16 · scope 16; six blockers across the four, every one folded below; v1 is in this branch's history at `be706e4`. The test lanes write against THIS; the implementation lanes implement THIS.)

Branch `feat/TASK-20261010-host-broker-pr2`, cut from `main` @ `cfaa91f` (PR-1 merged as #200). The routing rule stands: tests written and run on Opus 5.5 high; planning, decisions, implementation and reviews on Fable 5.1 xhigh. Host only — no schema bytes, no persisted record shape changes (D-PR2-16). Size: ~2k code · ~2k tests · docs — Gate 5 reviews the diff in two halves (engine, doors) plus one security pass over the whole.

### What the code says today (re-read 2026-10-11 — the anchors the contract stands on)

- `accessHandler.ts` closes `query` over the frame: `present()` = `attended && delegatedRunFor(appId) === undefined` (`:174`), `ownsSession` = `attended && session.generation === generation` (`:177`), the per-app minute FIRST (`:276`), then ownership → status → expiry (`markExpiredOnce`) → attendance (`logRefused` + `notGranted`) → sidecar (`suspendAccess('source-restricted')`) → `isReadOnlySelect` → `scopedRead` → ended-meanwhile → `maskRows` → `logRead` BEFORE the rows leave (`:336-349`) → `noteRead`. `list` (`:385`) filters `live && (!session || ownsSession) && (present || unattended)` SILENTLY — no refused line. `request` (`:187`) is the frame's own flow: presence, the ladder, the gather, the frame-ends re-check, the park.
- `grants.ts`: `grantsForApp(db, appId, now)` → `LiveGrantRow[]` with `live` (active, not expired, no sidecar fact); `readerGeneration(appId)` is the reader's attended generation while its view is open; a session grant made from host chrome while the app is CLOSED is stored with `generation: undefined` and binds to the NEXT frame (`:70-71`, `:179`, `consent.ts:213`) — the default duration from Settings or a chat beside a closed app lands exactly there.
- `scopedRead.ts`: one queue, one Worker, `runOnWorker(worker, job)` under `config.timeoutMs`, the bytes cache per grant (`ACCESS_SCOPED_CACHE_MS`), `clearScopedReadCache(grantId?)`. The responder (`scopedRead.worker.ts:49-58`) keys `isJob` on `statement` + `caps` — a job without `statement` is IGNORED (silence → the clock → the worker retired), so a dump job needs its own guard FIRST.
- `packages/db/src/scoped-read.ts`: steps 1–5 (open · drops + stray check · withhold · mask · `query_only` · drift) then the two guards and ONE statement through `runScratchStatement(scratch, sql, params, {maxRows, maxBytes, rowWeight})` (which steps every row to count `totalRows` even past the byte cap).
- `packages/db/src/userdb/access.ts:344-351` `coalesced()`: two `read` lines merge into `count` ONLY with an identical `sql` on both — a line without `sql` never coalesces. `PRUNE_TIERS` (`:297-302`) evict unguarded `read` lines first; 200 entries / 64 KiB per source.
- `userdb.ts` `scratchRun(appId, statements)` (`:2911`): `new SQL.Database(await runtimeSnapshot(appId))` inside try/finally; the forbidden-statement guard per entry; `jsonCharWeight`. The registry (`getAppSchema`) holds only DDL applied through `applyAppDdl`; a table the app's code created at runtime is in the runtime bytes (`describeAppData` reads them) and NOT in the registry.
- `agent/dataTools.ts`: `buildDataTools({appId, getDb, onProposal?, allowWrites?})`; `data_query` → `scratchRun` → `renderRows` (the `<query_result>` block + trailer); `data_propose_write` → `nonDataStatementReason` → dry run → `onProposal`; `executeApprovedWrite` dry-runs on the REAL app's bytes (`:273`). `packages/knowledge/prompts/tools/data-query.md:19-20` tells the brain "no other app's tables … exist from here — a query naming them fails"; `data-propose-write.md` says nothing of other apps.
- `agent/intentContext.ts` `buildIntentTurnContext(db, appId, intent, threadId)`: ONE production caller (`useBuilderChat.ts:707`); the data lane's block = identity + `### The app’s data` + DDL (cap `CONTEXT_CAPS.schema` 10,000) + doc titles.
- `agent/laneTools.ts` `laneToolsFor(route, deps)`: the exhaustive switch; `LaneToolDeps` is also built as a literal in `chatRouter.test.ts:95-103` and `useBuilderChatLanes.test.tsx:179,200`. `useBuilderChat.send` order: classifier (`:645-661`) → abort check (`:677`) → clarify → context (`:699-707`) → persist the user message (`:708-712`) → `presentCardTool` (`:728`) → `onScheduleProposal` (`:743-749`) → `laneToolsFor` (`:758-783`) → `agent.send` (`:785`). `classifierApplies` (`chatRouter.ts:79-87`): never under the host brain or a server turn; the data lane exists only for routed turns with an app attached under a direct brain.
- The schedule card is the model: `scheduleProposeTool.ts`, `scheduleCard.ts`, `schedule/ScheduleCard.tsx` (its lead line `SCHEDULE_CARD.lead` — ChatLog's rule: every model-authored card SAYS it is the agent asking), `useBuilderChat.ts` `:602, :743-749, :1021, :1046-1051, :503-506, :1172-1191`, `ChatLog.tsx:291-293`; `onResolveSchedule` is passed at `run/RunView.tsx:920, :1448, :1464, :1497` and `views/BuilderView.tsx:135`.
- `schedule/appThink.ts`: `renderThinkContext({app, schema, queries, maxRows})` (`:189-202`) — overview, `Schema:` (≤ 8 KiB), ONE `<query_result>` delimiter of fenced `data (name: …)` blocks, the trailer; `runContextQuery` (`:209`); `executors.ts:131-134` composes `transportFor` lazily on first use.
- `access/egress.ts`: `readerBrainRoute(appId, hasKey)` (`:80-97`) is the app transport's order; the chat beside an ATTACHED app routes IDENTICALLY (`useBuilderChat.ts:430-470` → `createDirectBuilder` → `builder.ts:390-400`: the same per-app pin under the same guard, the same key rows, the same overrides; `freshPick` only with NO app attached) — measured. The chat DOOR exists only where the data lane runs: a keyed BYOK or local route (never host, subscription, webllm or the demo brain).
- `access/userAsk.ts` `startUserAsk(readerAppId, {renew?, decline?})` opens an EXISTING pending instead of parking (`:76-79`) and discards `openReview`'s false (`:102`); parks `provenance: 'user'` with the host's fixed purpose and no `hash` (`consent.ts:240` records a decline only with one). `AccessConsentSheet.tsx:216-228` renders `userPurpose` for a user-provenance pending and offers *don't allow* only for `provenance === 'app'` (`:259`); `AccessStrip.tsx:116` renders app asks only. `ParkAccessRequestInput.settle` (`consent.ts:188`) is called once with the outcome and the grant.
- `accessAskLadder` (`consent.ts:100-107`): rate · `accessAsksOff()` (the Settings switch *never let apps ask*) · `db.isAccessMuted(reader)` · the decline by `accessRequestHash({hints, renew})` (semantic fields; the purpose excluded).
- The vocabulary scan (`accessCopy.test.ts:584-700`) walks EVERY `.ts`/`.tsx` under `access/` but `copy.ts` and refuses the whole words *grant · reader · scope · log* in string literals (TYPE literals included), template parts and JSX text; `MACHINE_TOKENS` exempts only the suspend reasons. `copy.ts` is a leaf (protocol types + a relevance type).
- `accessLogEntrySchema` (`protocol/src/access.ts:426`) is `strictObject` with NO `via` seat; `ACCESS_PROVENANCES` is `['app', 'user']` — D3 is forced: a `via` or `provenance: 'chat'` key is a strict-parse REFUSAL and is never written in PR-2.
- The kit page is 2,733,634 B against the 2,750,000 B ceiling — 16,366 B of headroom (D6: a crossing is a STOP for the owner's ceiling conversation, never a silent bump).

### PR-2 decisions (each reversible by one word; the fold that produced it in brackets)

- **D-PR2-1 One policy, one verdict; effects are NAMED by the policy and PERFORMED by the service.** `access/policy.ts` is pure (protocol imports, the leaf `access/grantFacts.ts`, `./copy.js`, and grants.ts TYPES): `authorise(ctx, caller, act) → Verdict`. A refusal may name ONE effect — `'refused-line'` · `'mark-expired'` · `'suspend-source-restricted'` — which `access/service.ts` performs exactly where today's handler performs it (`read` only; `list`, `materialise` and `recordRead` apply none — `list` writes nothing today, and the others iterate LIVE rows). The order is today's `query` order. [DS-1/F2/S-F4: the effect was `'log-refused'`, which the vocabulary scan refuses; F4: `durationOf`/`expiresAtOf`/`isExpired` move to the leaf `grantFacts.ts`, re-exported by grants.ts unchanged for every caller.]
- **D-PR2-2 Three callers, no `user` caller.** `{ kind: 'frame', appId, generation?, present }` (generation absent = the hidden frame; `present` = the handler's `present()` read per op), `{ kind: 'chat', appId, threadId, liveGeneration? }` (`liveGeneration` = `readerGeneration(appId)` at the call), `{ kind: 'schedule', appId, taskId, runId }`. The sheets and Settings keep reading grants.ts directly.
- **D-PR2-3 Session grants per door — an UNBOUND entry is never readable.** `ownsGrant(caller, found)` = `found.grant.readerAppId === caller.appId && (!found.session || (callerGen !== undefined && found.generation !== undefined && found.generation === callerGen))` with `callerGen` = `frame.generation` | `chat.liveGeneration` | (schedule → `undefined`). So: a frame owns its own generation's session grant (today's rule); the chat owns one only while the reader's view is OPEN in this tab at the same generation; a session grant made from host chrome while the app was closed (`generation: undefined`) is readable by NOBODY until the next frame binds it; the schedule never. [S1/DS-4/F1/S-F3 — the four reviews' shared blocker: `undefined === undefined` admitted the default grant to a closed app's chat.] **For the person** [DS-4]: a chat ask reviewed while the reader is CLOSED (`readerGeneration(appId) === undefined`) seeds the sheet to `{ duration: 'day', unattended: false }` through the existing seed mechanism (`seedRenewal` generalised to `seedSheet`), so the default allow is one the chat can use now; *while it's open* stays offered. Recorded as the one-word reversal of D13's session default for this case.
- **D-PR2-4 Attendance per door.** Frame: `present`. Chat: `true` (the user typed) — the chat door does NOT read `delegatedRunFor`: while a delegated run is in flight on app A's frame, A's frame handler refuses session grants and asks, but the chat beside A still reads with `attended: true` — rows reach the user's brain, never the frame [S11, named in the table and the delta]. Schedule: `false` for EVERY trigger, *run now* included — a scheduled question always reads the away set, so what the person sees on *run now and review* is what the schedule will see [S12 (a)]; the line carries `attended: false`. **Rule 4 is D30 as a rule** [S-F13]: `!attended && (found.session || !grant.unattended)` → refused — a session grant is never read without someone present, whatever `unattended` says (today's handler admits that latent cell; the one deliberate invert of the characterisation, named in the table).
- **D-PR2-5 The per-minute limiter stays a frame-only effect.** The chat door is bounded by the user's turns and the lazy read (D-PR2-7), the scheduler door by the run ceilings; both by the Worker's serialisation, the per-set cap and the 10 s per-grant cache.
- **D-PR2-6 The dump.** `scopedScratchDump` selects ONLY the scope's recorded columns of each granted table after the withhold and the value mask ran on the copy (a credential-named column is never in a scope; a generated column that still builds a credential fails closed as today). `types[i]` is the declared type text ONLY when it matches the allow-list `/^(INT|INTEGER|REAL|TEXT|BLOB|NUMERIC|BOOLEAN|DATE|DATETIME|VARCHAR|CHAR|DOUBLE|FLOAT|DECIMAL)(\(\d{1,5}(,\s?\d{1,5})?\))?$/i`, else `''` — the ONE home of that rule; app-authored type text never reaches a prompt or a CREATE [S5]. Caps: `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_ROWS` 5,000 and `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_BYTES` 2 MiB (UTF-8 JSON) per table, and ONE `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_SET_BYTES` 8 MiB across the whole set in grant order [S7/S-F8]: `materialiseGrants` passes each dump `maxTotalBytes = SET − bytes so far`; inside a dump the per-table `maxBytes = min(MAX_BYTES, remaining)`, the crossing table is CUT (`truncated`, `totalRows`), later tables get `maxBytes 0` → `rows: []`, `truncated: true`, `totalRows` [F11]; a grant whose dump would start past the set cap is skipped `too-large`. The Worker's clock for a dump is `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_TIMEOUT_MS` 5 s. Drift suspends `source-changed` as a read does; a timeout or a failure SKIPS the grant and counts NO strike (the SQL is the host's); `unavailable` (no Worker) skips every grant.
- **D-PR2-7 Dump without a line; ONE `read` line per grant when rows are about to reach a brain, BEFORE they do.** `materialise(caller)` dumps (cached 10 s per grant) and writes NO history line — the DDL block carries names, types and counts, the `list`-class disclosure. `recordRead(caller, set, { grantIds, sql? })` writes one `read` line per named grant — `{kind:'read', grantId, readerAppId, readerName, tables: <every granted table>, rows: <rows handed over>, sql?: loggedSql(sql), attended}` — after re-checking the grant is still owned, active and unexpired (else that grant is `refused` with `reason: 'ended'`), and `noteRead`s it; a line the history refuses → `reason: 'failed'` and that grant's tables are NOT handed over. The chat door calls it from `data_query` ONLY when the statement names a shared table (D-PR2-10) with the real SQL, so the db coalesces identical reads within the minute as it does an app's; the scheduler door calls it once per step with no `sql` (the rows go into the prompt). [S-F1 — the lazy line; S2/F3: the v1 claim "count: 3" was false.] **`coalesced()` is extended** [S2/F3]: two `read` lines with `sql === undefined` on BOTH sides, the same `grantId`, `readerAppId`, `attended` and `tables`, within `ACCESS_LOG_COALESCE_MS`, merge (`count` summed, newest `rows`); a line with `sql` still coalesces only with an identical one; `imported` never.
- **D-PR2-8 Aliases, de-collided against the reader's FULL object names.** `aliasFor(sourceName, grantedTables, taken)`: the slug is lower-case, every run of non-`[a-z0-9]` → `_`, trimmed, ≤ 32 chars, prefixed `app_` when it does not start with a letter; accepted only when none of `${slug}__${t}` (for every granted table) is in `taken`, else `2`, `3`, … appended to the slug. `taken` (lower-cased) = the registry's tables AND views (`db.getAppSchema(readerAppId)`) ∪ the runtime's table names (`db.describeAppData(readerAppId)` — a table the app's code created at runtime) ∪ `snug_kv` ∪ every `sqlite_*` ∪ every earlier alias's full names [S4/F12]. The set's `readOnlyTables` (every `name`, sorted) is the exact-name list the propose tool refuses against.
- **D-PR2-9 `scratchRun(appId, statements, { attach })`.** After opening the copy and before any statement: `CREATE TABLE "<name>" ("c1", "c2", …)` — UNTYPED, always (rows keep their storage class; the brain learns types from the DDL block) — and the rows through one prepared `INSERT` per table [S-F7: no type text ever reaches a CREATE]. The copy stays throwaway. A name that collides in the opened copy throws `SCRATCH_UNAVAILABLE` naming the table — `data_query` reports it as text; nothing else runs (the belt behind D-PR2-8).
- **D-PR2-10 The chat door opens on a `data`-lane turn, attaches lazily, and is gated like the frame.** In `useBuilderChat.send`, once the route is known: when `route.lane === 'data'`, an app is attached, `appMayUseAccess(contextTarget)` [S8 — not `allows('access')` alone: an unowned reader's chat may not read what its frame may not] and `chatDoorOpen(chatBrainRoute(db, contextTarget))` [DS-6/S-F5: `kind ∈ {anthropic, openai, local}` — never the host, subscription, webllm or demo brains] → `const { accessService } = await import('../access/service.js')` (a lazy edge, the load-order rule [F13]) → `materialise({kind:'chat', appId: contextTarget, threadId, liveGeneration: readerGeneration(contextTarget)})` ONCE → re-check `controller.signal.aborted` exactly as `:677` does [F14] → the set goes to `buildIntentTurnContext(…, { shared })` and to `laneToolsFor(…, { shared })`. `data_query` then: `named = set.tables.filter(t => namesTable(sql, t.name))` (the whole-identifier match — bare, `"quoted"`, `` `quoted` ``, `[bracketed]`, case-insensitive, conservatively also inside a string literal [F16]); when `named` is empty the query runs with NO attach and writes nothing; else `recordRead(caller, set, { grantIds: distinct, sql })` → a refused grant answers the brain `CHAT_DOOR.ended(sourceName)` and the query does not run → attach the named grants' tables → `scratchRun`. The `answer`, `provider`, `schedule` and `feature` lanes never materialise. `data_propose_write`, `executeApprovedWrite` and `appThink.dryRunCandidates` NEVER attach — a shared name there is "no such table" on a copy of the real database [S14/S-F6].
- **D-PR2-11 The brain asks; the ladder still applies; the user allows.** `access_propose {purpose, hints?}` — the purpose through `accessPurposeSchema`, the hints through `accessHintsSchema`. The tool is OFFERED only when `appMayUseAccess(target) && !accessAsksOff() && !db.isAccessMuted(target)` [S-F9], and at the CALL it answers `NOT staged` by name — the Settings switch is on · the user turned off asks from this app · the user said don't allow to this ask (`db.listAccessDeclines(target)` holds `accessRequestHash({hints: hints ?? {}})`) · an ask is already waiting for the user (`pendingAccessStore.get()[target] !== undefined`) · a second proposal this turn [DS-2/S6/F6/DS-8]. The card persists on `meta.access` (additive) and is re-validated on every read; the rehydrate and `readAccessCardRow` DROP a card whose `appId` is not the thread's pinned app, and *review* resolves the app from the thread row, never the card [S10]. *Review* → `startUserAsk(appId, { ask: { purpose, hints, settle } })`, which with `ask` ALWAYS parks anew (`parkAccessRequest` dismisses an older pending — an app's ask is answered not-now, the user's act wins) with `hash: accessRequestHash({hints: ask.hints ?? {}})`, `askedIn: 'chat'`, `provenance: 'user'` (D3), and answers `'opened' | 'answer-other-first' | 'no-app'`: under the yield rule (`openReview` false) the just-parked pending is dismissed at once and the card shows `CONSENT_UI.answerOtherFirst` with its acts kept [DS-8/F5/S-F10]. The sheet for a chat ask shows *don't allow*, which records the decline (so "won't ask this again" is TRUE through the tool's rung) [DS-2]. Outcomes the card can show: allowed · not now · declined (no `muted` — no card surface mutes) [DS-2/F6]. The strip's line (with *stop*) remains the undo surface when the reader is open beside the rail; the card's outcome line carries no undo and equals `STRIP_OUTCOME.allowed` [DS-16].
- **D-PR2-12 One derivation, pinned, not rewired; the chat line is derived.** `egress.ts` exports `chatBrainRoute(db, appId)` (= `readerBrainRoute`, measured identical for an attached app) and `chatDoorOpen(route)`; `accessEgress.test.ts` pins `chatBrainRoute` equal to the config the REAL `createDirectBuilder` send path builds across every brain arm. `useBuilderChat`'s memo is NOT rewired (synchronous, no db at hand) — recorded in next-steps as "pinned, not rewired — rewire when builder.ts and transport.ts share one route resolver". `egressFor` pushes `{kind:'chat', text: EGRESS.chat(readerName)}` right after the brain line ONLY when `chatDoorOpen(chatBrainRoute(db, readerAppId))` and the reader is owned [DS-6/S-F5]; the `away` line gains its *ask <Reader>'s AI* clause and the away box's hint says the AI is asked on a schedule too [DS-7].
- **D-PR2-13 The scheduler door.** `appThink` asks `deps.sharedFor(appId, ctx)` (the executors' default: a lazy import of the service [F13], `materialise({kind:'schedule', appId, taskId: ctx.task.id, runId: ctx.run.id})` then `recordRead(caller, set, {})` — the set handed back holds only the recorded grants; tests inject). `renderThinkContext` with a NON-empty set renders, after the `Schema:` block, `renderSharedDdl(set)` (the *From <Source>* lines and any `CHAT_DOOR.unreadable` note — the host's sentences, OUTSIDE the delimiter) and, INSIDE the one `<query_result>` delimiter, the step's own query fences FIRST, then one `data (name: <alias>__<table>)` fence per shared table under ONE `step.context.maxRows` budget shared across the shared tables in order (the cut said in band); the trailer unchanged [DS-12/S7]. An EMPTY set renders byte-identically to today [F14]. The step's own `SELECT`s run with the set attached.
- **D-PR2-14 `request` stays in the handler.** The ladder, the gather and the park are the frame's own flow; only the presence decision goes through `authorise(…, {kind:'ask'})`. The chat `ask` cell is unreachable in PR-2 (the card's review goes straight to `startUserAsk`) and is pinned `ok` for PR-3's `discover` [S-F14].
- **D-PR2-15 Vocabulary.** Every new module under `access/` (`policy.ts`, `service.ts`, `materialise.ts`, `limits.ts`, `grantFacts.ts`, `AccessCard.tsx`) carries no banned word in ANY string literal — effect names, act and caller kinds, skip reasons included (the scan reads type literals too); every sentence a person reads lives in `access/copy.ts` and is pinned by `accessCopy.test.ts`.
- **D-PR2-16 No persisted shape changes.** The grant record, the log entry, `PendingWriteProposal` and the chat row's `meta` keep their schemas; `meta.access` is a new key beside `meta.schedule`. [S-F6: v1's `readOnlyTables` on the proposal is DROPPED — the real database holds no alias table, so the belt was SQLite's own "no such table".]
- **D-PR2-17 The propose tool refuses an alias table by name, before any dry run.** `data_propose_write` refuses a batch in which any statement names a shared table (the D-PR2-10 matcher) with `CHAT_DOOR.readOnly(sourceName)` — covering `INSERT INTO own SELECT … FROM <alias>` too — and stages nothing; its dry run never attaches (D-PR2-10), so a statement that slipped past would fail "no such table" on the preview and again on `executeApprovedWrite`'s own dry run; the real database is never touched.
- **D-PR2-18 Materialise and recordRead never throw.** Every grant-level failure is a skip with a reason (`drift` · `timeout` · `unavailable` · `failed` · `too-large` · `copy-failed` · `ended`); a service-level failure answers the empty set with ONE skip whose `sourceName` is the reader's own name and `reason: 'failed'` — the turn proceeds without shared tables and the DDL block says so.
- **D-PR2-19 Measure the kit twice** [F8]: at the start of the implementation phase and again before Gate 5 (`pnpm --filter host build && pnpm run check-host-kit`), the byte count journaled; over the ceiling → STOP for the owner's ceiling conversation (D6) with the optional bytes named (the card and the propose tool are the only optional ones).
- **D-PR2-20 The dist seam** [F9]: after every `packages/db` edit `pnpm --filter @snugprotocol/db build`; after any prompt edit `pnpm --filter @snugprotocol/knowledge gen:content && pnpm --filter @snugprotocol/knowledge build`; the playground's `test` script is red at tsc until every module lands, so lanes report single suites with `pnpm --filter playground exec vitest run <file>`.

### Module contracts

**`access/limits.ts`** (new; PR-3 moves them to protocol Appendix B)
```ts
export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_ROWS = 5_000;
export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024;
export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_SET_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024;
export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000;
```

**`access/grantFacts.ts`** (new LEAF: protocol imports only) — `durationOf`, `expiresAtOf`, `isExpired` moved verbatim from grants.ts; grants.ts re-exports them (no caller changes).

**`access/policy.ts`** (new, PURE — imports protocol values/types, `./grantFacts.js`, `./copy.js`, and grants.ts TYPES only)
```ts
export type AccessCaller =
  | { kind: 'frame'; appId: string; generation?: number; present: boolean }
  | { kind: 'chat'; appId: string; threadId: string; liveGeneration?: number }
  | { kind: 'schedule'; appId: string; taskId: string; runId: string };
export type AccessAct =
  | { kind: 'ask' }
  | { kind: 'read'; grantId: string; sql: string }
  | { kind: 'materialise'; grantId: string }
  | { kind: 'list' }
  | { kind: 'release'; grantId: string };
export interface PolicyContext {
  now: number;
  find(grantId: string): FoundAccessGrant | undefined;        // the service binds findAccessGrant(db, ·)
  sourceRestricted(sourceAppId: string): boolean;             // the service binds appHasSidecarFact(db, ·)
}
export type PolicyEffect = 'refused-line' | 'mark-expired' | 'suspend-source-restricted';
export interface Refusal { ok: false; code: string; message: string; retryable: boolean; effect?: PolicyEffect; grant?: FoundAccessGrant }
export type Verdict = { ok: true; grant?: FoundAccessGrant; attended: boolean } | Refusal;
export function attendedFor(caller: AccessCaller): boolean;                     // frame → present · chat → true · schedule → false
export function callerGeneration(caller: AccessCaller): number | undefined;    // frame.generation · chat.liveGeneration · undefined
export function ownsGrant(caller: AccessCaller, found: FoundAccessGrant): boolean;   // D-PR2-3
export function authorise(ctx: PolicyContext, caller: AccessCaller, act: AccessAct): Verdict;
```
Rules, in order (`read` and `materialise`; `materialise` skips rule 6):
1. `found = ctx.find(grantId)`; absent or `!ownsGrant` → `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED` / `notGranted` / not retryable (no effect) — another reader's grant, an unknown id and an unbound session grant are byte-identical.
2. `status === 'revoked'` → `ACCESS_REVOKED` / `revoked`; `'suspended'` → `ACCESS_REVOKED` / `sourceChanged` when `suspendedReason === 'source-changed'` else `paused`; not retryable.
3. `isExpired(grant, now)` → `ACCESS_EXPIRED` / `expired`, effect `mark-expired`.
4. `attended = attendedFor(caller)`; `!attended && (found.session || !grant.unattended)` → `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED` / `notGranted`, effect `refused-line` for a FRAME caller only (a chat or schedule skip is silent).
5. `ctx.sourceRestricted(grant.sourceAppId)` → `ACCESS_REVOKED` / `paused`, effect `suspend-source-restricted`.
6. (`read` only) `!isReadOnlySelect(sql)` → `ACCESS_QUERY_REFUSED` / `queryRefused`.
→ `{ ok: true, grant: found, attended }`.
`ask`: a frame with `present: false` or a `schedule` caller → `ACCESS_UNATTENDED` / `unattended` / retryable; a chat caller → ok. `list` → ok (the service filters rows with `authorise(materialise)` per row and applies no effect — today's `list` admission exactly). `release`: `found` and `ownsGrant` → ok, else `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED`.

**`access/service.ts`** (new; the ONE engine)
```ts
export interface AccessReadInput { grantId: string; sql: string; params?: readonly unknown[] }
export interface MaterialisedTable {
  grantId: string; sourceAppId: string; sourceName: string; alias: string;
  name: string;                                   // `${alias}__${table}` — the table in the scratch copy
  table: string;                                  // the source's own table name
  columns: string[]; types: string[];             // types: '' where the dump allowed none
  rows: unknown[][]; truncated: boolean; totalRows?: number;
  duration: AccessDuration; expiresAt?: string;
}
export type MaterialiseSkipReason = 'drift' | 'timeout' | 'unavailable' | 'failed' | 'too-large' | 'copy-failed' | 'ended';
export interface MaterialiseSkip { grantId: string; sourceAppId: string; sourceName: string; reason: MaterialiseSkipReason }
export interface MaterialisedSet { tables: MaterialisedTable[]; skipped: MaterialiseSkip[]; readOnlyTables: string[] }
export const EMPTY_MATERIALISED_SET: MaterialisedSet;
export interface RecordReadInput { grantIds: readonly string[]; sql?: string }
export interface RecordReadOutcome { recorded: string[]; refused: MaterialiseSkip[] }
export interface AccessService {
  read(caller: AccessCaller, input: AccessReadInput): Promise<AccessHandlerResult>;   // today's `query` body, byte-identical for frames
  materialise(caller: AccessCaller): Promise<MaterialisedSet>;                       // dumps; no line; never throws
  recordRead(caller: AccessCaller, set: MaterialisedSet, input: RecordReadInput): Promise<RecordReadOutcome>;   // the line BEFORE rows reach a brain; never throws
  list(caller: AccessCaller): AccessHandlerResult;
  release(caller: AccessCaller, grantId: string): Promise<AccessHandlerResult>;
}
export function accessService(): AccessService;                                     // one instance over accessDeps()
export function __setAccessServiceForTests(over?: Partial<AccessService>): void;    // the __setAccessDepsForTests pattern (lane B spies materialise/recordRead)
```
`read`: `queryRateLimited` FIRST for a frame caller → `authorise` → the named effect (`logRefused` with `attended: false` / `markExpiredOnce` / `suspendAccess('source-restricted')`) → `scopedRead` with `stillLive` → ended-meanwhile → `maskRows` → `logRead` BEFORE the rows leave → `noteRead` → the answer; the outcome switch moves here verbatim. `materialise`: `grantsForApp(db, appId, now).reads.filter(live)` in order → per row `authorise(materialise)` (a refusal → skipped silently with the verdict mapped to a reason: not owned/unattended → simply absent, never a line) → `scopedDump` with `stillLive` and `maxTotalBytes = SET − so far` → after the dump: `current = find(grantId)`; not owned / not active / expired → skip `ended` [S3] → the alias → the tables. `recordRead`: per `grantId` in the set: re-check as above (→ `ended`), `logRead` (a refusal → `failed`), `noteRead`; answers the recorded ids. `list`/`release`: today's bodies over the caller.

**`access/materialise.ts`** (new)
```ts
export function aliasFor(sourceName: string, grantedTables: readonly string[], taken: ReadonlySet<string>): string;   // D-PR2-8
export function takenNamesFor(db: UserDb, readerAppId: string): Promise<Set<string>>;                                 // registry tables+views ∪ runtime tables ∪ snug_kv ∪ sqlite_*
export function materialiseGrants(db: UserDb, caller: AccessCaller, rows: readonly LiveGrantRow[], deps: { dump: typeof scopedDump; now(): number; find(grantId: string): FoundAccessGrant | undefined }): Promise<MaterialisedSet>;
export function toAttach(set: MaterialisedSet, grantIds?: readonly string[]): ScratchAttachTable[];
export function namesTable(sql: string, name: string): boolean;                                                       // the whole-identifier match (D-PR2-10)
```

**`packages/db/src/scoped-read.ts`** (+; the shared core factored into one private `scopeCopy(scratch, scope)`; the read byte-identical — `scoped-read.test.ts`'s existing rows unchanged)
```ts
export interface ScopedDumpCaps { maxRows: number; maxBytes: number; maxTotalBytes: number }
export interface ScopedDumpTable { name: string; columns: string[]; types: string[]; rows: unknown[][]; truncated?: boolean; totalRows?: number }
export type ScopedDumpResult =
  | { ok: true; tables: ScopedDumpTable[] }
  | { ok: false; reason: 'drift' | 'failed'; drift?: ScopedReadDrift; message: string }
  | { ok: false; reason: 'copy-failed'; message: string };
export function scopedScratchDump(SQL: SqlJsStatic, bytes: Uint8Array, scope: ScopedReadScope, caps: ScopedDumpCaps): ScopedDumpResult;
export const DUMP_TYPE_ALLOW: RegExp;   // D-PR2-6's allow-list, the one home
```
**`packages/db/src/userdb/access.ts`** (changed): `coalesced()` per D-PR2-7. **`packages/db/src/userdb/userdb.ts`** (+): `export interface ScratchAttachTable { name: string; columns: readonly string[]; rows: ReadonlyArray<readonly unknown[]> }`; `scratchRun(appId, statements, options?: { attach?: readonly ScratchAttachTable[] })` per D-PR2-9. Exported from the package index with `ScopedDump*`, `DUMP_TYPE_ALLOW`.

**`access/scopedRead.worker.ts`** (+): `ScopedDumpJob { id: number; kind: 'dump'; bytes: Uint8Array; scope: ScopedReadScope; caps: ScopedDumpCaps }`; `isDumpJob` (id a number, `kind === 'dump'`, bytes a `Uint8Array`, scope a record, caps with numeric `maxRows`/`maxBytes`/`maxTotalBytes`) is checked FIRST, then `isJob` unchanged; a dump job that fails its guard answers `{ id, result: failed(…) }` for its id, never silence [F10]; `ScopedReadAnswer.result` widens to `ScopedReadResult | ScopedDumpResult`. **`access/scopedRead.ts`** (+): `scopedDump(input: { grantId; bytes; scope; caps; stillLive?; timeoutMs? }): Promise<ScopedDumpOutcome>` (`ScopedDumpResult | { ok: false; reason: 'timeout' | 'unavailable' | 'ended'; message }`) — the same queue, the same bytes cache, `runOnWorker` generalised to a job and a per-job timeout; the dump RESULT cached per grant for `ACCESS_SCOPED_CACHE_MS` in a second map that `clearScopedReadCache` and `resetScopedReadForTests` clear too (a cached result is keyed by grant AND caps, so a different `maxTotalBytes` is a miss).

**`access/accessHandler.ts`** (changed): `caller()` = `{ kind: 'frame', appId, ...(frame.attended ? { generation: frame.generation } : {}), present: present() }` read PER OP; `request` keeps its body with `authorise(ctx, caller(), {kind:'ask'})` as its first line; `query` → `accessService().read(caller(), …)`; `list` → `.list(caller())`; `release` → `.release(caller(), grantId)`. `answerFor` stays; `maskRows`, `loggedSql`, `logRead`, `logRefused` MOVE to the service. `accessHandler.test.ts` is untouched and green.

**`agent/dataTools.ts`** (changed)
```ts
export interface SharedTablesSeat { set: MaterialisedSet; recordRead(grantIds: readonly string[], sql: string): Promise<RecordReadOutcome> }
export interface BuildDataToolsOptions { …; shared?: SharedTablesSeat }
```
`data_query`: D-PR2-10's lazy path (`namesTable` from `access/materialise.ts` — a leaf function; `toAttach(set, grantIds)`). `data_propose_write`: D-PR2-17's refusal BEFORE the class check; no attach. `executeApprovedWrite` UNCHANGED.

**`agent/sharedDdl.ts`** (new, pure; the ONE renderer both doors call): `renderSharedDdl(set: MaterialisedSet, now: number): string` — per source `CHAT_DOOR.heading(sourceName, duration, expiresAt, now)` then one `CHAT_DOOR.tableLine(…)` per table; after the LAST source `CHAT_DOOR.rule` ONCE; each skip `CHAT_DOOR.unreadable(sourceName)`; an empty set → `''` [DS-11/F14]. Column names rendered verbatim when `/^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$/`, else `"double-quoted"`.

**`agent/intentContext.ts`** (changed): `buildIntentTurnContext(db, appId, intent, threadId, options?: { shared?: MaterialisedSet; now?: number })`; the data lane's block appends `renderSharedDdl(shared)` after the app's DDL and before the doc titles, under the same `CONTEXT_CAPS.schema` cap (the marker says so).

**`agent/laneTools.ts`** (changed): `LaneToolDeps.shared?: SharedTablesSeat`, `LaneToolDeps.onAccessProposal: (proposal: AccessProposal, appId: string) => boolean` (required — the exhaustive discipline; lane B updates the two literal deps objects); `data` → `buildDataTools({…, shared})` + `presentCardTool` + the propose tool under D-PR2-11's offer rule; `answer` → `presentCardTool` + the same; `accessProposeTool.js` imported lazily like its siblings. **`agent/tools.ts`** `buildByokTools(sink, { …, onAccessProposal? })` adds the tool beside `schedule_propose` under the same offer rule when the sink has an app target; **`agent/builder.ts`** `BuildHandlers.onAccessProposal?` threaded like `onScheduleProposal`.

**`agent/accessProposeTool.ts`** (new)
```ts
export const ACCESS_PROPOSE_TOOL_NAME = 'access_propose';
export interface AccessProposal { purpose: string; hints?: AccessHints }
export type OnAccessProposal = (proposal: AccessProposal, appId: string) => boolean | void;
export function buildAccessProposeTool(options: { getDb: () => Promise<UserDb>; resolveAppId: () => Promise<string | undefined>; onProposal?: OnAccessProposal }): AgentTool;
```
`inputSchema { purpose: string; hints?: { words?: string[]; tables?: string[] } }`; the REAL schemas decide. Answers, each one line the model reads: a purpose failing the display rule → `Error: …` naming the rule; no installed app → `Error: … needs an installed app`; `onProposal` absent → `NOT staged: … tell the user to allow it from the app’s access (⋈)`; the five `NOT staged` rungs of D-PR2-11 (`asks to read other apps are turned off here` · `the user turned off asks from this app` · `the user said don’t allow to this ask — carry on without the other app’s data` · `an ask is already waiting for the user` · `a request is already waiting in this turn`); ok → `Suggested (NOT allowed — the user decides on the card): "<purpose>" … Tell the user it is waiting for their review; never say it was allowed.`

**`packages/knowledge/prompts/tools/access-propose.md`** (new; registered as a `ToolPromptName` member + `gen:content`) [DS-10] — the body: *"## Tool: access propose — Asks the user to let THIS app read another app's data. It does NOT grant anything: the host shows your ask as a card in this chat, and *review* opens the host's own sheet where the user picks the app, the tables and how long. Call it only when the user's question needs data this app does not hold ("compare this with my ledger", "what's in my pantry") and your context has no *From <Source>* section — if one is there, you already have that data: query it with data_query. Call it once per turn. If the host answers NOT staged, tell the user why and carry on without the other app's data. Say what you asked for and that it is waiting for their review; never say it was allowed. ### Parameter: purpose — Required. One line, at most 200 characters, in the user's words, saying what you will do with the data: "to compare spending with the ledger". Never speak as Snug or the host; never claim the user already agreed. ### Parameter: hints — Optional: `words` (up to 16, each at most 32 characters) and `tables` (up to 16 names you expect, like `transactions`) that help the host rank the user's apps. Never shown to another app."* **`data-query.md`** [DS-3]: lines 19-20 become *"This tool reaches this app's data — and, when your context has a *From <Source>* section, those tables too, by the exact names shown there (`ledger__transactions`, never `Ledger.transactions`). Nothing else exists from here: no host tables, no other app's tables beyond the ones listed."* **`data-propose-write.md`**: after its first paragraph, *"Never propose a change to a table listed under *From <Source>* — those are read-only copies of another app's data; tell the user they can change it in that app."* A knowledge test pins the three sentences.

**`agent/accessCard.ts`** (new; mirrors `scheduleCard.ts`)
```ts
export type AccessCardResolution =
  | { kind: 'allowed'; sourceName: string; tables: string[]; duration: AccessDuration }
  | { kind: 'not-now' } | { kind: 'declined' } | { kind: 'dismissed' } | { kind: 'failed' };
export interface AccessCardState { purpose: string; hints?: AccessHints; appId: string; threadId: string; resolution?: AccessCardResolution; messageRowId?: number }
export type PersistedAccessCard = Omit<AccessCardState, 'messageRowId'>;
export function stageAccessCard(proposal: AccessProposal, options: { appId: string; threadId: string }): AccessCardState;
export function accessCardToMeta(card: AccessCardState): { access: PersistedAccessCard };
export function metaToAccessCard(meta: unknown): AccessCardState | undefined;      // purpose/hints re-parsed through the protocol schemas; appId/threadId non-empty; an unknown resolution → undefined
export function persistAccessResolution(db, resolved: AccessCardState): void;      // MERGES the row's meta
export function readAccessCardRow(db, threadId, rowId): AccessCardState | undefined;   // ALSO drops a card whose appId ≠ db.getThread(threadId)?.appId (S10)
```

**`access/AccessCard.tsx`** (new; copy in `ACCESS_CARD`): props `{ card, messageId, busy, onResolve?: ResolveAccessCard }`, `ResolveAccessCard = (card, messageId, resolution: AccessCardResolution) => void`. Renders FIRST `ACCESS_CARD.lead` (*the agent asks:*, the schedule card's slot — the anti-imitation line every model-authored card carries [DS-5]), then the title `CONSENT_SHEET.userTitle(readerName)` (the exact title the sheet its *review* opens shows; the reader's name from the library row of the THREAD's app), the purpose as a text node inside `<q className="access-quote">` (bidi-isolated, the strip's rule), the acts *review* / *not now* (disabled while `busy` or without `onResolve`). *Review* → `startUserAsk(threadApp, { ask: { purpose, hints, settle } })`; `'answer-other-first'` → the card shows `CONSENT_UI.answerOtherFirst`, acts kept; `'no-app'` → stale. *Not now* → `onResolve(card, messageId, {kind:'not-now'})`. After a resolution the one outcome line as `<div role="status" tabIndex={-1} data-testid="access-card-outcome" data-outcome={kind}>` that takes focus on mount when `document.activeElement` is null or `document.body` (the strip's `OutcomeLine` rule) [DS-9]: `STRIP_OUTCOME.allowed(…)` · `ACCESS_CARD.notNowLine` · `ACCESS_CARD.declined`. The row is the truth on a remount (`readAccessCardRow`).

**`access/userAsk.ts`** (changed): `UserAskOptions.ask?: { purpose: string; hints?: AccessHints; settle?: (outcome: ConsentOutcome) => void }`; `startUserAsk(readerAppId, opts): Promise<'opened' | 'answer-other-first' | 'no-app'>` — without `ask` the recipe is byte-identical but for the answer; with `ask`: ALWAYS parks anew (hash, `askedIn: 'chat'`, `settle`, the candidates ranked by `ask.hints`), seeds `{duration:'day', unattended:false}` when `readerGeneration(readerAppId) === undefined` (D-PR2-3), then `openReview`; false → `answerAccess(pending, {kind:'dismissed'})` at once → `'answer-other-first'`. **`access/consent.ts`**: `PendingAccessRequest.askedIn?: 'chat'`, `ParkAccessRequestInput.askedIn?: 'chat'`; `renewSeedOf`/`seedRenewal` generalised to `sheetSeedOf`/`seedSheet` (same shape). **`access/AccessConsentSheet.tsx`**: the says-branch is three-way — `pending.askedIn === 'chat'` → `CONSENT_SHEET.askedInChat(readerName)` + the `<q>` quote (test id `access-sheet-says`), the `access-sheet-user` node absent, *don't allow* OFFERED; else `provenance === 'app'` → `says` + quote; else `userPurpose` [DS-15]. `AccessStrip.tsx` is NOT touched (a chat ask has no strip).

**`agent/useBuilderChat.ts`** (changed): `ChatMessage.access?: AccessCardState`; per turn `stagedAccess`; `onAccessProposal` → `stageAccessCard` + `patchMessage(agentId, { access })`; the finalize spread + the row-id patch; the rehydrate `metaToAccessCard(m.meta)` filtered by the thread's pin (S10); `resolveAccess(card, messageId, resolution)` on `BuilderChat` (the `resolveSchedule` body); D-PR2-10's materialisation. **`views/ChatLog.tsx`**: `onResolveAccess?: ResolveAccessCard`; `<AccessCard>` rendered beside the schedule card when `message.access !== undefined && allows('access')`. **`run/RunView.tsx`** (`:920, :1448, :1464, :1497`) and **`views/BuilderView.tsx`** (`:135`) pass `onResolveAccess` wherever they pass `onResolveSchedule` [S-F11 — named, not "found by grep"].

**`schedule/appThink.ts`** (changed): `AppThinkDeps.sharedFor?(appId: string, ctx: StepContext): Promise<MaterialisedSet>`; `RenderThinkContextInput.shared?: MaterialisedSet; now?: number`; `renderThinkContext` per D-PR2-13; `runContextQuery(db, appId, sql, attach)`. **`schedule/executors.ts`**: `defaultSharedFor` composed beside `transportFor` (a lazy import of the service).

**`access/egress.ts`** (changed): `EgressKind` += `'chat'`; `export function chatBrainRoute(db: UserDb, appId: string): ReaderBrainRoute`; `export function chatDoorOpen(route: ReaderBrainRoute): boolean` (`kind ∈ {anthropic, openai, local}`); `egressFor` per D-PR2-12 (the reader's name from `db.getApp(readerAppId)`).

**`access/copy.ts`** (+; every new sentence single-homed and pinned; the date and duration words are copy.ts's own `shortDate` and `DURATION_WORDS`)
```ts
export const CHAT_DOOR = {
  heading: (sourceName, duration: AccessDuration, expiresAt: string | undefined, now: number) => `### From ${sourceName} (read-only · access ${duration === 'session' ? "while it's open" : expiresAt !== undefined ? `until ${shortDate(expiresAt, now)}` : 'until you stop it'})`,
  tableLine: (name, columns: readonly string[], types: readonly string[], rows: number, truncated: boolean, totalRows?: number) => string,   // `ledger__transactions(id INTEGER, amount REAL, note TEXT) — 412 rows` · `… — showing 5000 of 9120 rows`
  rule: 'the tables under *From …* above are copies of other apps’ data the user allowed this chat to read — query them with data_query by their full names as written (ledger__transactions); never propose a change to them',
  unreadable: (sourceName) => `${sourceName}’s data could not be read this time`,
  ended: (sourceName) => `${sourceName}’s access ended — ask the user to allow it again`,
  readOnly: (sourceName) => `${sourceName}’s data can’t be part of a change here — read it with data_query and propose the change with the values`,
};
export const ACCESS_CARD = { lead: 'the agent asks:', review: 'review', notNow: 'not now', notNowLine: 'not now — the agent may ask again', declined: 'you said don’t allow — the agent won’t ask this again', waiting: 'waiting for your review' };
CONSENT_SHEET.askedInChat = (readerName) => `asked in ${readerName}’s chat:`;
CONSENT_SHEET.awayHint = (readerName) => `if ${readerName} ever runs on a schedule — or its AI is asked on one`;
EGRESS.chat = (readerName) => `the chat beside ${readerName} — the same AI — whenever you ask it about ${readerName}’s data`;
EGRESS.away = (readerName) => `also while you’re away — on a schedule it can read and send with no one watching, and so can a scheduled *ask ${readerName}’s AI*`;
```

### Characterisation (AC9, amended) — the table and its two drivers [F7]
`__tests__/accessPolicyTable.ts` holds the hand-written TABLE (fixtures + expected `{code, message, retryable, effect}` per cell) and a driver over the REAL `createAccessHandlerFor`; `__tests__/accessHandlerTable.test.ts` drives the frame cells through the handler — GREEN TODAY, proving the table; `__tests__/accessPolicy.test.ts` drives every cell through `authorise` — red until R1. Dimensions: callers {frame visible g0 present · frame visible g0 during a delegated run (`beginDelegatedRun`, present false) · frame visible g1 · frame hidden · chat liveGeneration 0 · chat with none · chat liveGeneration 0 during a delegated run · schedule} × grants {persisted active (± unattended) · revoked · suspended source-changed · suspended imported · expired by the clock · session g0 (± unattended) · session UNBOUND (made from host chrome with the app closed, before any handler composes) · a source holding a WhatsApp fact} × acts {`read` with `SELECT 1` and with `DELETE …` · `materialise` · `list` · `release` · `ask`}. Mute, decline and rate stay the handler's ladder, pinned by `accessHandler.test.ts`. The ONE named invert: {frame during a delegated run} × {session g0, unattended:true} — today admitted, the policy refuses (D30 as a rule).

### Contract v2.1 notes — decided after the three test lanes (2026-10-11; binding for the implementation lanes)

- **A-Q1 Effects belong to the RULE, not the act:** `authorise(materialise)` names the same effect a `read` refusal would (`refused-line` for a frame, `mark-expired`, `suspend-source-restricted`); the service PERFORMS an effect only on `read`. The table pins the effect on materialise refusals.
- **A-Q2 Composition binds:** a visible frame or a chat whose view is open composes the handler, which binds an unbound session grant (`noteReaderGeneration`) — so the hidden frame, the closed-app chat and the schedule are the callers for which a grant can still be unbound. `ownsGrant` on a hand-built unbound grant is false for EVERY caller.
- **A-Q3 `release` of a stopped grant:** the verdict is ok (found and owned); the service answers `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED` because `releaseAccess` answers `'not-granted'` — today's byte-identical answer.
- **A-Q4 `scopedDump.bytes`** is the read's thunk (`() => Promise<Uint8Array>`, the same bytes cache); `timeoutMs` defaults to `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_TIMEOUT_MS` (scopedRead.ts imports `./limits.js`); the service passes it explicitly anyway.
- **A-Q5 Bytes** are `jsonUtf8Weight` per kept row (the scoped read's rule); `weight > maxBytes` cuts; a grant whose remaining set budget is `<= 0` is `too-large`.
- **A-Q6 The `sqlite_` family** is a prefix rule in `aliasFor` (a candidate full name starting with `sqlite_` is taken); `takenNamesFor` holds the concrete names plus `snug_kv`.
- **A-Q7 `recordRead` for a multi-table grant:** `rows` = the sum handed over across that grant's tables in the set; `tables` = every table in the grant's scope.
- **A-Q8 Order:** `tables`, `skipped` and `refused` in grant order (`grantsForApp(...).reads.filter(live)`).
- **B-Q1 The seed names are `sheetSeedOf` / `seedSheet`** (the old `renewSeedOf`/`seedRenewal` are REMOVED — the orchestrator moved the two existing rows to the new names; nothing else imports them).
- **B-Q2 The card's selectors:** the acts are `<button>`s whose text is exactly `ACCESS_CARD.review` / `ACCESS_CARD.notNow`; the purpose is `q.access-quote`; the lead line is a text node; the outcome line carries `data-testid="access-card-outcome"`, `role="status"`, `tabIndex={-1}`, `data-outcome`.
- **B-Q3 A `dismissed` outcome (the yield rule) is NOT a resolution:** the card writes nothing, shows `CONSENT_UI.answerOtherFirst`, keeps its acts live; `onResolve` is not called.
- **B-Q4 Any refused grant** (`ended` or `failed`) answers the brain `CHAT_DOOR.ended(sourceName)` and the statement never runs.
- **B-Q5 The shared DDL block has its OWN cap** of `CONTEXT_CAPS.schema` (the app's DDL keeps its own) — the truncation marker applies per block.
- **B-Q6 A chat ask's pending carries the brain's purpose** as `pending.purpose`, `provenance: 'user'`, `askedIn: 'chat'`; the sheet's title stays `CONSENT_SHEET.userTitle(reader)`.
- **B-Q7 The offer rule is lane-based, not brain-based:** `access_propose` rides the `data` and `answer` lanes (and the builder set) under `appMayUseAccess && !accessAsksOff() && !isAccessMuted` whatever the brain; only the MATERIALISATION needs `chatDoorOpen`.
- **C-Q1 The scheduler door's `recordRead`** passes `{ grantIds: <every grant id in the set> }` and no `sql`.
- **C-Q2 `sharedFor` is asked only when a brain will be sent something:** after `transportFor` resolved and the app exists, after the abort check, before `send`.
- **C-Q3 The heading date** uses the step's clock (`ctx.now()`), never the wall clock; the chat door uses `accessDeps().now()`.
- **C-Q4 The shared fences** take the step fences' exact shape (`\`\`\`data (name: <alias>__<table>)`, the JSON columns line, one JSON row per line, the closing fence, the cut line); the shared budget is ONE `step.context.maxRows` across the shared tables, separate from each step query's own `maxRows`; a non-empty set with no step queries still emits the delimiter and the trailer.
- **C-Q5 The table line's count** is the set's own `rows.length` with the dump's `truncated`/`totalRows` (the renderer knows no scheduler cut); the identifier quoting lives in `renderSharedDdl` (copy.ts formats what it is given).
- **C-Q6 `renderSharedDdl` groups by grant in set order** (one heading per grant, a grant's tables in order); a skip-only set renders the unreadable notes and no rule.
- **Lazy loads in the suites:** a module that does not exist yet is loaded through `import(/* @vite-ignore */ specifier)` with a variable specifier (a literal dynamic import of a missing module fails the whole file at transform time); the implementation changes nothing about that.
- **The vocabulary plant rows** in `accessCopy.test.ts` append to the REAL new modules for milliseconds and restore them: the D2a lanes do NOT run `accessCopy.test.ts`'s vocabulary rows while another lane edits those modules (`-t` to the copy rows only); D2b and the verification lane run the whole file alone. *(Moot since the Gate-5 fold DOORS-4: the proof runs in memory over the real bytes and writes no production module.)*
- **The lane's D-PR2-9 attach type** is loosened locally in `scratch-run.test.ts` and the dump types are written out locally in `scoped-read.test.ts` so tsc stays clean until the exports land — the implementation exports the contract's names and the lane's local types go away in the Gate-5 fold (a rider, not a blocker).

## PR-3 — Gate-2 contract v2 (2026-10-11; FOLDED from four fresh-context plan reviews on Fable xhigh — security 19 findings · feasibility 21 · design 23 · scope 12; six blockers across the four, every one folded below; v1 is in this branch's history at `5770cb7`. The test lanes write against THIS; the implementation lanes implement THIS.)

Branch `feat/TASK-20261010-host-broker-pr3`, cut from `main` @ `8412b6c` (PR-2 merged as #201). The routing rule stands: tests written and run on Opus 5.5 high; planning, decisions, implementation and reviews on Fable 5.1 xhigh. **High** — `packages/protocol` (spec 1.1 → 1.2), `packages/runner` (the seat's three ops), the one sanctioned crossing of C2 widens from read to a bounded, journaled, undoable write. ONE PR (the owner merges one; D-PR3-1), **≈ 9k lines** on the feasibility estimate (F19); Gate 5 reviews it in two halves (the wire + db; the engine + UI + docs) plus one security pass over the whole, and the 3a/3b split stays available to Gate 5 if the halves cannot be read.

### What the code says today (re-read 2026-10-11 — the anchors the contract stands on; corrected per F17/SC9)
