- **Measured for the Write Broker** (sql.js 1.14.1 / SQLite 3.49.1): a `CREATE TEMP TRIGGER … ON t` with UNQUALIFIED names in its body captures an INSERT's new rowids (explicit `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` values included) and an UPDATE/DELETE's OLD rows exactly, inside the same transaction; a nested `BEGIN` fails "cannot start a transaction within a transaction"; `pragma_table_list` answers `wr` and `type`; `RETURNING` works (refused anyway); TEMP objects never travel in `export()`; an app's own trigger on `t` fires on a host write; `INSERT OR REPLACE`'s conflict-delete fires no `BEFORE DELETE` trigger (`recursive_triggers` off); `sqlite3_changes()` excludes trigger, foreign-key-action and REPLACE-resolution changes.
- **The kit page**: 2,742,366 B against 2,750,000 — 7,634 B of headroom; the inline Worker is a 1:1 JS string literal in the page (≈ 51.8k chars today), so Worker bytes count fully. **The feasibility estimate for PR-3 is +35–47 KB minified (F11) — the ceiling conversation is certain, not conditional (D-PR3-28).**

### PR-3 decisions (each reversible by one word; the fold that produced it in brackets)

- **D-PR3-1 One PR, two review halves.** The owner merges one PR. Two commit ranges — (a) the wire + db; (b) the engine + UI + docs — reviewed fresh-context each, plus one security pass over the whole. The spec push to `snugprotocol/spec` is the owner's act after the merge (one line, wait for a yes).
- **D-PR3-2 The grant RECORD v2 — `columns` are the GRANTED columns; `withheld` the rest; defaults are OMITTED.** `scope.tables[].{ name, columns (1..64, the ticked non-credential columns), withheld?: string[] (the UNTICKED non-credential columns; disjoint from `columns`; never credential-named), write?: true }`, `kv?: true`, `refresh?: true`, `access: 'read' | 'write'` (`'write'` iff some table has `write` — `superRefine`), `provenance: 'app' | 'user' | 'chat'`. Strict, additive, every new seat optional with the 1.1 meaning when absent. **The writer omits every default** (`withheld` when empty; `write`/`kv`/`refresh` when false) and `canonicalAccessGrantIntent` normalises absent ≡ default, so a defaults-off v2 record is byte-identical AND intent-identical to a 1.1 record (a backup taken before the upgrade restores unsuspended) [F10]; a widened grant (any seat set) is not intent-identical. A v2 record on a 1.1 hub is a strict-parse refusal → unreadable → NO access (fail closed, Q19). What may be read is `columns` — one rule; `withheld` exists for the drift check and the sheet. A generated column (`table_xinfo` hidden 2/3) may still be LISTED in `columns` for reads; it is never nameable in a write (D-PR3-9) [S2].
- **D-PR3-3 The VIEW v2 (tolerant) — additive keys appear ONLY when set.** `tables[].{ name, columns, write?: true }`, `kv?: true`, `refresh?: true`, `access: 'read' | 'write'`. `withheld` is never on the view. A read-only grant's view is byte-identical to today's (`accessHandler.test.ts:252-263` and the e2e key list stay green).
- **D-PR3-4 The ask may say `write` — from an app.** The `request` variant gains `access?: 'read' | 'write'` (strict, optional; absent = read); the handler threads it as `wantsWrite` → `ParkAccessRequestInput` → `PendingAccessRequest` → the sheet and the strip (D-PR3-23/24). `AccessRequestSemantics` gains `access` (absent = `'read'`) — a declined read ask does not decline a later write ask, nor the reverse. The SDK's `request(purpose, { hints, renew, access })`. **The chat's `access_propose` does NOT gain `access` in PR-3** (its consumer, the chat's write, lands with PR-4 — D-PR3-16) [SC2].
- **D-PR3-5 Feature detection is the 1.1 fallback, not a key.** No `accessOps` on host-ready [SC4]. A 1.1 host answers `write`/`refresh`/`discover`, and a `request` carrying `access`, `MALFORMED` → `ok: false` with the `requestId` recovered (today's `answerUnparseable`); KB 87 v2 says: "a `MALFORMED` answer to `write`, `refresh` or `discover`, or to an ask that carried `access`, means the runner predates 1.2 — show the honest fallback, and ask again without `access`". One-word reversal: "accessOps" (its natural home is PR-6, with the embedded helper that would read it).
- **D-PR3-6 The log entry v2.** `kind: + 'wrote' | 'undone' | 'refreshed'`; `via?: 'app' | 'chat' | 'schedule' | 'user'` (which door asked — `user` for an act from host chrome: *undo*); `authority?: 'permission' | 'approval' | 'task'` on a `wrote` line (the standing per-table tick · a card or result-page approve · PR-4's task authority — **spelled without the banned word** `write-grant`); `changes?: int ≥ 0` on `wrote`/`undone`; `undoId?: id` on `wrote`; `tables: [<the one table>]` on `wrote`/`undone`; `sql?` on `wrote` = the forward statement as the history keeps it (`loggedSql`: walked whole, cut at 200) — shown behind the existing disclosure as *what it changed* [DS7]; `reason?` on `refreshed` = ONE of `ACCESS_REFRESH_REASONS = ['no-handler', 'timeout', 'capped', 'refused', 'failed']` (machine tokens worded in `copy.ts` like `SUSPEND_HISTORY_WORDS`; a handler's summary never reaches a line) [DS13]. `grantId`, `readerAppId`, `readerName` stay REQUIRED (every `wrote`/`undone`/`refreshed` line has a grant — own-mode writes write no line, D-PR3-8). `coalesced()` stays reads-only. `PROTECTED_KINDS` gains `wrote`. `clearAccessLog` keeps every non-`read` kind. `AccessHistory`'s empty state counts `read`, `wrote` and `refreshed`; `useHasAccessState` is unchanged (a `wrote` line implies a grant, which implies `readBy`).
- **D-PR3-7 The undo journal `accessUndo:<targetAppId>` holds ROWS, never SQL.** ONE JSON array row per TARGET app, newest first; `accessUndoEntrySchema` (strict): `{ id (uuid v4), at, expiresAt, authority: 'permission' | 'approval' | 'task', via, grantId, readerAppId, readerName (host-written), steps: [{ table, op: 'insert' | 'update' | 'delete', columns: string[] (UPDATE: the SET columns in order; DELETE: every non-generated column; INSERT: the INSERT list), rows: [{ rowid: int, before?: unknown[], after?: unknown[] }] (INSERT: `after`; UPDATE: `before` and `after` over `columns`; DELETE: `before`) }] (1..64 steps; the frame's `write` is exactly one), undoneAt? }` — **no statement text is persisted or executed from the file** (the `wrote` line's `sql` is display only): the undo BUILDS its statements at undo time from the structure, with every identifier quoted and validated against the live `table_xinfo`, every value a bound parameter [S3]. Caps: ≤ `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES` (64 KiB) per entry, ≤ `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_ENTRIES` (20) LIVE entries per (target, reader), ≤ `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_ROW_BYTES` (256 KiB) per row [F20], kept `ACCESS_UNDO_TTL_MS` (7 d). `putAccessUndo` prunes expired and undone entries first; **a strict-mode write whose reader already holds 20 live entries on that target is REFUSED** (`ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED`, "too many changes are waiting to be undone — undo them or wait") rather than evicting [S9]; the ONE credential walk runs per captured cell UNDER ITS COLUMN (`findRecordCredential({ [column]: value })`) before the entry is written — a hit refuses the write (D-PR3-12) [S12]. `markAccessUndone` sets `undoneAt`; `listAccessUndo` answers live entries. Swept by `deleteApp`; DROPPED on an untrusted import, kept on a trusted pull (the user's own device). `accessWants:<appId>` is DEFINED here — `accessWantsSchema` = ADR §11's shape verbatim: `z.array(z.strictObject({ purpose: accessPurposeSchema, hints: accessHintsSchema.optional() })).min(1).max(ACCESS_WANTS_MAX_ENTRIES).superRefine(bytes ≤ ACCESS_WANTS_MAX_BYTES)` [SC12]; the key helpers; the untrusted import drops it (PR-5 re-parses from the HTML).
- **D-PR3-8 ONE Write Broker, two modes — only strict mode journals.** **Strict mode** (another app's data — authority `permission` from a frame's `write`): the full rule set of D-PR3-9/10/12, undo REQUIRED, the journal entry, the `wrote` line, the doorbells. **Own mode** (the app's OWN data the user approved — `executeApprovedWrite`'s two callers): the DML class only (`nonDataStatementReason`, today's guard), any statement shape, the previewed-count drift halt, atomic and counted through the SAME synchronous driver seat with `requireUndo: false, capture: false` — **NO journal, NO history line, NO `undoId`** (an own-app undo surface lands with PR-4's result page, AC31) — plus `data-changed { table }` to the app's own live frame after the commit (the open app re-reads what its chat changed; `table` from `dmlTargetTable`, omitted when unknown). `executeApprovedWrite` is a thin caller; `ExecuteApprovedWriteOutcome` is unchanged; its characterisation stays green. [DS1/SC1 — one-word reversal: "journal"]
- **D-PR3-9 The pure half (`packages/db/src/brokered-write.ts`) — parse, plan, apply, literal-valued.**
  - `parseBrokeredStatement(sql) → ParsedDml | ParseRefusal`: a small tokenizer (comments, 'strings', "quoted"/`quoted`/[bracketed] identifiers, numbers, `?`/`?NNN`/`:name`/`@name`/`$name` placeholders, punctuation); ONE statement (`isSqlTailEmpty` on the rest); the verb is a plain `INSERT INTO t (c1, …) VALUES (…)[, (…)]` (the column list REQUIRED; no `OR …`, no `DEFAULT VALUES`, no `ON CONFLICT`), `UPDATE t SET c = <value>, … WHERE <terms>` (no `OR …`, no `FROM`), or `DELETE FROM t WHERE <terms>`; `WHERE` REQUIRED for UPDATE/DELETE; no `WITH`, no `RETURNING`, no `ORDER BY`/`LIMIT`, no schema qualifier, no `t.col` qualifier. **A value is a literal or a placeholder. A WHERE is one or more terms — `<column> <op> <literal|placeholder>` (`=`, `<>`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `LIKE`), `<column> IN (<literals|placeholders>)`, `<column> IS [NOT] NULL` — joined by `AND`/`OR`/`NOT` with parentheses; no function call, no `CASE`, no arithmetic, no `SELECT`/`EXISTS` anywhere; any other token is refused BY NAME** [SC3 — one-word reversal: "functions"]. Answers `{ op, table, columns: <every column the statement NAMES>, setColumns?: string[] (UPDATE, SET order), insertColumns?: string[], literals: string[], placeholders: number, sql }`.
  - `tableFacts(db: Database, table: string, scope) → { ok: true; columns: ColumnFact[] } | { ok: false; reason: 'refused'; message }` reads the RAW handle (never a scoped copy — the copy has no triggers [F3]): the table exists and is `type = 'table'` (not virtual/shadow), not WITHOUT ROWID (`pragma_table_list.wr`); NO trigger on it in `sqlite_master` OR `sqlite_temp_master` [S13]; its CREATE text carries no `ON CONFLICT REPLACE` and (when the table has any column outside `columns`) no `CHECK` [S2's residual, folded as a refusal]; when `PRAGMA foreign_keys` is 1, no `pragma_foreign_key_list` of ANY table names `t` with an `on_delete`/`on_update` action other than NO ACTION [S13]; for a DELETE: **every live column of the table is in the grant's `columns`** (no withheld, no credential-named, no generated column) [S5]; `ColumnFact = { name, generated: boolean }` from `table_xinfo`. Refusals are worded for the reader without the why: "that table can't be changed from another app" [DS19].
  - `planBrokeredWrite(SQL, bytes, scope, statement, caps: { maxChanges, maxUndoBytes }) → BrokeredPlan | PlanRefusal` (runs in the Worker): parse → `table` ∈ scope with `write` (else `not-granted`) → `tableFacts` on the RAW copy → every NAMED column (SET, the INSERT list, WHERE) ⊆ that table's `columns`, minus every generated column, minus every column `scopeCopy` reports it MASKED a cell in — a withheld, credential-named, generated or masked column anywhere is refused BY NAME before any dry run (`WHERE api_key LIKE 'sk-a%'` with `changes` as the oracle; a generated column over NULLed inputs; a masked value — are the three oracles) [S1, S2, F-S1] → every literal and bound param through `findRecordCredential` (refused on a hit) → `scopeCopy(scratch, scope, { forPlan: true })` — the drops, the withhold, the mask, the drift check, **no `query_only`, `ignore_check_constraints` reset to 0** [F2, S5] → a DRY RUN inside `BEGIN … ROLLBACK` with the capture objects (D-PR3-10) → `changes ≤ maxChanges` (else `too-many`), the captured rows' JSON ≤ `maxUndoBytes` (else `too-large`) → `BrokeredPlan { sql, params, op, table, columns, setColumns?, insertColumns?, previewChanges }` — NO row and no value leaves the Worker. Reasons: `refused` · `not-granted` · `drift` · `too-many` · `too-large` · `failed` · `copy-failed`.
  - `applyBrokeredBatch(db: Database, plans, caps, options: { expectChanges?: number[]; requireUndo: boolean; capture: boolean; steps?: UndoStep[] (an undo: the structured steps to REVERSE) }) → Applied | ApplyRefusal` — SYNCHRONOUS on the real handle: `tableFacts` AGAIN on the live handle per plan (the apply's check is the rule, the Worker's the early answer) → `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` ("within a transaction" → `busy`) → per plan: the capture objects (when `capture`) → exec (for an undo, the statement BUILT from the step: INSERT-undo `DELETE FROM t WHERE rowid = ? AND c1 IS ? AND …` (the `after` values), UPDATE-undo `UPDATE t SET c1 = ?, … WHERE rowid = ? AND c1 IS ? AND …` (SET = `before`, guard = `after`), DELETE-undo `INSERT INTO t (rowid, c1, …) VALUES (?, …)` (`before`)) → `getRowsModified()` → `> maxChanges` → ROLLBACK `too-many`; `expectChanges[i] !== changes` → ROLLBACK `drifted`; an undo whose any statement changed ≠ 1 row → ROLLBACK `drifted` ("<Source> changed these rows since") [S18] → the captured rows read, each cell walked under its column (a hit → `requireUndo ? ROLLBACK 'unsafe' : no undo`) → JSON ≤ `maxUndoBytes` else `requireUndo ? ROLLBACK 'too-large' : no undo` → drop the capture objects → `COMMIT` → `{ steps: UndoStep[], undoable }`. **A `committed` flag; `finally` runs `ROLLBACK` (errors swallowed) whenever it is false, THEN drops the capture objects; a throw is answered `failed`** [S11]. `forbiddenStatementReason` is the belt on every plan's `sql` and on every built undo statement [S3].
- **D-PR3-10 Capture by TEMP trigger, with per-apply names.** Inside the transaction: `CREATE TEMP TABLE snug_undo_rows_<hex8> (snug_rowid INTEGER, c0, c1, …)` (positional columns, mapped back by index — a target column named `r` or `rowid` never collides [F18]) and `CREATE TEMP TRIGGER snug_undo_trigger_<hex8> BEFORE UPDATE|DELETE ON t BEGIN INSERT INTO snug_undo_rows_<hex8> VALUES (old.rowid, old.c1, …); END` (UPDATE captures `old.*` AND `new.*` of the SET columns; INSERT: `AFTER INSERT … VALUES (new.rowid, new.c1, …)` for the INSERT list), created WITHOUT `IF NOT EXISTS` (a creation failure is `failed`, and the drop can never remove an object the app made) [S11]; run the statement; read the rows; drop both. The affected set is the statement's OWN.
- **D-PR3-11 The driver seat is the ONLY way onto the handle; the driver knows when a namespace is mid-transaction.** `SnugDbDriver.brokeredWrite(namespace, input: { plans, caps, expectChanges?, requireUndo, capture, steps? }): Promise<BrokeredWriteResult>` — the driver imports `applyBrokeredBatch`, runs it on the namespace's handle, `markDirty`s, raises the write signal (D-PR3-20), and answers errors as data; the userdb FACADE forwards it with the tombstone/`noteNamespace` rules; **`scheduledDbDriver` carries it and answers `{ ok: false, code: FORBIDDEN_STATEMENT, retryable: false }` as data, never forwards** [F15/SC9]; NO new `DbRequestFrame` op. **`createDbDriver` keeps a per-namespace `inTransaction` flag** from the verbs `runExec` runs successfully (`BEGIN`/`SAVEPOINT` set it; `COMMIT`/`END`/`ROLLBACK` to the outermost clear it; `runImport` clears it) and `brokeredWrite` answers `busy` (retryable) from the flag BEFORE touching the handle; the apply's own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` is the belt [F4/S4]. The `instanceId` seat is retired as a mechanism; `executeApprovedWrite`'s awaited `BEGIN`/exec/`COMMIT` through `handle` goes away with it (its interleaving hazard with it). **Named as pre-existing in the delta and in next-steps:** sql.js `export()` closes and reopens the connection, so a source frame's open transaction is discarded by the next persist (≤ 250 ms), read, dump, plan or `describeAppData` — PR-3 does not change the read path; the driver's persistence without reopening is its own task.
- **D-PR3-12 C1 in a write, five refusals, all decided from the schema and the scope — never from a row's value.** (1) any column outside the table's `columns`, any generated column, or any column the copy masked a cell in, named anywhere in the statement → refused by name at plan; (2) a credential-shaped literal or bound value → refused; (3) a DELETE on a table with any column outside `columns` (withheld, credential-named or generated) → refused at plan [S5]; (4) a CHECK over the table when it has an unshared column, a trigger, an `ON CONFLICT REPLACE`, a foreign-key action → refused at plan AND at apply; (5) a credential-shaped value in a captured cell (judged under its column) → ROLLBACK `unsafe` — reachable only by a value in a GRANTED column that the read path already masks, so the answer tells the reader nothing it could not learn from a read. A strict-mode `drifted` or `unsafe` answers the reader the generic sentence and no count. The undo journal therefore never holds a credential; it holds the source's granted-column values (UPDATE/INSERT) and, for a DELETE, a whole row of a table every column of which is shared — at rest in the user's own file where the table already is.
- **D-PR3-13 The policy's three acts.** `{ kind: 'write'; grantId; table }`: rules 1–3 as `read`; rule 4′ attendance REQUIRED for every caller in PR-3 (a frame must be `present`; the chat always is; a schedule never → `ACCESS_UNATTENDED`, retryable); rule 5 as `read`; rule 7 the named table has `write` → else `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED` with `ACCESS_APP_MESSAGES.writeNotGranted` ("this access is read-only for that table"). `{ kind: 'refresh'; grantId }`: rules 1–5 as `read`, then `grant.refresh` → else `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED` (`refreshNotGranted`). `{ kind: 'discover' }`: the `ask` presence rule. `stillWritable(find, caller, grantId, table, now)` = `stillReadable` ∧ the table's `write`. The characterisation table gains the three acts over the same callers × grants, both drivers.
- **D-PR3-14 The service's three verbs.** `write(caller, { grantId, sql, params?, authority: 'permission' }) → { ok: true, op: 'write', changes }` (no `undoId` on the wire — an app can do nothing with it [DS16]): the frame's minute FIRST (writes count in the same per-app window as reads), the verdict, `brokeredWrite` (D-PR3-15); `refresh(caller, { grantId }) → { ok: true, op: 'refresh', fresh, reason? }` (no `summary` [S10/SC5/DS17]); `discover(caller, { hints? }) → { ok: true, op: 'discover', candidates }` (D-PR3-21). `undo` is a host-chrome act of the user (`undoBrokeredWrite`), like `revokeAccess`. The frame handler maps the three ops; the chat and scheduler doors reach none of them in PR-3. `read` and `recordRead` ring `access-read` (D-PR3-20).
- **D-PR3-15 The engine half (`access/writeBroker.ts`).** `brokeredWrite(db, { targetAppId, statement, authority: 'permission', via, grant, caller })` (strict): (1) `stillWritable` at the dequeue of the plan job (the `stillLive` thunk) [S8]; (2) **the Worker's `plan-write` job on a FRESH export** — never the bytes cache (`scopedPlanWrite` evicts the grant's cached bytes first; writes are rare and the preview must be of the handle it will apply to) [F5/S15], under the read's clock (a timeout is a strike through `noteTimeout` — three pause the grant `reader-misbehaved`; `unavailable` → `queryFailed(noWorker)`); (3) **`stillWritable` again, SYNCHRONOUSLY, after the plan answers and before the seat** — a grant stopped meanwhile answers `ACCESS_REVOKED`/`ACCESS_EXPIRED` as `read`'s `endedMeanwhile` and nothing is applied [S8]; (4) the reader's live-entry count on this target < 20 else `ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED` [S9]; (5) `db.driver.brokeredWrite(target, { plans: [plan], caps: { maxChanges: ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS, maxUndoBytes: ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES }, expectChanges: [plan.previewChanges], requireUndo: true, capture: true })`; (6) after the seat answers ok: `putAccessUndo(target, entry)` → the `wrote` line (`via`, `authority`, `changes`, `undoId`, `tables: [table]`, `sql`) appended at once — host-authored but for the walked `sql` seat (`loggedSql` omits it on a hit; a storage throw after the commit is the one residual, named) [S19] → `clearSourceReadCaches(db, target)` (every grant whose source is the target) → the doorbells (`data-changed { table }` to the target's live frame; `access-source-changed { grantId }` to every OTHER live reader of the target, `except: readerAppId`) → the answer. Refusals map: parse/column/literal/facts/`too-many`/`too-large`/`unsafe`/the live-entry cap → `ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED` (not retryable; a rule named in words that reveal no source fact [DS19]); `busy` and a strict `drifted` → `ACCESS_BUSY` (retryable, "the other app is in the middle of a change — try again"; no count) [S15]; `not-granted` → `ACCESS_NOT_GRANTED`; `drift` → the read's `sourceChanged` after `suspendAccess('source-changed')`; a SQL failure → `ACCESS_QUERY_FAILED`. Own mode: `applyApprovedWrite(db, proposal)` plans on the main thread (`nonDataStatementReason`, the dry run through `scratchRun` as today for `previewed`) → the seat with `requireUndo: false, capture: false, expectChanges: proposal.previewed` → `data-changed` → the outcome as today (`drifted` → `ExecuteApprovedWriteOutcome.drifted`). `undoBrokeredWrite(db, targetAppId, undoId, { via: 'user' })`: the live entry → **every step's table and columns re-validated against the live `table_xinfo` (the table exists, every named column exists, none generated), `tableFacts` on the live handle, the values are bound parameters** [S3] → the seat with `steps` in REVERSE order (`requireUndo: false, capture: false`; the built statements through `forbiddenStatementReason` as the belt) → `markAccessUndone` → the `undone` line (`changes`, `via: 'user'`, `tables`) → the cache clear → `data-changed` to the target and `access-source-changed` to EVERY live reader of the target (the reader whose write was reversed holds the stalest view — no `except` on an undo) [S19] → `{ ok: true }`; busy → "try again in a moment"; a guard mismatch → "couldn't undo — <Source> changed these rows since" (nothing changed: ROLLBACK); a schema mismatch → "this change can no longer be undone" and the entry marked undone-failed.
- **D-PR3-16 The chat door stays READ-ONLY in PR-3.** `data_propose_write` keeps PR-2's refusal of any alias table (`CHAT_DOOR.readOnly`), `CHAT_DOOR.heading` keeps "read-only", `PendingWriteProposal` gains nothing, `data-propose-write.md` is untouched; the chat's cross-app write through the card (the alias rename in the Worker plan, the one-app rule, the `target` seat on the persisted card, the service route on approve, the per-table "can be changed" mark in the DDL block, `CHAT_DOOR.changes`) lands with PR-4 beside the data-lane tool changes — ADR-0076's fold section says §2's routing sentence lands with §9. The chat's materialised set DOES include the kv table under a `kv` grant (`<alias>__snug_kv`, D-PR3-22 — one line in `toAttach`). [SC2 — one-word reversal: "rename"]
- **D-PR3-17 The refresh engine (`access/refresh.ts`) rides the exported dispatchers.** `refreshSource(db, deps: AppRunDeps, { grant, requestedBy, via }) → RefreshOutcome` — `deps` from the CALLER (the service's `refresh` loads `refresh.ts` and `appRun.ts`'s `defaultAppRunDeps` through ONE lazy edge, the executors' pattern, so no top-level cycle is added [F8]; tests pass `fakeDeps()`): (1) **the gates: `allows('schedule')`, an existing `schedulerState` row, `globalPause === false`** — else `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` (not retryable; `ACCESS_APP_MESSAGES.refreshOff` "schedules are off for this file") [S7]; (2) the cooldown map (`sourceAppId → lastDispatchAt`, in-memory, `ACCESS_REFRESH_COOLDOWN_MS`; within it → `{ fresh: false, reason: 'recent' }`, nothing run, no line); (3) the pre-dispatch ceiling check (`wouldExceedCeiling(countersSoFar(db, …), { ai: 1, net: 1 })` over the FILE's day — the one counter there is; capped → `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` reason `capped`, the `refreshed` line written); (4) stamp the cooldown AT DISPATCH; (5) `requestedBy = oneLine(readerName)` capped at `LIMITS.DISPLAY_NAME_CHARS` at the ONE place the input is built [S17]; (6) `placeRun(deps.live, source)` → `Dispatch { mode: 'unattended', posture: 'refresh', app, step: { kind: 'app-run', appId: source, input: { kind: 'refresh', requestedBy } }, title: REFRESH_RUN.title(requestedBy), runId: uuid, taskId: 'access-refresh', key, payload, deadline: now + SCHEDULE_RESULT_TIMEOUT_MS, spent: {0,0}, afterHandover: false, db, signal, now }` → `dispatchLive` (under `beginDelegatedRun({ …, posture: 'refresh' })`) or `dispatchHidden` (today's hidden path) with the PR-1 handover loop (`MAX_HANDOVERS`); (7) the attempt's calls folded into `schedulerState.daily` (`foldDailyCalls`, shared with the queue — never a no-op now that the row is required); (8) the `refreshed` line on the SOURCE's history BEFORE the answer (`reason` from the fixed set when the run failed; `refused` when the record holds refusals); (9) on a `result` end: `clearSourceReadCaches(db, source)` → `{ fresh: true }`; `no-handler` → `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` (`refreshNoHandler`); `beginDelegatedRun`'s refusal or a second hidden mount → `ACCESS_BUSY`; `timeout` → `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED`; a seat-less host → `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED`. **No run row**; the kv key is cleared in the engine's `finally` (a key stranded by a crash is ≤ 1 KiB of inert kv the SDK never reads without its hint). The handler's `summary` goes nowhere. **The three *fetch* affordances — the chat's, the scheduler's and the ⋈ sheet's *fetch now* — wait for PR-4**; PR-3 ships the engine and the frame op [SC7/DS14 — one-word reversal: "fetch"]. KB 85 gains "A fetch another app asked for": the ONE signal is `input.kind === 'refresh'`; `taskId` is `'access-refresh'` and names no schedule; `input.requestedBy` is the asking app's display name; fetch and answer as for a schedule [DS20].
- **D-PR3-18 The dispatchers are EXPORTED IN PLACE.** `appRun.ts` exports `Dispatch`, `DispatchEnd`, `Attempt`, `dispatchLive`, `dispatchHidden` and `defaultAppRunDeps` (no new file — no importer changes a line [SC8/F12]); `Dispatch` carries `title`, `db`, `signal`, `now`, `taskId`, `runId` and `posture: 'schedule' | 'refresh'` directly instead of the full `StepContext` (the executor builds it from its `ctx`; `dispatchLive` read only `ctx.task.title`, `ctx.run.*`, `ctx.signal`, `ctx.db`); `countersSoFar(db, spent, soFar)` and a new `foldDailyCalls(db, calls)` move to `protection.ts` (the queue calls the latter). `appRunHandshake.test.tsx` is untouched.
- **D-PR3-19 A refresh on the LIVE frame refuses, never asks — and the sticky posture applies as for any delegated run.** `DelegatedRun.posture: 'schedule' | 'refresh'` (`beginDelegatedRun`'s input gains it; default `'schedule'`); `createRunScopedGate` branch (1) gains: `run.posture === 'refresh'` → push `{ host, method, why: 'refused' }`, return `false`, park nothing (`ScheduledRefusal.why` gains `'refused'`; the hidden path's refusing gate is unchanged); **`beginDelegatedRun` records the sticky generation for BOTH postures (the `touched` write stays unconditional)** — a refresh runs the source's APP CODE under a reader's act, and a handler that answers `schedule-result` and POSTs afterwards must meet the same dialog D-PR1-3 built; the cost (after a refresh, the source's *remember for this session* is unavailable on that frame until the user reopens it) is the cost PR-1 accepted, named in the delta, reversible by one word [S6]. The access door closes for the window (D-PR1-8), the open-url seat too (F-1). `RunningChip` renders for a `delegatedRunStore` entry whose `posture === 'refresh'` when the scheduler has nothing running — label `RUNNING_CHIP.fetching(sourceName)` ("fetching new data in Ledger"), title `REFRESH_RUN.title(requestedBy)` ("Budget asked"), accessible name "Ledger is fetching new data — Budget asked", NO act in PR-3 (the run is bounded by 90 s; one-word reversal: "cancel") [DS8/F13]; `AuthRepairChip` gains `via: 'refresh'` → `AUTH_REPAIR_VIA_REFRESH` "a fetch another app asked for ran into this" [DS20]. The hidden path (source closed) shows no chip (never registered).
- **D-PR3-20 Doorbells, three events, ids only (`access/doorbell.ts`).** (a) **The write signal is the driver's**: `CreateDbDriverOptions.onNamespaceWrite?: (e: NamespaceWriteEvent) => void`, `NamespaceWriteEvent = { namespace; kind: 'exec' | 'kv' | 'import' | 'brokered'; table?; changes? }`, raised **in `handle()`** — `case 'exec'` after `runExec` answered ok when `isRowModifyingStatement(sql) && getRowsModified() > 0` (the table from `dmlTargetTable(sql)`: comment-stripped, the first identifier after `INSERT [OR …] INTO` · `REPLACE INTO` · `UPDATE [OR …]` · `DELETE FROM`, unquoted; `undefined` when it cannot tell), `case 'kvSet'` after `runKvSet` answered ok (`kind: 'kv'`), `case 'import'` (`kind: 'import'`), and from `brokeredWrite` (`kind: 'brokered'`); **never inside `runKvSet`/`runHostKvSet` themselves, never from the host's `kvSet`/`kvGet` seat** (the `snug:schedule:<runId>` handshake rings nothing) [F9/S14/SC9], never from a read, never from `markDirty`. `subscribeWrites(listener): () => void` lives on `SnugDbDriver` (`createDbDriver` keeps its own listener set); the userdb FACADE keeps ITS set and re-subscribes it to each new inner in `importUserDb` [F15]. (b) `armDoorbell()` once per open file (the userdb open seam; armed on use): a signal on namespace N of kind `exec`/`kv`/`import` → coalesce per N (`ACCESS_DOORBELL_COALESCE_MS` 2 s: the first rings at once, later ones inside the window ring ONCE more at its end) → `grantsForApp(db, N).readBy.filter(live)` → for each reader with a live frame, `notifyAppHost(reader, ACCESS_SOURCE_CHANGED_EVENT, { grantId })`; a `kv` signal rings only grants with `kv`; a `brokered` signal is the broker's to ring (c). (c) The BROKER rings after a commit: `data-changed { table }` to the TARGET's live frame; `access-source-changed { grantId }` to every other live reader of the target `except` the writing reader; after an UNDO: `data-changed` to the target and `access-source-changed` to EVERY live reader [S19]. (d) The SERVICE rings `access-read { grantId }` to the SOURCE's live frame after `logRead` (a frame read) and after each recorded grant in `recordRead` — uncoalesced. No event carries a row, a value, a name or a count beyond the one id or table (AC24's negative). DDL on a source rings nothing (the next read's drift check pauses the grant — named in the delta).
- **D-PR3-21 `discover` is a class, cached per reader.** `discover { hints? }` → the `ask` presence verdict → a per-reader cache of the class for `ACCESS_DISCOVER_CACHE_MS` (60 s; in-memory, dropped by `resetAccessSession`) — a hit answers the cached class and exports nothing; a miss runs `collectSources(db, appId, hints)` (the same gather `request` uses — `describeAppData` per app) → `candidates = 'none' | 'one' | 'several'` over `matched.length + rest.length`; nothing recorded, nothing shown, `request`'s window untouched (discover → *use my ledger* → `request` is never refused). The library export load is bounded to one gather per reader per minute, below `request`'s own gap [S16, read with AC23's intent].
- **D-PR3-22 The copy: `withheld`, `kv` through the ONE walk, `sqlite_sequence`, the masked set.** `ScopedReadScope` gains `tables[].withheld?` and `kv?`. In `scopeCopy`: `withholdCredentialColumns` ALSO overwrites each table's `withheld` columns with the same fallbacks (fail closed); `driftOf` compares the live non-credential set against `columns ∪ withheld` (a withheld column that vanished or a new column still drifts); `dropOutsideScope` keeps `snug_kv` when `kv` and then DELETES, row by row from JS, every row whose key is credential-named (`isCredentialKeyName`), whose key starts `snug:`, **whose value does not parse as JSON, or whose PARSED value `findRecordCredential({ [key]: parsed })` refuses** (the ONE walk: auth-like keys at any depth, URL userinfo, the shapes whole and token by token, the entropy rule under every nested credential-ish key — the start-anchored `scanForCredentialValues` over the stored JSON text would miss a quoted `"sk-…"`) [S1]; `strayObject` admits `snug_kv` when `kv`; the drift check never looks at `snug_kv`; the `sqlite_sequence` belt: `DELETE FROM sqlite_sequence WHERE lower(name) NOT IN (<granted>)` when the table exists (R-76). **`scopeCopy` answers the set of `(table, column)` it masked at least one cell in** (the planner's third oracle rule); `scopeCopy(scratch, scope, { forPlan? })` skips `query_only` and resets `ignore_check_constraints` for a plan copy. The dump includes `{ name: 'snug_kv', columns: ['key', 'value'], types: ['TEXT', 'TEXT'] }` LAST when `kv`; `accessTableNameSchema` still refuses `snug_kv` in `tables`. The read path's cell mask stays start-anchored (pre-existing; the copy's withhold and mask are the rule) — named in the delta.
- **D-PR3-23 The consent sheet v2 — chips by default, columns behind a disclosure, options open when they matter.** `ticked: Record<appId, Record<table, { columns: string[] (ticked, pre-filled with every non-sensitive column); write: boolean }>>`; **the chips stay on the default path**; each table row gains one disclosure `access-columns-<app>-<t>` (`CONSENT_SHEET.chooseColumns` "choose columns…") that swaps the chips for pre-ticked per-column checkboxes (`access-column-<app>-<t>-<c>`; a sensitive column a DISABLED unticked box reading `never shared`; the 8-cut as *show all N columns* inside) [DS5]; a table whose every column is unticked is un-allowable (`CONSENT_SHEET.pickAColumn`). The write toggle `access-write-<app>-<t>` (`CONSENT_SHEET.canChange`, OFF) sits on the table row; the `SourceOptions` row under the chosen source holds `access-kv` (`CONSENT_SHEET.settingsAndNotes(sourceName)` "Ledger's settings & notes", hint "what Ledger keeps outside its tables — never its connections or keys") and `access-refresh` (`CONSENT_SHEET.fetchFirst(sourceName)` "may ask Ledger to fetch new data before reading it", hint "through Ledger's own connections, at most once every five minutes, counted on your daily schedule limits") [DS23/S7]; both the toggles and the options row live under ONE *more options…* disclosure (`access-options-more`) that **starts OPEN whenever `pending.wantsWrite` or any seeded `write`/`kv`/`refresh` is true** (a renewal of a write access shows its toggle ticked without a click) [DS6]. Under any ticked write toggle, one breath: `CONSENT_SHEET.undoNote(sourceName, table)` "while you're here, Budget can change up to 200 rows at a time in Ledger's transactions; every change is listed in Ledger's access and can be undone for seven days" [DS3/DS4/DS18]. **No egress line for write or refresh** (the egress block lists places data can go; its title stays true); `EGRESS.closing(sourceName, canChange)` ends "keeps a history of its reads and changes" when any write is ticked [DS4]. `STRIP.title`/`CONSENT_SHEET.title(readerName, wantsWrite)` → "Budget wants to read — and change — another app's data"; `STRIP_OUTCOME.allowed(readerName, sourceName, tables, duration, write)` → "Budget can now read and change Ledger's transactions · while it's open" (every table) / "read Ledger's transactions and accounts, and change transactions"; `CONSENT_UI.stopped` says "no longer reads or changes" when the access carried write; the chat card's outcome follows (`resolution.allowed.write`) — `AccessStrip` reads one more seat [DS2]. `ConsentDecision.allow { sourceAppId, tables: { name, withheld: string[], write: boolean }[], duration, unattended, kv, refresh }`; `CreateGrantInput.tables` likewise; `ConsentOutcome.allowed.write: string[]`; `SheetSeed` gains `tables?`, `kv?`, `refresh?`; `renew` and `preselect` carry the old grant's whole scope. `createGrantFromDecision` writes v2 with defaults OMITTED (`columns` = ticked minus sensitive, `withheld` = the rest minus sensitive when non-empty; `write`/`kv`/`refresh` only when true; `access` derived; `provenance: 'chat'` when the pending's `askedIn === 'chat'` — R-86 closes); the `granted` line as today. Every new sentence outside `EGRESS.*`/`CHAT_DOOR.*` uses the STRAIGHT apostrophe [DS22].
- **D-PR3-24 History, rows, Settings.** `AccessHistory` renders the who as "you" when `entry.via === 'user'` (the reader's name moves INTO the sentence) [DS7]; `historyLine(entry, now, readerName)`: `wrote` → "changed 3 rows in transactions · 2 min ago" (+ `via` `chat` → "from its chat", `schedule` → "on a schedule"); `undone` → "undid Budget's change to transactions · 3 rows put back · 2 min ago"; `refreshed` → "asked Ledger to fetch new data · 2 min ago" (+ the reason's words: `no-handler` "Ledger can't fetch on request" · `timeout` "took too long" · `capped` "Ledger is at the daily limit" · `refused` "a change Ledger tried to send was refused" · `failed` "Ledger's fetch failed"); the `wrote` row keeps the statement behind the existing disclosure titled `ACCESS_SHEET.whatItChanged` "what it changed". `AccessHistory` gains `undoable: ReadonlySet<string>` and `onUndo?(undoId)`: a `wrote` row whose `undoId` is live renders *undo* (`access-history-undo`) behind the inline armed confirm (`access-undo-confirm` with *yes, undo* / *keep*, the `clearArmed` pattern) whose question is `ACCESS_SHEET.undoArm(table, n)` "put 3 rows in transactions back as they were before this change? (if Ledger changed them since, nothing happens)" [DS12/S18] and the one outcome line (`access-history-undo-outcome`: `ACCESS_SHEET.undone` · `.undoFailed(reason)`). `AccessSheet`'s `SheetData` gains `undoable` (from `listAccessUndo`) and the act; `showHistory` unchanged. `GrantRow`: a write badge `access-row-write` (`ACCESS_SHEET.canChange` "can also change rows"; past tense on an ended row `.couldChange`) beside `access-row-words` — **no second act** (MUST 17 stands; *fetch now* waits for PR-4) [DS14]. Read sentences amended: `ACCESS_SHEET.iconTitle` "what this app can read or change, and who can read or change it"; `SETTINGS_CARD.intro` "which of your apps can read — or change, when you allowed it — another app's data; the app that was read keeps a history of its reads and changes"; `SETTINGS_CARD.clearHistoryHint` "when access was allowed, stopped or paused — and every change — stays on record"; `ACCESS_SHEET.noHistory` "nothing on record yet" [DS21]. `ACCESS_APP_MESSAGES` additions (every one reveals no source fact and invites no re-ask [DS19]): `writeNotGranted` "this access is read-only for that table"; `writeRefused(rule)` "the change was refused — <rule>" with rules worded "that table can't be changed from another app" · "names no rows — a change to every row is made in the other app itself" · "a column it names isn't shared" · "a computed column can't be used in a change" · "a value in it can't be matched" · "too many rows — at most 200 at a time" · "too large to undo" · "too many changes are waiting to be undone — undo them or wait"; `busy` "the other app is in the middle of a change — try again"; `refreshNotGranted` "this access does not allow asking for new data"; `refreshNoHandler` "the other app does not fetch on request"; `refreshFailed(reason)`; `refreshOff` "schedules are off for this file".
- **D-PR3-25 Vocabulary.** Every new module under `access/` (`writeBroker.ts`, `refresh.ts`, `doorbell.ts`) carries no banned word in ANY string or type literal — authorities are `'permission' | 'approval' | 'task'`, refusal reasons `refused`, `not-granted`, `too-many`, `too-large`, `unsafe`, `busy`, `drifted`, `failed`, `copy-failed`, `drift`; every sentence a person reads lives in `access/copy.ts` and is pinned by `accessCopy.test.ts`; the Worker job's kind is `'plan-write'`.
- **D-PR3-26 Limits move to the protocol.** `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_ROWS` · `_BYTES` · `_SET_BYTES` · `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_TIMEOUT_MS` move from `access/limits.ts` to `packages/protocol/src/access.ts` (Appendix B); `limits.ts` is deleted; `scopedRead.ts` and `materialise.ts` import the protocol.
- **D-PR3-27 No persisted shape changes beyond the three the wire names** (the grant record, the log entry, the undo entry). The scheduled task, the run row, `schedulerState`, the chat row's `meta.*` keys and `PendingWriteProposal` keep their schemas.
- **D-PR3-28 The kit ceiling conversation is open now.** The estimate is +35–47 KB against 7,634 B of headroom (F11); PR-2's probe already cut the dead lanes, so a second probe cannot find 40 KB. The three measurements (before I-W; after I-W + I-D; after I-E + I-U) are EVIDENCE for the owner's decision, not the trigger: the PR body carries the paragraph — the byte count, the optional bytes (the refresh engine and `discover` — each a one-word cut), and the one-line decision `scripts/check-host-kit.mjs:30` `KIT_SIZE_CEILING_BYTES`; CI's `check-host-kit` is red on the branch until the owner decides; the PR opens either way (the PR-2 precedent). The tokenizer + planner + facts live ONLY in the Worker bundle; the main bundle carries `applyBrokeredBatch`, the undo builder, `tableFacts` and `dmlTargetTable`.
- **D-PR3-29 The dist seam** (D-PR2-20 restated): after every `packages/protocol` edit `pnpm --filter @snugprotocol/protocol build` (and `gen:schemas` after a frame/record edit); after every `packages/db` edit `pnpm --filter @snugprotocol/db build`; after every `packages/sdk` edit its build; after any prompt or KB edit `pnpm --filter @snugprotocol/knowledge gen:content && … build`; the playground's `test` script is red at tsc until every module lands, so lanes report single suites with `pnpm --filter playground exec vitest run <file>`.

### The spec's new normative text (I-S writes exactly this; the whitepaper and the KB paraphrase it) [DS15]

- **§22.4 The undo journal (`accessUndo:<targetAppId>`).** A hub that brokers a write (§24.5) MUST keep, per target app, a journal of the rows each brokered write changed — for an INSERT the new rowids and the inserted values; for an UPDATE the rowid, the previous and the new values of the columns the statement set; for a DELETE the whole previous row — as data, never as statement text: an undo is built from the journal at the time of the undo. ≤ 20 live entries per (target, reader), ≤ 64 KiB per entry, ≤ 256 KiB per row, kept seven days; never a credential (a write whose captured values would carry one is refused); an undone entry is kept, marked, until it expires; swept with the app; DROPPED on an untrusted import, kept on a trusted pull. Rule: a strict parser of 1.1 cannot read this row and MUST ignore it.
- **§24.5 Writes — a host MUST:** (18) broker a write only under a grant whose named table carries `write: true` and only for a PRESENT caller (an app's frame: the visible frame of its generation; the chat beside the app: always; a scheduled step: never at 1.2 → `ACCESS_UNATTENDED`), counting it in the reader's per-minute window first; (19) accept exactly ONE plain `INSERT INTO t (cols) VALUES …`, `UPDATE t SET … WHERE …` or `DELETE FROM t WHERE …` whose values are literals or bound parameters and whose WHERE is a conjunction/disjunction of simple comparisons on the granted columns — a withheld, credential-named, generated or masked column named anywhere, a subquery, a CTE, `RETURNING`, a conflict clause, a schema qualifier, a function, a missing WHERE, or a credential-shaped value → `ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED`, never `MALFORMED`; (20) refuse a write on a table that is virtual, WITHOUT ROWID, carries a trigger (temporary ones included), an `ON CONFLICT REPLACE`, a foreign-key action, or a CHECK over an unshared column; refuse a DELETE unless every column of the table is granted; (21) dry-run the statement in the bounded context on a scoped copy and refuse past `ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS` (200) or past `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES`; then apply it atomically on the live database — `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, the capture, the statement, the journal entry, `COMMIT` — with no other statement interleaved; answer `ACCESS_BUSY` (retryable) when the target holds an open transaction or changed between the dry run and the apply; no row leaves the context, and the reader learns only `changes`; (22) write the `wrote` line on the target's history (`via`, `authority`, `changes`, the table, the statement as the history keeps it) with the journal entry, before the answer; (23) offer *undo* from the target's history for a live entry: rebuild the inverse from the journal, apply it in reverse step order atomically, change nothing unless every row still holds the values the write left (else fail whole), write `undone`, and never offer to undo an undo; (24) after a commit or an undo, hint the target's live frame `data-changed { table }` and every other live reader `access-source-changed { grantId }`.
- **§24.6 Refresh — a host MUST:** (25) run the SOURCE where it lives (one instance, §24.7) with `input { kind: 'refresh', requestedBy }` over the schedule handshake only under a grant with `refresh: true`, under the unattended posture (a mutating connected call is refused, never asked; nothing is remembered), counted on the file's daily ceilings and refused before dispatch when they would be crossed or when schedules are off or paused; (26) run a source at most once per `ACCESS_REFRESH_COOLDOWN_MS` (five minutes) — within it answer `fresh: false, reason: 'recent'` and run nothing; (27) write `refreshed` on the source's history before the answer, with who asked and, on failure, a reason from the fixed set; a source without a handler → `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` (not retryable); the handler's own text never reaches the reader.
- **§24.7 One instance, doorbells, capability truth — a host MUST:** (28) never run a second copy of the source beside an open one for a refresh — the placement rule of a scheduled run applies, and the frame that hosted a refresh stays ask-only afterwards exactly as one that hosted a scheduled run; (29) after a successful row-modifying statement on a source, hint every live reader `access-source-changed { grantId }`, coalesced ≥ `ACCESS_DOORBELL_COALESCE_MS`; after a read, hint the source's live frame `access-read { grantId }`; ids only — never a row, a value, a name or a count; (30) answer an op it does not serve `MALFORMED` with the `requestId` recovered — how a 1.2 app learns it is on a 1.1 host.
- **§25 loses** write access, user-chosen columns, `snug_kv`, the doorbell, the count-only discover, the hint to the source, the chat/scheduler read; **keeps** the builder-time declaration (§11), grants inside shared bundles, sidecar sources, the source-initiated grant, two-device appends, the imported-file marker; **adds** the task write authority's arming surface (§9), the chat's cross-app write and the three *fetch* affordances (§2/§6, landing with §9), write on a source-initiated grant, a per-op grid, step chaining. The changelog entry carries Q19 and the `MALFORMED` fallback.

### Module contracts (the wire)

**`packages/protocol/src/access.ts`** (+)
```ts
export const ACCESS_OPS = ['request', 'query', 'write', 'refresh', 'list', 'release', 'discover'] as const;   // frame variants appended in THIS order
export const ACCESS_PROVENANCES = ['app', 'user', 'chat'] as const;
export const ACCESS_LOG_KINDS = ['granted', 'read', 'refused', 'revoked', 'expired', 'released', 'suspended', 'wrote', 'undone', 'refreshed'] as const;
export const ACCESS_LOG_VIAS = ['app', 'chat', 'schedule', 'user'] as const;
export const ACCESS_WRITE_AUTHORITIES = ['permission', 'approval', 'task'] as const;
export const ACCESS_REFRESH_REASONS = ['no-handler', 'timeout', 'capped', 'refused', 'failed'] as const;
export const ACCESS_CANDIDATE_CLASSES = ['none', 'one', 'several'] as const;
export const ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS = 200; export const ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES = 64 * 1024; export const ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_ROW_BYTES = 256 * 1024; export const ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_ENTRIES = 20; export const ACCESS_UNDO_TTL_MS = 7 * 86_400_000;
export const ACCESS_REFRESH_COOLDOWN_MS = 5 * 60_000; export const ACCESS_DISCOVER_CACHE_MS = 60_000; export const ACCESS_DOORBELL_COALESCE_MS = 2_000;
export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_ROWS = 5_000; export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024; export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_MAX_SET_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024; export const ACCESS_MATERIALISE_TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000;
export const ACCESS_WANTS_MAX_BYTES = 2 * 1024; export const ACCESS_WANTS_MAX_ENTRIES = 4;
export const ACCESS_SOURCE_CHANGED_EVENT = 'access-source-changed'; export const ACCESS_READ_EVENT = 'access-read'; export const DATA_CHANGED_EVENT = 'data-changed';
export const ACCESS_ERROR_CODES = { …twelve…, ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED, ACCESS_BUSY, ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED } as const;   // fifteen
// VIEW: tables[].write?: z.literal(true).optional(); kv?: z.literal(true).optional(); refresh?: z.literal(true).optional(); access: string (1..32)
// RECORD: scopeTableSchema + withheld?: array(column).max(64).refine(no duplicates, none credential-named, disjoint from columns); write?: z.literal(true); accessGrantSchema + kv?: z.literal(true), refresh?: z.literal(true), access: z.enum(['read', 'write']) with superRefine "access === 'write' ⇔ some table write"; provenance over ACCESS_PROVENANCES
// LOG ENTRY: kind over ACCESS_LOG_KINDS; via?; authority?; changes?: z.int().min(0); undoId?: uuid; reason on refreshed ∈ ACCESS_REFRESH_REASONS (the display-line rule stays for the other kinds); superRefine: authority/undoId only on wrote, changes on wrote|undone
export const accessUndoRowSchema = z.strictObject({ rowid: z.int(), before: z.array(accessParamSchema).optional(), after: z.array(accessParamSchema).optional() });
export const accessUndoStepSchema = z.strictObject({ table: accessTableNameSchema, op: z.enum(['insert', 'update', 'delete']), columns: z.array(column).min(1).max(ACCESS_MAX_COLUMNS), rows: z.array(accessUndoRowSchema).max(ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS) });   // before/after length === columns length; op ⇔ which seats are present
export const accessUndoEntrySchema = z.strictObject({ id: uuid, at, expiresAt, authority, via, grantId: uuid, readerAppId, readerName, steps: z.array(accessUndoStepSchema).min(1).max(64), undoneAt?: isoInstant }).superRefine(bytes ≤ ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES; every cell walked under its column → refusal);
export const accessWantsSchema = …(ADR §11's shape, D-PR3-7);
export function canonicalAccessGrantIntent(grant): string;   // + withheld, write, kv, refresh with absent ≡ default
export interface AccessRequestSemantics { hints?; renew?; access?: 'read' | 'write' }
export function parseAccessUndoEntry(raw); export function parseAccessWants(raw);
```
**`packages/protocol/src/frames.ts`**: request variants appended in `ACCESS_OPS` order — `request` gains `access: z.enum(['read', 'write']).optional()`; `{ op: 'write', grantId, sql: accessSqlSchema, params?: accessParamsSchema }` · `{ op: 'refresh', grantId }` · `{ op: 'discover', hints?: accessHintsSchema }` (strict); response variants (tolerant) `{ ok: true, op: 'write', changes: z.int().min(0) }` · `{ ok: true, op: 'refresh', fresh: z.boolean(), reason: z.string().max(32).optional() }` · `{ ok: true, op: 'discover', candidates: z.enum(ACCESS_CANDIDATE_CLASSES) }`. No host-ready change. `json-schemas.ts` comments say seven/eight; `constants.ts:33-41` wording; `index.ts` exports. Tests: the "unknown op" example becomes `op: 'forget'`; twelve → fifteen; the nine intent fields → thirteen with absent ≡ default pinned in both directions (1.1 bytes ↔ v2 defaults-off) and a "DOES move" row per new seat; the undo entry (caps, the per-cell walk, steps 1..64, before/after arity); the wants; a 1.1-shaped `request`/`query`/`list`/`release` still parses; the response union stays tolerant; `review-regressions` unchanged and green.

**`packages/runner`**: `host.ts` `accessSeat.ok` + three enumerated cases (`write` → `changes`; `refresh` → `fresh`, `…(reason)`; `discover` → `candidates`); the comments. `host-access.test.ts`: `OP_BODIES`/`OK_RESULTS` + three; the absent-stays-absent twin for `refresh`; **the unknown-op fallback pinned**; value-blind over seven ops; the size belt over a `discover`.

**`packages/sdk`**: `types.ts` `SnugAccess` + `write(grantId, sql, params?)`, `refresh(grantId)`, `discover(hints?)`, `onSourceChanged(listener: ({ grantId }) => void)`, `onDataChanged(listener: ({ table }) => void)`, `onRead(listener: ({ grantId }) => void)` (the written-to app repaints; the source learns it was read) [DS10]; result types `AccessWriteResult = { ok: true; changes: number } | AccessFailure`, `AccessRefreshResult = { ok: true; fresh: boolean; reason?: string } | AccessFailure`, `AccessDiscoverResult = { ok: true; candidates: 'none' | 'one' | 'several' } | AccessFailure`; `request(purpose, { hints?, renew?, access? })`; `access.ts` the three settlers + `mismatch` twins; the three listeners over `onHostEvent`; `index.ts` exports the three event names. Tests: eleven keys/methods; the settlers; a `write` answered to a `query` → mismatch; the three listeners receive exactly the id/table; the embedded block pin stays green.

**`packages/db`**
```ts
// scoped-read.ts (+)
export interface ScopedReadScope { tables: ReadonlyArray<{ name; columns; withheld?: readonly string[]; write?: boolean }>; kv?: boolean }
export interface ScopeCopyReport { masked: ReadonlySet<string> }   // `${table}.${column}` lower-cased
// scopeCopy(scratch, scope, { forPlan?: boolean }) → ScopingFailure | ScopeCopyReport; withheld overwritten like credential columns; driftOf over columns ∪ withheld; snug_kv kept + filtered through findRecordCredential over the PARSED value when kv; the sqlite_sequence belt; strayObject admits snug_kv when kv; forPlan skips query_only and resets ignore_check_constraints
// scopedScratchDump: + the snug_kv table LAST when kv
// brokered-write.ts (new, pure; no DOM/Worker/userdb import)
export type BrokeredOp = 'insert' | 'update' | 'delete';
export interface ParsedDml { op: BrokeredOp; table: string; columns: string[]; setColumns?: string[]; insertColumns?: string[]; literals: string[]; placeholders: number; sql: string }
export function parseBrokeredStatement(sql: string): { ok: true; parsed: ParsedDml } | { ok: false; reason: 'refused'; message: string };
export interface ColumnFact { name: string; generated: boolean }
export function tableFacts(db: Database, table: string, scope: { columns: readonly string[] }, op: BrokeredOp): { ok: true; columns: ColumnFact[] } | { ok: false; reason: 'refused'; message: string };
export interface BrokeredCaps { maxChanges: number; maxUndoBytes: number }
export interface BrokeredPlan { sql: string; params?: readonly unknown[]; op: BrokeredOp; table: string; columns: string[]; setColumns?: string[]; insertColumns?: string[]; previewChanges: number }
export type PlanRefusal = { ok: false; reason: 'refused' | 'not-granted' | 'too-many' | 'too-large' | 'unsafe' | 'failed' | 'copy-failed'; message: string } | { ok: false; reason: 'drift'; drift: ScopedReadDrift; message: string };   // 'unsafe' at the plan too (Gate 4 D-ID-8: the cell rule and the per-cell walk run early on the copy's masked values — the early answer; the apply's walk on the live values is the rule)
export function planBrokeredWrite(SQL, bytes, scope: ScopedReadScope, statement: ScopedReadStatement, caps: BrokeredCaps): { ok: true; plan: BrokeredPlan } | PlanRefusal;
export interface UndoRow { rowid: number; before?: unknown[]; after?: unknown[] }
export interface UndoStep { table: string; op: BrokeredOp; columns: string[]; rows: UndoRow[] }
export type ApplyRefusal = { ok: false; reason: 'busy' | 'drifted' | 'too-many' | 'too-large' | 'unsafe' | 'refused' | 'failed'; message: string; previewed?: number[]; current?: number[] };
export function applyBrokeredBatch(db: Database, input: { plans: readonly BrokeredPlan[]; caps: BrokeredCaps; expectChanges?: readonly number[]; requireUndo: boolean; capture: boolean } | { undo: readonly UndoStep[]; caps: BrokeredCaps }): { ok: true; steps: UndoStep[]; changes: number[]; undoable: boolean } | ApplyRefusal;   // SYNCHRONOUS; tableFacts on the live handle first; BEGIN IMMEDIATE … COMMIT; committed flag + ROLLBACK in finally; per-apply temp names
export function buildUndoStatement(step: UndoStep, row: UndoRow): { sql: string; params: unknown[] };   // identifiers quoted; the guard on `after`
export function dmlTargetTable(sql: string): string | undefined;
// driver.ts (+)
export interface NamespaceWriteEvent { namespace: string; kind: 'exec' | 'kv' | 'import' | 'brokered'; table?: string; changes?: number }
export interface CreateDbDriverOptions { …; onNamespaceWrite?: (event: NamespaceWriteEvent) => void }
export interface SnugDbDriver { …; brokeredWrite(namespace: string, input: BrokeredWriteInput): Promise<BrokeredWriteResult>; subscribeWrites(listener: (event: NamespaceWriteEvent) => void): () => void }
export type BrokeredWriteResult = { ok: true; steps: UndoStep[]; changes: number[]; undoable: boolean } | ({ ok: false; retryable: boolean } & ApplyRefusal) | { ok: false; code: string; message: string; retryable: boolean };
// the per-namespace inTransaction flag; busy answered from it before the handle is touched
// userdb.ts (+): the facade forwards brokeredWrite (tombstone, noteNamespace) and keeps its subscribeWrites set across importUserDb's inner swap; accessors: listAccessUndo(targetAppId, readerAppId?): AccessUndoEntry[] (live) · listAllAccessUndo(targetAppId) · putAccessUndo(targetAppId, entry): 'ok' | 'reader-cap' · markAccessUndone(targetAppId, id, at, failed?: boolean)
// app-settings-keys.ts (+): ACCESS_UNDO_SETTING_PREFIX 'accessUndo:' + the pair; ACCESS_WANTS_SETTING_PREFIX 'accessWants:' + the pair; sweepAccessForDeletedApp + both; reconcileImportedAccessGrants: untrusted drops both prefixes
```
Tests: `brokered-write.test.ts` (NEW): every parse refusal by name (a function, `CASE`, arithmetic, a qualifier, a subquery, `RETURNING`, `ON CONFLICT`, `OR REPLACE`, no WHERE, no column list, two statements); the column rule (withheld, credential-named, generated, masked — each refused BEFORE any dry run); the table facts at BOTH seats (a trigger, a TEMP trigger, virtual, WITHOUT ROWID, `ON CONFLICT REPLACE`, a CHECK with an unshared column, `foreign_keys=ON` + CASCADE refused, `foreign_keys=OFF` admitted; a DELETE on a table with one withheld column refused while an UPDATE on it succeeds); a credential-shaped literal/param/captured cell refused; the dry run's counts on a `forPlan` copy (a read copy refuses a write); the apply on a real handle: busy from the flag and from a nested BEGIN, `expectChanges` drift, the exact captured rows per op incl. an explicit PK insert, the undo built from steps and guarded by `after` (a row changed since → the whole undo rolls back; a reused rowid fails whole), the per-apply temp names gone after every path, an injected throw mid-apply leaves no open transaction and no temp object, an undo never captures; `dmlTargetTable`. `scoped-read.test.ts` (+ withheld NULLed/drift/`SELECT *` shows `***`; kv kept and `kvSet('openai', 'sk-…')`, `kvSet('config', { token: 'eyJ…' })`, `kvSet('note', 'see Bearer …')`, a non-JSON value, a `snug:` key — none crosses; the `sqlite_sequence` belt; the masked set reported; the dump's `snug_kv` LAST). `driver.test.ts` (+ the write signal raised for a frame's exec with changes > 0, a frame's kvSet, an import, a brokered write — and NOT for a read, the host's `kvSet(ns, 'snug:schedule:x')` or its clear; the `inTransaction` flag; `brokeredWrite` through the facade incl. the tombstone; `subscribeWrites` survives an import swap). `access.test.ts` (db; the undo accessors, the per-reader cap answer, the row cap, TTL, `markAccessUndone`, the sweep, the import drop vs trusted keep, the `wrote` protection tier, `clearAccessLog` keeps the acts).

### Module contracts (the engine)

**`access/policy.ts`** (+): `AccessAct` + `{ kind: 'write'; grantId; table }` · `{ kind: 'refresh'; grantId }` · `{ kind: 'discover' }`; `authorise` per D-PR3-13; `stillWritable`. **`access/service.ts`** (+): `write`, `refresh` (the lazy edge to `refresh.ts` + `defaultAppRunDeps`), `discover` (the per-reader class cache; `resetAccessSession` drops it); `read`/`recordRead` ring `access-read`; the facade + overrides gain the three. **`access/accessHandler.ts`**: the three cases; `request` threads `frame.access` as `wantsWrite`. **`access/writeBroker.ts`** (new) per D-PR3-15 (`brokeredWrite`, `applyApprovedWrite`, `undoBrokeredWrite`, `clearSourceReadCaches`). **`access/refresh.ts`** (new) per D-PR3-17 (`refreshSource`, `refreshInFlightStore`? — no: the chip reads `delegatedRunStore`; `__resetRefreshForTests`). **`access/doorbell.ts`** (new) per D-PR3-20. **`access/scopedRead.ts`** (+): `ScopedPlanWriteJob { id; kind: 'plan-write'; bytes; scope; statement; caps: BrokeredCaps }`, `scopedPlanWrite(input: { grantId; bytes; scope; statement; caps; stillLive? }): Promise<PlanOutcome>` (the same queue; **evicts the grant's cached bytes first and never caches the plan**; the READ's clock); the worker: `isPlanWriteShaped` guarded FIRST beside the dump's, answering `failed` for its id on a malformed job. **`access/materialise.ts`**: `toAttach` includes the kv table. **`agent/dataTools.ts`**: `executeApprovedWrite` thin → `applyApprovedWrite`; nothing else. **`schedule/appRun.ts`** per D-PR3-18; **`schedule/protection.ts`** + `countersSoFar`, `foldDailyCalls`; **`schedule/runPlacement.ts`** + `posture`; **`schedule/runScopedGate.ts`** + the refusing branch; **`schedule/copy.ts`** + `REFRESH_RUN.title(requestedBy)`, `RUNNING_CHIP.fetching(appName)`, `AUTH_REPAIR_VIA_REFRESH`, `ScheduledRefusal.why` + `'refused'`; **`schedule/RunningChip.tsx`** the refresh entry; **`run/AuthRepairChip.tsx`** `via: 'refresh'`; **`state/userdb.ts`** `armDoorbell()` after open and after every swap.

### Residual risks the delta will name
- A standing write permission is a power exercised while the user looks elsewhere (never while nobody is present in PR-3): one statement, one table, literal values, 200 rows, the column rule, the journal, the line, the doorbell, instant revocation (re-checked at the dequeue and before the apply).
- The undo journal holds the source's granted-column values at rest in the user's own file (never a credential; never statement text; never shown to the reader); an undo changes nothing unless every row still holds the values the write left; rowid renumbering (VACUUM) on a table without an INTEGER PRIMARY KEY makes an undo fail whole rather than hit another row.
- A reader can make a source RUN once per five minutes (GET-only during the window, counted on the file's day, logged `refreshed`, refused when schedules are off or paused); `requestedBy` is the reader's display name entering the source's frame as data, folded to one line; after a refresh the source's frame stays ask-only until reopened (D-PR1-3's cost); a stranded kv key after a crash is inert.
- The driver's write signal tells every live reader WHEN a source changed (ids only) — a timing side-channel the 2 s coalescing blurs; DDL rings nothing; the host's own handshake writes ring nothing.
- A 1.1 hub cannot read a v2 grant (fail closed); a 1.1 host answers the three ops and an ask carrying `access` `MALFORMED`.
- `access-source-changed` and `access-read` reach only LIVE frames; a hidden frame is never registered and never rung; a 1.1 source app repaints on its next open.
- Pre-existing, named now: sql.js `export()` closes and reopens the connection, so a source frame's open transaction is discarded by the next persist (≤ 250 ms), read, dump, plan or `describeAppData`; the read path's cell mask is start-anchored while the journal's walk is token-split.
- A CHECK, UNIQUE or partial index the source wrote over an UNSHARED column: writes on such tables are refused when a CHECK exists; a UNIQUE over an unshared column cannot collide with a value the reader cannot supply.
- A discover answers a class from the whole library once a minute per reader (an export per app) — bounded, nothing recorded.
