### Contract v2.1 notes — decided after the five test lanes (2026-10-11; binding for the implementation lanes; every lane question answered, the proposed answer taken unless a note says otherwise)

*The wire (W)*
- **W-Q1** `accessRequestHash`: absent and `'read'` hash identically to the 1.1 bytes (declines recorded before the upgrade still match); `'write'` adds `"access":"write"` to the key-sorted canonical JSON.
- **W-Q2** Defaults are OMITTED from the canonical intent; a 1.1 record's intent string is today's nine-key string byte for byte; `withheld: []` parses and normalises to absent; a widened record adds `kv`/`refresh` at the root and `withheld`/`write` per table, key-sorted.
- **W-Q3** The VIEW is tolerant of keys, not of shapes: `kv: false` or `write: 'yes'` on a known seat is MALFORMED; a read-only view carries none of the three keys.
- **W-Q4** `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_ROW_BYTES` is enforced in `putAccessUndo` (the one row per target); the entry schema caps one entry at `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES`.
- **W-Q5** `via` may ride ANY kind; `authority` and `undoId` are `wrote`-only; `changes` is `wrote|undone`-only; a `refreshed` line's `reason` ∈ `ACCESS_REFRESH_REASONS` (`'recent'` refused — the cooldown writes no line); the other kinds keep the display-line rule for `reason`.
- **W-Q6** Each captured cell is walked as `findRecordCredential({ [column]: value })`; `readerName` is host-written and not walked (the log entry's precedent).
- **W-Q7** KB 00's clause is added beside the pinned phrase, never spliced into it; the literal is "or change, when you allowed it". `10-overview-and-contract.md` carries no access clause today and gains none.
- **W-Q8** KB 87's write rule lives under the pinned heading "## Rules for Reading Another App", verbatim with `at most ${ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS} rows at a time`; the "**Read, never write.**" bullet is removed.
- **W-Q9** The helper's three arms are `data.event === '<event>'` for `access-source-changed`, `data-changed`, `access-read` (the protocol constants' values); the 1.1 arm stays verbatim; the usage fence names none of them.
- **W-Q10** The settlers: `write` → `{ ok: true, changes }` (a host's extra seat dropped); `refresh` → `{ ok: true, fresh, reason? }` (`reason` only when sent); `discover` → `{ ok: true, candidates }`; another op's success → today's mismatch HOST_ERROR; each listener mirrors `readChange` and reduces to exactly `{ grantId }` / `{ table }` / `{ grantId }`.
- **W-Q11** `discover(hints?: AccessRequestHints)`, `write(grantId, sql, params?: AccessParam[])`, `refresh(grantId)`, `AccessRequestOptions.access?: 'read' | 'write'`; the listeners return `() => void`. The lane's local result types are replaced by the real exports at Gate 5.

*The db (D)*
- **D-Q1** `BrokeredPlan.columns` is the table's GRANTED columns (the scope's); `ParsedDml.columns` is what the statement names. The apply re-decides every table rule from the plan alone.
- **D-Q2** `applyBrokeredBatch(db, { plans, caps, expectChanges?, requireUndo, capture } | { undo: UndoStep[], caps })`; the driver's `BrokeredWriteInput` mirrors the same union (D-PR3-11's `steps?` IS the `undo` variant).
- **D-Q3** `ROLLBACK` in `finally` runs ONLY when this apply's own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` succeeded — a `busy` answer leaves the app's open transaction exactly as it was.
- **D-Q4** A `-` immediately followed by a numeric token is ONE signed literal; any other arithmetic is refused by name.
- **D-Q5** `ParsedDml.literals` = every string literal's unescaped value and every numeric literal as written; `NULL`/`TRUE`/`FALSE` are not literals there.
- **D-Q6** A parse refusal's message names the offending token; a column-rule refusal names the column; the rest of the wording is the engine's (`ACCESS_APP_MESSAGES.writeRefused`).
- **D-Q7** The contradiction resolved: "prunes expired and undone first" is the PRUNING ORDER when the row needs room. Every put drops EXPIRED entries; UNDONE entries are KEPT (marked) until they expire and are the first to go when the row cap needs room; a LIVE entry is never evicted (S9). §22.4's sentence stands.
- **D-Q8** `putAccessUndo` throws `UserDbError ACCESS_LIMIT` (the log's precedent) when, after pruning expired and undone entries, the row would exceed `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_ROW_BYTES`, leaving the file byte-identical. **The engine pre-checks BEFORE the seat**, conservatively: the live entries' serialised bytes + `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES` must fit the row cap, else the write is refused `ACCESS_WRITE_REFUSED` (`writeRefused('waiting')`) — so the accessor's throw is a belt, unreachable by construction; were it reached after a commit, the `wrote` line carries no `undoId` and the delta names it.
- **D-Q9** A target the file does not hold → `NOT_FOUND`, as `appendAccessLog`.
- **D-Q10** No new seat: `markAccessUndone(targetAppId, id, at)` stamps `undoneAt` for a done AND a failed undo (both stop being live; a fourth argument, if passed, is ignored). The sheet's failed-undo sentence is transient (this session); after a reload the `wrote` row simply offers no undo.
- **D-Q11** The key helpers: `ACCESS_UNDO_SETTING_PREFIX` `'accessUndo:'` + `accessUndoSettingKey(targetAppId)` / `targetAppIdFromAccessUndoSettingKey(key)`; `ACCESS_WANTS_SETTING_PREFIX` `'accessWants:'` + `accessWantsSettingKey(appId)` / `appIdFromAccessWantsSettingKey(key)`; full-prefix parse; an empty id throws.
- **D-Q12** `deleteApp` sweeps only `accessUndo:<appId>` (its own journal as a target) and `accessWants:<appId>`; entries in other targets' journals whose reader is the deleted app stay (the user may still undo what it changed; the reader's name is on the entry).
- **D-Q13** `scopeCopy` is exported; `masked` holds the lower-cased `table.column` pairs where the VALUE mask overwrote at least one cell (withheld and credential-named columns are not in it — the column rule refuses them anyway).
- **D-Q14** Foreign-key actions are checked at the LIVE seat only (the raw copy opens with `foreign_keys = 0`); the plan seat checks a trigger in `sqlite_master`, virtual, WITHOUT ROWID, `ON CONFLICT REPLACE` and the CHECK rule.
- **D-Q15** A captured cell outside `accessParamSchema` (a BLOB, or TEXT longer than `ACCESS_PARAM_STRING_MAX_CHARS`) → with `requireUndo` ROLLBACK (`too-large` for long text, `unsafe` for a BLOB); without it, commit with no undo. Never a storage throw after a commit.
- **D-Q16** `changes` is always present on an `exec` signal (the statement's count); the test's conditional pin holds.
- **D-Q17** The belt catches a stale or planted `sqlite_sequence` row naming a table outside the grant.
- **D-Q18** The fixtures use high-entropy credential-shaped values (the walk's real shapes). Noted; no contract change.
- **D-Q19** A DELETE-undo whose rowid was reused hits UNIQUE → `failed` (a SQL error); the engine words `failed` and `drifted` alike at the sheet ("changed these rows since").
- **D-Q20** `changes` on an undo apply is per step (rows put back); the engine sums for the `undone` line.
- **D-dev** The caps may stay literals in the db tests until Gate 5; I-D imports them from the protocol in production code. The `brokeredWrite` refusal through `scheduledDbDriver` is E2's pin (written).

*The access engine (E1)*
- **E1-Q1** `ACCESS_APP_MESSAGES.writeRefused(rule)` takes a token: `'statement'` (not one plain INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE with literal values — every parse refusal) · `'table'` (a trigger, virtual, WITHOUT ROWID, REPLACE, an FK action, a CHECK over an unshared column, a DELETE on a partly shared table) · `'no-where'` · `'column'` (withheld, credential-named or masked) · `'computed'` · `'value'` (a credential-shaped literal, param or captured cell) · `'too-many'` · `'too-large'` · `'waiting'` (the 20-entry cap or the row cap); every answer shares the prefix "the change was refused — "; the sentences are D-PR3-24's (plus `statement`: "only one plain INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE with literal values is allowed").
- **E1-Q2** `undoBrokeredWrite(db, targetAppId, undoId, { via: 'user' })` → `{ ok: true } | { ok: false; reason: 'busy' | 'changed' | 'gone' | 'failed'; message }` with `message = ACCESS_SHEET.undoFailed(reason, sourceName)`; `'gone'` = a schema mismatch (a missing table or column, a generated column) or an entry no longer live (`markAccessUndone` for the schema case). The sentences CONTAIN their token word (U's pins read "contains the reason"): `busy` → "couldn't undo — <Source> is busy — try again in a moment" · `changed` → "couldn't undo — <Source> changed these rows since" · `gone` → "couldn't undo — the change is gone — it can no longer be undone" · `failed` → "couldn't undo — <Source> failed to put the rows back". An undo is never journaled.
- **E1-Q3** A refusal's named effect is performed on a `read` only (A-Q1 stands); a refused write, refresh or discover leaves the history and the grant unchanged.
- **E1-Q4** Rule 4′ answers `ACCESS_UNATTENDED` with `ACCESS_APP_MESSAGES.unattended`, retryable, no effect, no line, for every caller — even a grant ticked *also while I'm away* — after rules 1–3, before rule 5.
- **E1-Q5** `refresh` is `read`'s rules 1–5 LITERALLY (rule 4 admits a persisted away grant with nobody present, never a session grant; a frame's refusal names `refused-line`, the schedule's is silent — and the effect is still performed only on a read, E1-Q3).
- **E1-Q6** A rate-limited write answers exactly as a read (`ACCESS_RATE_LIMITED`, `queryRateLimited`, retryable); every op counts.
- **E1-Q7 = U-Q11** `ConsentDecision.allow.tables`, `CreateGrantInput.tables` and the sheet's seed accept a bare table name ≡ `{ name, withheld: [], write: false }`; the sheet always emits objects; every PR-2 fixture stays byte-identical.
- **E1-Q8** The `inTransaction` flag must not strand: the driver CLEARS it whenever the connection is reopened (`runExport`, `persist`, `runImport`) and on a `COMMIT`/`END`/`ROLLBACK` that fails "no transaction is active"; `SAVEPOINT`/`RELEASE` count as BEGIN/COMMIT at the outermost. (I-D implements; an Opus pins lane adds the recovery rows before verification.)
- **E1-Q9** A plan's fresh export is NOT kept in the bytes cache; the next read of that grant exports again.
- **E1-Q10** The discover cache is keyed by the reader's app id, read with `accessDeps().now()`, a hit while `now − at < ACCESS_DISCOVER_CACHE_MS`, dropped by `resetAccessSession`; a hidden or delegated-window frame is refused before any gather.
- **E1-Q11** A refresh admitted by the policy on a file without the `schedulerState` row answers `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` with `refreshOff`, not retryable, and writes NO line (gate refusals write nothing; only `capped` writes the line).
- **E1-Q12** The strict seat input is `{ plans: [plan], caps: { maxChanges: ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS, maxUndoBytes: ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES }, expectChanges: [plan.previewChanges], requireUndo: true, capture: true }`; own mode `{ requireUndo: false, capture: false, expectChanges: proposal.previewed }`; `executeApprovedWrite` makes no awaited `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` through `driver.handle` any more.
- **E1-dev** The doorbell ignores `kind: 'brokered'` (the broker rings) — an E2 pin to add with the pins lane.

*The scheduling seams (E2)*
- **E2-Q1** The gates read the platform the deps hand them: `deps.platform().scheduler` present AND `deps.platform().capabilities.schedule !== false` (production deps = `defaultAppRunDeps()`, the real platform); plus the `schedulerState` row and `globalPause === false` from the db.
- **E2-Q2** The three gates, the seat-less refusal and `busy` write NO line and stamp NO cooldown; `capped` writes its line (reason `capped`) and stamps nothing.
- **E2-Q3** `capped` → `refreshFailed('capped')`; `no-handler` → `refreshNoHandler`; `timeout`/`refused`/`failed` → `refreshFailed(reason)`; `busy` → `ACCESS_APP_MESSAGES.busy`, retryable.
- **E2-Q4** A record holding refusals outranks the app's `ok` (the scheduler's own rule): the answer is `ACCESS_REFRESH_FAILED` (not retryable) and the line's reason is `refused` — the source tried to send a change on a reader's act, and the person should see it.
- **E2-Q5** The source's `schedule-result { ok: false }` → reason `failed`; its summary appears nowhere.
- **E2-Q6** A second refresh of a source while the first is in flight → `{ fresh: false, reason: 'recent' }` (the cooldown stamps at dispatch); `ACCESS_BUSY` only when `beginDelegatedRun` refuses (another run in flight) or a second hidden mount.
- **E2-Q7** `refreshSource(db, deps, { grant, requestedBy: <the reader's RAW library name>, via })`; the engine applies `oneLine` ONCE and uses it for the kv input and `REFRESH_RUN.title`; the line's `readerName` is the host-written library name.
- **E2-Q8** The delegated record: `{ appId: source, appName, runId: uuid, taskId: 'access-refresh', title: REFRESH_RUN.title(oneLine(requestedBy)), generation, posture: 'refresh' }`; the hint `{ taskId: 'access-refresh', runId }`; the kv payload exactly `{ taskId, runId, input: { kind: 'refresh', requestedBy } }`.
- **E2-Q9** The deadline is `now + deps.resultTimeoutMs` (production deps carry `SCHEDULE_RESULT_TIMEOUT_MS`).
- **E2-Q10** Every mutating call inside a refresh window records `why: 'refused'` (the posture check is FIRST in branch (1); never `already-asked`, no timer, no prompt).
- **E2-Q11** `armDoorbell(db: UserDb): () => void` (subscribes once); `ringDataChanged(targetAppId, table: string | undefined)`; `ringSourceChanged(db, sourceAppId, options?: { except?: string })` — uncoalesced, every LIVE reader minus `except`; `ringRead(sourceAppId, grantId)`.
- **E2-Q12** Coalescing per source namespace: the first signal rings at once; any further signal inside 2 s of that ring causes exactly ONE more ring at `t0 + 2000` (the window end); a lone signal has no trailing ring; another source's window is independent; only live grants are rung; a kv-only window rings kv grants only.
- **E2-Q13** The chip composes its accessible name as PR-1's: `${appName} is fetching new data — ${title}` ("Ledger is fetching new data — Budget asked"); the label `RUNNING_CHIP.fetching(appName)`; no button; when the scheduler has something running, today's chip wins.
- **E2-Q14** `SCHEDULED_DB_REFUSAL.brokered` = "a scheduled run never changes another app's data" beside the two existing sentences; the stub never forwards.
- **E2-Q15** A `refreshed` line carries no `tables`.
- **E2-dev** `via: 'refresh'` on the auth-failure store needs `state/net.ts` (`AuthShapedFailure.via` + `'refresh'`; the handler's `scheduledRun` seam becomes `scheduledVia?: () => 'scheduled-run' | 'refresh' | undefined`) and `run/appRuntime.ts` (the attended runtime reports `delegatedRunFor(appId)?.posture === 'refresh' ? 'refresh' : 'scheduled-run'`; the hidden runtime `'scheduled-run'`) — **assigned to I-E2**; the `netState.test.ts` pin is the pins lane's.

*The UI, the knowledge, the e2e (U)*
- **U-Q1** `historyLine(entry, now, readerName, sourceName)`; `AccessHistory` passes the sheet's app name.
- **U-Q2** `ACCESS_SHEET.undoArm(table, n, sourceName)`; `AccessHistory` takes a `sourceName` prop.
- **U-Q3** `CONSENT_SHEET.undoNote(sourceName, table, readerName)`.
- **U-Q4** The bytes: `pickAColumn` "choose at least one column" · `moreOptions` "more options…" · `couldChange` "could also change rows" · `undo` "undo" · `undone` "undone" · `undoFailed(reason, sourceName)` per E1-Q2 · `refreshFailed(reason)` per reason, naming no source fact: `capped` "the day's schedule limit is reached — try again tomorrow" · `timeout` "the other app took too long" · `refused` "a change the other app tried to send was refused" · `failed` "the other app's fetch failed" · `no-handler` → `refreshNoHandler`.
- **U-Q5** A `wrote` line's via clause comes after the time: "changed 3 rows in transactions · 2 min ago · from its chat".
- **U-Q6** `REFRESH_HISTORY_WORDS` are functions of `sourceName`, keyed exactly by `ACCESS_REFRESH_REASONS`.
- **U-Q7** `egressFor`'s options gain `canChange?: boolean`, passed only when a write is ticked; the kinds are unchanged.
- **U-Q8** `write: string[]` (the written tables) on `STRIP_OUTCOME.allowed(…, write)`, `CONSENT_UI.stopped(…, write)` and `StripOutcome` `'stopped'`; an empty array gives today's line.
- **U-Q9** `AccessHistory` props `{ entries, now, undoable, onUndo, sourceName }`; `onUndo(undoId): Promise<{ ok: true } | { ok: false; message: string }>`; the outcome line is `ACCESS_SHEET.undone` or `undoFailed`'s message as handed over (the sheet passes `message` through — U's "contains" pin holds).
- **U-Q10** `access-undo-confirm` (`role="group"`, `aria-label = ACCESS_SHEET.undo`), `access-undo-yes`, `access-undo-keep`.
- **U-Q11** = E1-Q7 (tolerate the string form).
- **U-Q12** `preselect` stays `{ appId, tables: string[] }`; the old scope's `write`/`withheld`/`kv`/`refresh` travel in the `SheetSeed`.
- **U-Q13** `resolution.allowed.write` is additive-optional on the card's persisted resolution (`readResolution` admits it; D-PR3-27 is unchanged — the key's hand-rolled parse grows one optional seat).
- **U-Q14** The chips are not on screen while the columns disclosure is open; `withheld` lists the unticked columns in the source's column order.
- **U-Q15** The note and the two option labels are pinned by text, not by id.
- **U-Q16** The KB clauses sit inside the existing sentences (the host-mcp example given), each appearing once.
- **U-Q17** `ACCESS_SHEET.you = 'you'` (a copy key equal to the pinned literal).
- **U-Q18** A source that announces but never answers ends `timeout` (90 s); `no-handler` is the engine's never-announced end. The e2e pins only the code; the engine's suite owns the reason.
- **U-Q19** The presence pins for the three modules are red until they land; the planted proofs `it.skipIf(absent)`; "limits.ts is gone" red until D-PR3-26.
- **U-dev** The e2e reader fixture's helper block is re-rendered from KB 87 v2 by **I-W** (the fixture's SYNC RULE); `#ask` stays the read ask and `#ask-write` carries `access: 'write'`.

*Cross-lane*
- **X-1** I-E2 also owns `state/net.ts` and `run/appRuntime.ts` for the `via: 'refresh'` seam (E2-dev) and `schedule/scheduledDbDriver.ts`.
- **X-2** The Opus PINS lane (before verification) adds: `netState.test.ts` `via: 'refresh'`; the driver's flag recovery rows (E1-Q8); `doorbell.test.ts` "a brokered signal rings nothing from the doorbell"; and swaps the lanes' local types for the real exports where the implementation lanes did not.
- **X-3** `packages/knowledge/src/__tests__/cross-app-access-kb.test.ts` holds both W's and U's rows; committed once.

## PR-4 — Gate-2 contract v2 (2026-10-11; FOLDED from four fresh-context plan reviews on Opus 5.5 xhigh — security 17 findings (0 blockers) · feasibility 20 (1) · design 20 (3) · scope 17 (1); five blockers across the four, every one folded below; v1 is in this branch's history at `aab3c78`. The test lanes write against THIS; the implementation lanes implement THIS.)

**Routing (owner, this session):** tests written and run on Opus 5.5 high; planning, decisions, judgement, implementation and reviews on Opus 5.5 xhigh.

### What the code says today (the seam maps' anchors, re-verified by the four reviews; each a claim checked at the line)

- **There is no data-lane turn body to lift.** `useBuilderChat.send` (`agent/useBuilderChat.ts:585-1175`) is ONE body for every lane; `runAgentTurn` runs inside `createDirectBuilder.send` (`agent/builder.ts:350`) under the BUILDER system prompt (`:266`) with F15 (`:273`), the per-send pin and `fitHostTurn`. `runAgentTurn` caps ROUND TRIPS (`maxIterations`, default 48; `packages/adapters/src/agent-turn.ts:66,106`) and dispatches every call of a round trip (`:171-178`); its only pre-send hook `round_trip_start` is synchronous and void (`:133`) and an abort becomes `CANCELLED` (`:207-213`). `buildDataTools` with `allowWrites` absent returns BOTH tools (`agent/dataTools.ts:287` vs `:101`).
- **The tool-free step's persona is the app-runtime JSON responder** — `createDirectAppTransport`'s base system is `buildHostSystemPrompt({appRuntime:true})` = layers 10 + 45 ("answer the app's request and nothing else") + 40 ("respond with ONLY a valid JSON object") (`agent/transport.ts:105-110`, `assemble.ts:107-110`), with the runtime contract as the END suffix (`:160-161`).
- **The scheduler's shapes are INTERNAL** (`packages/protocol/src/schedule.ts` is not in `json-schemas.ts`'s sixteen; pinned `schedule.test.ts:876`), every one `strictObject`: `app-think` (`:326-331`, comment `:313` "no tools, no connected calls"), the task (`:356-385`), the step result (`:337-342`), the run (`:422-444`, ≤ 8 KiB; proposal items `{appId, sql (isSingleDmlStatement — admits functions, arithmetic, `OR REPLACE`), summary?, counts?}` with `counts` OPTIONAL). An older hub DROPS a run entry its schema refuses, on import and export (`userdb/schedules.ts:633-668`).
- **The engine:** `defaultTransportFor` refuses only the demo override (`schedule/appThink.ts:158-161`; keyless BYOK falls to the mock silently, `agent/adapter.ts:200`); each candidate proposal is dry-run ALONE on its own copy (`:302-341`); `RESPONSE_SCHEMA` tells the model proposals are "never run automatically" (`:130-144`); the step summary on a failure is the adapter's raw message (`:386`). `executors.ts`: `defaultSharedFor` records eagerly (`:81-91`); `finalizeOutcome` whitelists fields (`:107-117`). `queue.ts`: proposals pooled from EVERY step whatever its status (`:392`), sliced to 3 (`:411-414`); an aborted run (`abortReason` — cancel, timeout, pause, file swap) still reaches the pooling point (`:313-318`, `:395-401`); the `app-think` pre-check reserves `{ai:1}` only (`:355-363`); a step that loses the `thinkMs` race (300 s, `:91`) is dropped with its calls UNFOLDED (`:364-381`, `:430`); `toStepResult` copies status + summary (`:172-176`); `fitRunRow` drops summaries then proposals (`:204-216`); the M17 row (`:420-428`). `StepContext.task` is captured at claim (`:282,331`).
- **The Write Broker (PR-3):** `brokeredWrite` is STRICT (another app's data; `access/writeBroker.ts:121-131`), checks journal room BEFORE the seat (`:344`) and journals AFTER COMMIT (`:352`; a post-commit schema refusal only `console.warn`s, `:296-299`). `applyApprovedWrite` (`:387-416`) is OWN mode — class guard → `scratchRun` (an export: it discards an open frame transaction, `driver.ts:529-531`) → seat `{own, expectChanges}` → `ringDataChanged`; no capture, journal, line, cache clear or `ringSourceChanged`. In the pure module (`packages/db/src/brokered-write.ts`): `applyPlans` refuses `too-many` per statement whatever `requireUndo` (`:1206`), commits before any journal (`:1224-1228`), and degrades a cell/byte miss to `undoable:false` when `requireUndo` is false (`:1211-1222`); `applyPlans` and `applyOwn` each open their OWN `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (`:1199`, `:1256`); `tableFacts` adds the reader-oracle rules (CHECK-over-unshared `:797`, DELETE full coverage `:798`) worded for "another app"; `applyUndo` uses `tableShape` + the SEC-4 credential-column refusal (`:1282-1298`); the module imports no schema (the Worker rule, `:115-123`). The journal schema refuses `snug_kv` as a table (`access.ts:355-358`), a column name > 64 (`:433`), > 64 columns or > 200 rows a step (`:659-666`). `ACCESS_WRITE_AUTHORITIES` is a flat string enum (`access.ts:193`); policy rule 4′ refuses every scheduled write (`access/policy.ts:243-255`; spec 1.2 §24.5 item 18).
- **App versions:** `pauseSchedulesForAppVersion` returns 0 for an `own` change (`schedule/appDrift.ts:28`), its caller skips `starter` (`state/appVersionChanged.ts:34`), `pauseForAppUpdate` early-returns (`protection.ts:168-173`), resume re-records versions (`acts.ts:236-237`).
- **History lifetimes:** a task keeps ≤ 50 runs / 64 KiB (`schedule.ts:111-112`); *clear history* keeps only pending/running/unseen needs-you (`userdb/schedules.ts:213`) although its sentence promises "what is still waiting for you stays" (`copy.page.ts:146`); deleting a task deletes its runs row (`:389-393`).
- **The executor** (`packages/auth/src/connected-fetch.ts`): symbolic resolution refuses a ceiling that is not exactly one host (`:960-965`) — every OAuth ceiling is multi-host by derivation (`connection-requirement.ts:665-691`); the ADR-0026 host guard runs ONLY on the symbolic branch (`:998`); the WHATWG parser strips tab/LF/CR before resolving, so `new URL('/\t/evil.example/a','https://api.x')` lands on `evil.example`; the literal path labels refusals with the raw host (`:826,837`); GET/HEAD never reach the confirm gate (`:1118-1131`); the body cap is 1 MiB, discard-on-overflow (`:1303`); `execute` takes no signal; R-54 scrubs exact substrings only (`packages/auth/src/scrub.ts:21-37`). `connectedFetchDepsFor` wires `sidecarAppFetch`, which autostarts the WhatsApp helper (`state/net.ts:381-399,467`).
- **Import:** `reconcileImportedSchedules` (`userdb/schedules.ts:727-793`) leaves an intent-equal task AS IS (`:748-751`), demotes any other keeping "the rest of it intact" (`:752-765`); trusted: untouched (`:740`). The reconcile tests live in `userdb.test.ts:467-730`. The sync push skips the run-row strip when no row matches `LIKE '%"proposals"%'` (`:691-698`, `userdb.ts:3754-3759`).
- **Surfaces:** `EnableConsent` has no authority seat and shows *may call: <hosts>* on every ask step (`EnableConsent.tsx:93-94`); the editor shows the consent only on create-when-spending or an imported task (`ScheduleEditor.tsx:193`) and sends `steps` on every save (`:172`); `updateTask` spreads `...task` (`acts.ts:197-216`); `ScheduleRow` line 2 shows when · next · app chips (`:203-226`); the result page has ONE changes section, *changes waiting for your OK* (`ResultDetail.tsx:325-329`), removes an approved item and keeps "applied" in React state (`:195-224`); the feed and the row's history print the first step's AI summary (`ResultsList.tsx:22-37`, `ScheduleRow.tsx:257`); the chat card's words are JSX literals in `views/ChatLog.tsx:203-241`; `AccessHistory`'s armed confirm moves focus only after the answer (`:86-101,141-146`).
- **Vocabulary:** `scheduleCopy.test.ts:576-631` is a REGEX scan (cannot see JSX text) refusing *task*/*proposal* in `schedule/*.ts(x)` + `views/Schedule*.tsx` + `access/refresh.ts`; `copy.ts:15-17` bans *routine*/*automation* and "run" as a noun; `accessCopy.test.ts` is the TS-parser walk; `agent/` and `views/ChatLog.tsx` are scanned by neither.
- **The kit** runs the scheduler (`apps/host` → `initScheduler`); every kit brain is host `tools:false` or demo (`apps/host/vite.config.ts:9-13`); 2,565,276 B against 2,750,000.

### PR-4 decisions (each reversible by one word; the fold that produced it in brackets)

- **D-PR4-1 Scope and shape.** PR-4 = **host-native jobs** (AC29, AC30, AC32) + **the task write authority and own-app undo** (AC31, AC32a, AC32b) + R-88's result line + the vocabulary scan's parse upgrade (AC32d) + the docs (AC32e). **ONE PR** (the owner asked for "the PR" and merges by hand), its Gate-5 review PARTITIONED into three halves each under ~5k lines — (i) host-native jobs (agent, fetch, prompts, kit), (ii) authority + own writes (protocol, db, broker, walk, queue, acts, reconcile), (iii) UI + copy + docs — the D-PR3-1 precedent. **Moved to PR-5 with numbered ACs** (AC39a–AC39e below): the chat's cross-app write (D-PR3-16's reversal — this review's S4, F6, D14, D15, D17 are its Gate-2 inputs, recorded in the fold record), the three *fetch* affordances and the refresh chip's *cancel*, the owner's ONE push of the staged spec revision + whitepaper edition 6. **Its own future task** (spec 1.3, §24.5 item 18): Q4's armed half — an authority over ANOTHER app's data. [SC1 (stacked PRs declined for one PR with partitioned reviews — the owner's instruction), SC2, SC10, SC11 — one-word reversals: "stack" (stacked PRs), "chat" (the cross-app write back in)]
- **D-PR4-2 The task authority covers the task's OWN app's data only.** Rule 4′ and spec 1.2 §24.5 item 18 stand; a scheduled `data_propose_write` keeps refusing shared tables; a cross-app item can only be pending; `authority:'task'` appears on no `wrote` line. [v1 — "cross"]
- **D-PR4-3 The own journal, one row per bucket.** Keys (host-internal, `app-settings-keys.ts`): `ownUndo:<appId>:<taskId>` (one per armed task) and `ownUndo:<appId>:approval` (the user's own approvals); each row ≤ `OWN_UNDO_ROW_MAX_BYTES` (256 KiB), entries 7 days (`ACCESS_UNDO_TTL_MS`). Entry `ownUndoEntrySchema` (INTERNAL module `packages/protocol/src/own-undo.ts`, NOT in `json-schemas.ts` — pinned): `{id: uuid v4, at, expiresAt, authority: 'approval' | 'task', via: 'chat' | 'schedule' | 'user', runId?, taskId?, steps: accessUndoStepSchema[1..ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_STEPS], undoneAt?}` — the per-cell walk, the 64 KiB cap and `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_STEPS` (now exported) reused, extracted not copied; no SQL text. **A task bucket** holds ≤ `OWN_UNDO_MAX_LIVE` (20) live entries — room is checked BEFORE the seat (count < 20 AND live bytes + (64 KiB − `ACCESS_UNDO_ENTRY_RESERVE_BYTES`) ≤ 256 KiB), else `full` and nothing applies (`held: 'full'`). **The approval bucket NEVER refuses** — the oldest live approval entry gives way (the user was present for each); an entry that alone cannot fit applies uncaptured (`undoable:false`, said). Undone entries give way first in both. Swept: `deleteScheduledTask` sweeps its bucket (the delete confirm warns, D-PR4-18), `deleteApp` the `ownUndo:<appId>:` prefix; dropped on an untrusted import, kept on a trusted pull. The §8.1 key goes into the staged spec revision (D-PR4-22). [SC3, F10, D3, S3 — "shared"]
- **D-PR4-4 ONE own-write seat that captures.** `BrokeredApplyInput` gains `{ own, capture: true, caps, expectChanges, requireUndo, admit }` (packages/db pure module + driver forwarding; `driver.test.ts:696`'s bare-`{own}` pins stay as written — the variant gets its own describe). Rules: `tableShape` + an OWN column rule, never `tableFacts` (its rules are reader oracles) — an UPDATE/INSERT naming a credential-named column, or a DELETE on a table holding one, is unplannable (its undo would hit SEC-4 anyway); generated columns are not captured; the plan's columns are every non-generated, non-credential-named live column; refusal words are own-mode words. **Plannability is decided for the WHOLE batch before any transaction**: all plannable → the capture path; any unplannable → an approval runs the bare `{own}` path for the whole batch, a task refuses `cannot-undo` — never a mixed batch, never one transaction shared by the two functions. `sum(changes) ≤ caps.maxChanges` over the WHOLE call, checked after each statement. `admit(steps) → boolean` (the engine's `ownUndoEntrySchema.safeParse` of the exact entry it will write — so `snug_kv`, a long column name, > 64 columns, > 200 rows a step, > 64 steps, a credential cell, > 64 KiB are all caught) is evaluated after the last statement and BEFORE `COMMIT`: false → `requireUndo:true` rolls back `cannot-undo`; `requireUndo:false` commits `undoable:false`. An approval passes `caps.maxChanges = Infinity`, and when `sum(previewed) > ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS` the engine asks for the bare `{own}` up front (never refused for its size). `brokered-write.ts` imports nothing from `own-undo.ts` or `access.ts` (the Worker rule; `admit` carries the schema). `ownCapturable(sql) → {ok:true} | {ok:false, rule}` is a pure `@snugprotocol/db` export over the parser (a named predicate; W-5's surface otherwise unchanged). [F1 (blocker), F2, F3, S8, F18, F19, F11 — "own"]
- **D-PR4-5 Own writes ring, invalidate and never discard a live transaction.** Every own write (approval or task) clears the namespace's read caches and rings `access-source-changed` to its live readers and `data-changed` to its own frame (§24.7 item 29). **Every seat PR-4 adds that exports a live namespace asks `driver.inTransaction(ns)` FIRST**: the approval order is class guard → `inTransaction` → `scratchRun` drift halt → seat; the armed walk asks before its re-dry-run; `appAgent`'s `data_query` and the scheduled dry run ask on the app and answer `Error: <App> is in the middle of a change — try later`. [v1, S12 — "ring"]
- **D-PR4-6 `executeApprovedWrite(db, proposal, {via})`** (callers: the chat card `via:'chat'`, the result page `via:'user'`) → the capture seat with `requireUndo:false`, authority `approval`, the approval bucket; the outcome union gains `undoId?` and `undoable` (an applied change that cannot be undone says so); busy → `failed` with the busy sentence. The class guard, the drift halt and their characterisation stay. [v1 — "approve"]
- **D-PR4-7 The authority record.** `scheduledTaskSchema` + `writeAuthority?: {rowsPerRun: int 1..ACCESS_WRITE_MAX_ROWS, armedAt: iso, armedVersion: int}` (one app — D-PR4-8; PR-4's surface writes `rowsPerRun: 200`) + `disarmed?: {reason: 'imported' | 'app-updated', at: iso}` (a trace of a disarm the user did not perform; cleared by the next arming). Both internal, strict, omitted when absent. **`canonicalScheduleIntent` excludes both** (consent state, like `enabled`; every untrusted import strips the authority, so inclusion would only demote an unarmed backup — AC31's "includes" amended). `'task'` and `'approval'` are spelled once: `OWN_WRITE_AUTHORITIES = ['approval','task'] as const satisfies readonly AccessWriteAuthority[]` (and `OWN_UNDO_VIAS` likewise over `AccessLogVia`), imported — never a literal under `schedule/`. [S2, D6, SC17 — "intent"]
- **D-PR4-8 Arming, its surfaces, and what disarms (ADR-0033 §1–§2).** **Offered only for a task whose *Ask <App>'s AI* steps all name ONE app** (the task's app) — one seat call per run, one undo per run; a multi-app task shows no tick. **The tick is OFF on every mount and never seeded** — not from a proposal, an app's suggestion, an imported or disarmed task, a draft or storage. It is ONE act on `EnableConsent` (all four hosts) and on the editor's task-level control *changes it suggests:* (•) *wait for my OK* / (•) *make them on its own*, shown under the steps when a step is *Ask <App>'s AI*. An EXISTING task's save goes through `EnableConsent` (the tick pre-set to the editor's choice) when: the choice moves to *make them on its own*; an armed task's `steps` change (compared by canonical JSON, never `patch.steps !== undefined`); its `ownerAppId` changes; its spec's minimum interval gets SHORTER. The editor warns above save on an armed task whose steps or timing changed: *changing what it does stops it making changes on its own — you’ll confirm again before saving*. *wait for my OK* disarms on save with no consent; a title, alert or missed-policy edit never disarms. **Disarmed by:** those edits (unless the same save passes the consent); the app's version changing from ANY source — proactively in `onAppVersionChanged` for every source (own and starter included; independent of the pause and its early returns), and by the walk's `armedVersion` belt (covers a version that arrives by trusted pull), trace `app-updated`; every untrusted import (trace `imported`); the row's act *stop making changes on its own*; the Settings act (D-PR4-10). **Kept by:** pause/resume and off/on (the walk's `stillArmed` refuses while off or paused), a trusted pull (named residual). **Shown wherever the task is:** the row's line 2 gains the chip *makes changes on its own* (a glyph whose accessible name is the words; the words on a wide screen); the editor's control shows the same state; a disarm the user did not perform leaves the attention line *no longer makes changes on its own — arrived with an imported file | this app was updated* with the one act *review*; on an armed task `paused('app-updated')` reads *paused: this app was updated — resuming won’t let it make changes on its own until you confirm again*, and `updatePausesSentence` adds *and stops it making changes on its own*. ADR-0033's one-armed-target rule is NOT inherited (each authority is bounded by its own app's data, `rowsPerRun` and its bucket) — said in the ADR-0076 fold. [D1 (blocker), D4, D5, D6, S2, S10, F14, SC8, SC14, S17 — "arm"]
- **D-PR4-9 Import.** `reconcileImportedSchedules` strips `writeAuthority` from EVERY task on an untrusted import (intent-equal and demoted alike) and writes `disarmed: {reason:'imported'}` where it stripped one; the import report gains `disarmedTasks`. `ownUndo:*` rows are dropped. `hasScheduleRunProposals` becomes `hasScheduleRunSeatsToStrip` (matches `"proposals"` OR `"applied"`); `scrubScheduleRunRows` strips `applied` with `proposals`. Trusted: untouched. [v1, F17 — "import"]
- **D-PR4-10 The kill switches are ACTS, not a row.** Per task: the row's menu *stop making changes on its own* (outcome: *it won’t change data on its own any more — turn it back on from edit*). Settings (`ScheduleSettingsCard`): *stop every schedule making changes on its own* — shown only while some task is armed, naming how many — clears `writeAuthority` on every task (one task write each); the walk's re-read applies nothing more. No `schedulerApply` key, schema, accessor, import merge, `switched-off` reason or disabled tick. [SC6 (over the v1 row; moots F16, S9, D9's file-scope hint) — "state" brings the row back]
- **D-PR4-11 The walk (`schedule/applyArmed.ts`).** At the queue's pooling point, after every step, BEFORE the run row is written. Runs only when the run ended with NO `abortReason` (a cancelled, timed-out, paused or file-swapped run applies nothing — its items stay pending, held `stopped`); only items from steps whose status is `ok` are armable (a failed, capped or refused step's items stay pending, unheld). Order: `inTransaction(app)` (busy → every item `held:'busy'`) → re-dry-run the items IN ORDER on ONE scratch copy (`db.scratchRun(appId, items)`, the chat card's rule; those fresh counts are the truth — `expectChanges` and the budget) → keep the longest prefix whose sum ≤ `rowsPerRun` (the rest `held:'over-limit'`) → `applyOwnWrite(…, {requireUndo:true, stillArmed})` where `stillArmed()` is evaluated after `applyOwnWrite`'s last await, immediately before `driver.brokeredWrite`: task present ∧ `enabled` ∧ no `pausedReason` ∧ same `armedAt` ∧ the app's current version = `armedVersion` ∧ `!globalPause` (false → `held:'stopped'`; a version mismatch also disarms with trace `app-updated`) → ONE seat call for the prefix → the `ownUndo` entry (`authority:'task'`, `via:'schedule'`, `runId`, `taskId`) and the claim row rewritten with the `applied` seat IN THE SAME SYNCHRONOUS TICK. Held reasons — ONE table (`SCHEDULE_HELD_REASONS`): `busy` · `changed` (the seat's in-transaction drift) · `cannot-undo` · `full` · `failed` · `over-limit` · `stopped`. The model never sees its own writes mid-turn. [F4, F5 (fresh counts are the truth, not a hold), F20, S7, S8, SC9, D7 — "apply"]
- **D-PR4-12 The chat card's trust belt (own half).** `metaToDataWrite` re-admits `outcome` only from its enum and `undoId` only as a uuid; the rehydrate DROPS any `dataWrite` whose `appId` is not the thread's pinned app (the S10 belt the access card has, `useBuilderChat.ts:546`). (The `target` half moves with the cross-app write to PR-5.) [v1, S4's own half — "belt"]
- **D-PR4-13 `fetch_connection` — GET only, host-pinned, tainted after a shared read.** `agent/fetchConnectionTool.ts` + `packages/knowledge/prompts/tools/fetch-connection.md`. Input EXACTLY `{connection, path, query?}` (extra keys ignored; no method can be expressed — HEAD dropped from AC30). `connection` ⊨ `CONNECTION_SLOT_RULE`; `path` ⊨ `^/(?!/)[!-~]*$` with no `?` `#` `\` (no whitespace, no C0), ≤ 2,048; `query?: Record<string, string | number | boolean>` via `URLSearchParams`. **Resolution:** an approved row whose ceiling is ONE host → `snug-connection://<slot><path>` (ADR-0026, host-clean); else `apiHost` = the single normalized member of the APPROVED row's `requirement.declaredApiHosts ∪ requirement.userLayer?.declaredApiHosts` (never `pendingRequirement`), which must be in `row.allowedHosts` → `new URL(path + query, 'https://' + apiHost)`, then the tool asserts `isHostAllowed(url.hostname, [apiHost])` BEFORE the executor (ADR-0026's guard, which the executor runs only for symbolic URLs); else refused by name. On the composed path a failure is answered by CODE only (a fixed host-free sentence per `NET_ERROR_CODES` entry naming the slot; two slots claiming one host → *two connections reach that service*). **Refused by name before the executor:** a sidecar-class slot (and the deps omit `sidecarFetch` — a missing transport, not only a check), an app that may not reach the network, an unapproved row, a LAN-class row (`lan` fact — left out of `fetchableConnections` at PR-4). **Taint at turn scope:** once the turn's `SharedTablesSeat.recordRead` has recorded any grant, `fetch_connection` answers `Error: not after reading another app’s data in this run — answer with what you have` for the rest of the turn (fetch-then-read stays allowed). Deps: `createScheduledConfirmGate()` (belt), `requestTimeoutMs: 30_000`, `onAuthShapedFailure` → `via:'scheduled-run'`. Counted: `onNetCall?: () => boolean` BEFORE the executor (`false` → `NET_CALL_LIMIT_MESSAGE`, `capped`). **Every string returned** (status line, content type, body, each `Error: <code> — <message>`) passes `scrubOrWithhold(…, 'deliver')` before the cut; `undefined` → `Error: withheld — the answer looked like it carried a credential`; the body framed by `renderProviderResult({maxChars: FETCH_CONNECTION_MAX_CHARS = 16_000})`. **GET-only is not read-only** (an API that acts on GET can be driven by fetched or shared content — bounded by the app's approved hosts, 8 calls, the net ceiling, the taint rule, disclosed by `tools.fetched`; the consent names each connection *it can use <Connection> without asking you each time*). ADR-0026's status gains the amendment. [v1, S1, S5, S6, S11, S14, F13, SC12 — "method"; "lan"; "taint"]
- **D-PR4-14 No `runDataTurn`.** `useBuilderChat.send` is untouched; `capToolCalls(tools, max)` (one shared counter; calls beyond the cap answer `Error: this run may make at most <max> tool calls — answer now with what you have`) lives beside `buildDataTools`; `executeAppAgent` composes `buildDataTools` + `buildFetchConnectionTool` + `capToolCalls` over its OWN `runAgentTurn` call — the fifth call site (code-map `:78` amended; it wires `onLlmEvent` and decides caching explicitly). The two stale closures are a next-steps rider. [SC5 — "adapter"]
- **D-PR4-15 `appAgent` — *Ask <App>'s AI* that can look things up.** `app-think` + `tools?: true` (internal; absent ≡ tool-free). `executors.ts` runs the gates itself and reaches `executeAppAgent` through a LAZY import; the host kit swaps `schedule/appAgent.ts` for a loud stub (pinned in `kitDeadLanes.test.ts` with the invariant). **Gates, in order (both executors):** the demo override or a keyless BYOK (`readerAdapterKind(db, appId) === 'demo'`) → `refused` with `blockedBy:'brain'` and `KEYLESS_BRAIN_REFUSAL` (rendered as its own state with the one act *open Settings*, never *needs your OK*); then, for a tools step, the data lane absent (`!chatDoorOpen(chatBrainRoute(ctx.db, step.appId))`) or the app holding a live sidecar fact → **degrade** to `executeAppThink` (the shared tables, tool-free) with `tools: {unavailable: 'brain' | 'sidecar'}`. **Otherwise** ONE `runAgentTurn` over `gatedAdapter(createTurnAdapter(<app pin>), admit)` — the AI-call ceiling is the ADAPTER WRAPPER: `complete()` first asks `countersSoFar(ctx.db, ctx.spent?.(), soFar, now)` + `{ai:1}` against the day's ceiling and answers `{ok:false, code:'CAPPED'}` without calling the inner adapter; `soFar.ai` increments only when it delegates; the step maps `CAPPED` to `capped`. `maxIterations = APP_AGENT_MAX_ROUND_TRIPS` (10), `capToolCalls(…, APP_AGENT_MAX_TOOL_CALLS)` (8). System = a new `buildHostSystemPrompt({ scheduledAgent: true })` branch: 10-host-identity + the new `scheduled-agent` system layer (nobody is watching; the tools; the cap; changes wait for the user unless this schedule may make them on its own; *never say a change was made — say what you proposed; the host reports what it made*) + platform-desktop on desktop + `renderRuntimeContract` as the END suffix; never layers 45 or 40; a contract-less app runs on identity + preamble. F15 (`CONSENT_REQUIRED` → `refused`, uncharged). **Deadline:** `executeAppAgent` stops itself at the queue's bound − 30 s (passed on `StepContext`), answering `capped`/`failed` WITH the calls it spent; the queue also folds a late outcome's calls when it settles. Tools: `data_query` (own data + the shared tables through a LAZY `SharedTablesSeat` bound to the schedule caller — a `read` line only when a statement names a shared table; `inTransaction` asked), the scheduled `data_propose_write` (D-PR4-16), `fetch_connection`. Reply → `scrubOrWithhold`. Hitting the round-trip cap reads *<App>’s AI looked things up 8 times and ran out of turns before answering* (`failed`). [v1, F7, F8, F9, F19, D13, S15(d) — "degrade"]
- **D-PR4-16 Scheduled proposals, both executors.** ONE gate helper (`schedule/proposalGate.ts`, extracted from `dryRunCandidates`): `scheduleProposalItemSchema.safeParse` → `findScheduleCredential` → `inTransaction` → dry-run count; ≤ 3 a run. The scheduled `data_propose_write` (`buildDataTools({…, scheduled: {armed, onProposal}})`) admits only through it (one statement, no params; a refusal answered in words the model can act on — *that change carries a value that looks like a credential — it was not proposed*); **under an armed task** an item `ownCapturable` refuses is answered *Proposed — but it can’t be made on its own (<rule>) and waits for the user’s OK; to make it applicable use literal values or ?, a WHERE on UPDATE/DELETE, no functions, arithmetic or conflict clause*. The tool-free step's `RESPONSE_SCHEMA` proposals description and the scheduled preamble state the same rule and say "never run automatically" ONLY when unarmed. The answers (unarmed: *Proposed — it waits for the user’s OK on the result*; armed: *Proposed — this schedule may make it on its own when it finishes, within its limits; the user can undo it*) carry D8's sentence. Shared tables stay refused (D-PR4-2). The chat's variant is byte-identical. [v1, S13, SC4, F11, D8 — "chat-shape"]
- **D-PR4-17 The run row's new INTERNAL seats — omit-defaults (D-PR3-2's rule).** Run + `applied?: {appId, undoId?, undoable?: false, items: [{summary?, changes}] ≤ 3}` (one group — one app; no SQL); proposal item + `held?: SCHEDULE_HELD_REASONS`; step result + `inOpenApp?: true` (an `app-run` that ran in the open app; absent = out of sight; an `app-think` is host-native by kind), `saw?: [{source ≤ 64, table ≤ 64}] ≤ 8` (only when non-empty; `oneLine`-folded — R-88), `tools?: {used: int 0..8, fetched?: [{connection: slot, calls: 1..8}] ≤ 8} | {unavailable: 'brain' | 'sidecar'}` (only on a `tools:true` step; slot names only, never a path or query), `blockedBy?: 'brain'`. `finalizeOutcome`, `toStepResult`, `fitRunRow` (drops `proposals` BEFORE `applied`, never `applied`) and the M17 row (keeps `applied`) carry them. A run of an unchanged task stays readable by a pre-PR-4 hub; a run carrying a new seat is dropped by one (the strict shapes' fail-closed direction, named). [v1, SC15, S14, D13, F17 — "seats"]
- **D-PR4-18 The result page and the schedule's changes.** `ResultDetail` has two sections, in order: **changes it made** (`WORDS.changesMade`) — the group reads *changed 12 rows in Ledger on its own · undo* (a task) or *you applied this — 2 rows changed · undo* (the user's approve, now PERSISTED as an `applied` group — a reload keeps it), *undone* after an undo, *undo ended after seven days* past it, *· can’t be undone* when `undoable:false`; then **changes waiting for your OK**, each held item with ONE line `HELD_WHY[reason]` above its acts (`cannot-undo` *not made — it couldn’t be undone, so it waits for you (applying it here can’t be undone either)* · `over-limit` *not made — it would pass the 200 rows this schedule may change each time* · `busy` *not made — Ledger was in the middle of a change* · `stopped` *not made — it was stopped from making changes on its own* · `changed` *not made — the data moved since the AI looked* · `full` *not made — 20 earlier changes can still be undone; it will make changes again as they age out* · `failed` *not made — something went wrong; nothing was changed*). *stop making changes on its own* appears ONCE, under the header's when-line (*this schedule makes changes on its own · stop*). Each step has ONE host line `stepFacts(step, result)` joined by middle dots: where (*Snug did this itself — <App> didn’t open* for an ask step · *ran in <App> while it was open* · *ran <App> out of sight while it was closed* — the word *background* never shown), saw (*read Ledger’s transactions (you allowed it)*; > 2 fold into *read 3 tables from 2 apps you allowed*), tools (*looked things up 3 times through Spotify* · *didn’t need to look anything up* · the unavailable sentence). **The schedule's changes list** (`listOwnUndo(appId, {taskId})`) — each entry *changed 12 rows in Ledger · 3 h ago · undo* — sits at the top of the schedule's history (the row's menu *history*) and is linked from each of its results, so an undo is findable for its whole seven days whatever was pruned or cleared. `keptOnClear` also keeps a run holding unexpired `proposals` or an `applied` group whose undo is still live (making `SETTINGS.clearArm` true). The delete confirm of a schedule holding live undo entries adds *— its changes from the last seven days can’t be undone after this*. **Host facts follow the AI's words** wherever a result's one line is printed (`resultFacts(run)`: *· changed 12 rows in Ledger on its own* / *· 1 change waiting for your OK*) — `ResultsList.resultSummary`, the row's history and, when the alert is `notification`, a host-composed notification body. The armed confirm (result page and chat card) moves focus to *keep* when it arms and to the outcome line when answered; the group is `role="group"` named by its question; *undo* ≥ 44 px; at 375 px each line and its *undo* wrap (`field-row-wrap`) and the confirm stacks below. [D2 (blocker), D7, D8, D18, D19 — "page"]
- **D-PR4-19 The chat card's own-app undo.** An applied own-app card offers *undo* only while `listOwnUndo(appId, {bucket:'approval'})` holds its `undoId` live (checked on render, like the ⋈ sheet's `undoable` set) → `undoOwnWrite(…, {via:'user'})` → `outcome: 'undone'` (new enum value); otherwise its line ends *· can no longer be undone*; an `undoable:false` apply reads *applied — 2 rows changed · this one can’t be undone*; counts use `rowsWord`, never *row(s)*. The card's words get ONE home, `agent/copy.dataWrite.ts` (`DATA_WRITE_CARD` — the existing JSX literals move there), scanned (D-PR4-21); the own-undo sentences live ONCE in `access/copy.ts` (`OWN_UNDO`: `arm(n, appName)` *put 12 rows in Ledger back as they were before this change? (if they changed since, nothing happens)*, reusing `ACCESS_SHEET.undoYes/undoKeep/undone/undoFailed`). [D16, D19, D20 — "card"]
- **D-PR4-20 The words.** One phrase names the authority on every surface: *make changes on its own* (tick, chip, menu act, Settings act, held lines, result). **The tick** (`CONSENT_ROWS.onItsOwn(appName)`): *let it make its changes to Ledger’s data on its own — never another app’s · up to 200 rows each time · undo any of it for seven days*; hint while unticked: *off: its changes wait for your OK on the result*; a schedule firing more than 20 times a week adds *it runs more than 20 times a week — once 20 changes are waiting to age out, the rest wait for your OK*. **The chooser** keeps the `<select>` and the pinned `stepLabel`, with ONE hint per kind (`STEPS.kindHint`): ask — *<App>’s AI answers from <App>’s data — <App> doesn’t need to open*; run — *<App> itself does the job, the way it was built — in the app if it’s open, out of sight if not* (no *routine*). The ask step's tick *let it look things up* carries *it can choose its own questions over <App>’s data and what you let it read from other apps, and fetch from <App>’s connections — read only · up to 10 AI calls each time*; with it on, *which data?* is replaced by that line and typed queries stay as starting context (the consent: *starts from these; may read more of <App>’s data itself*); when the brain cannot use tools for that app the editor shows the tick's line *this brain will answer without looking things up* (the result never first says so). **The consent** shows the call row only for `tools:true` — *may look up (read only — it can’t send or change anything there; it can use them without asking you each time): <connection display names from `fetchableConnections`>* — and *may call: none — it only reads data* for a tool-free ask step; the cost row *up to 10 AI calls and 8 look-ups each time — up to N AI calls a day (the daily limit is 100)*. A tools step costs `{ai: APP_AGENT_MAX_ROUND_TRIPS, net: APP_AGENT_MAX_TOOL_CALLS}` in `STEP_COST`, `dailyAiBound` and the weekly estimate. `stepWords` appends *· may look things up*. Every proposal channel (`schedule_propose`, an app's suggestion) may carry `tools:true`, never an authority; `schedule-propose.md` describes the two shapes. Typographic ’ throughout; "run" never a noun. [D4, D9, D10, D11, D12, D13, SC13, F15, S16 — "words"]
- **D-PR4-21 The vocabulary scan parses.** `scheduleCopy.test.ts`'s scan moves onto the TS-compiler walker (one shared `literalsIn` helper under `__tests__/`, which `accessCopy.test.ts` then imports), so JSX text is scanned; a pinned machine-token exemption list; the lists gain PR-4's new `schedule/` and `access/` modules and `agent/copy.dataWrite.ts`. Existing JSX text that trips it is reported by the test lane and fixed in copy, never exempted. [v1, D16 — "scan"]
- **D-PR4-22 Docs and the public text — every in-repo sentence PR-4 falsifies is edited in PR-4; only the PUSH waits.** The spec draft (`docs/spec-drafts/SPEC.md`): §24.5's own-app sentence (own writes are journaled host-internally under `authority:'approval'|'task'`, no access line), §25's items that land (the arming surface of the task authority, own-app undo), §8.1's `ownUndo:` key — a STAGED editorial revision of 1.2 recorded in `docs/spec-changelog.md` as staged, pushed with PR-5's revision (SPEC_SYNC's procedure minus the push). The whitepaper (`docs/whitepaper/src/paper.html:1601`, `:1403` — "never applies them") as edition 6, an unpublished draft, PDF rebuilt, `check-whitepaper` green. `product-vision.md:11`; `/sync-website`. ADR-0074's status + README row (amended by 0076: §5 tools and shared tables, §6 an armed task's changes apply, §8 the standing-approval deferral taken); ADR-0076's Amends line gains 0074 §6, §9 → `accepted on merge`, a "Folded from the Gate-2 reviews (PR-4)" section; ADR-0033's status ("accepted — its SHAPE gains its first arming surface in ADR-0076 §9 (a scheduled task's authority over its own app's data, through the Write Broker, not this gate); the gate's arming and its persisted store remain deferred"); ADR-0026's status (amended by 0076 §9: the host-native fetch tool resolves a multi-host connection to its one declared API host; an app's symbolic addressing is unchanged); `decisions/README.md`. The threat delta (D-PR4-23). [SC7, SC12, S15, S17 — "docs"]
- **D-PR4-23 The threat delta** `docs/security/threat-model-delta-task-write-authority.md` + the fold (v3.11): invariant rows `:285` (an imported file arms nothing — the authority clause) and `:289` (an unattended *Ask* may look things up — GET only, host-pinned, tainted after a shared read — and an armed task makes its own app's changes through the capture seat) and the §5 intro `:276-278` rewritten; R-53 amended; R-89 restated (cross-app unattended writes still never); R-71/R-72 extended to the unattended tool loop; R-75 extended (an authority rides a trusted sync); R-88 closed; new rows from R-98: fetched or shared content steering an armed own write (bounded by own scope, `rowsPerRun`, the bucket, undo, disclosure); GET-only is not read-only (S5); the taint rule's remaining channel — the reader's own rows can reach its own hosts (S6); a stop reaches another device only at its next pull, and a trusted pull can revert an unpushed local disarm (S15a); R-54's exact-substring scrub vs a transformed echo — the shape backstop (S15b); the app-authored runtime contract is the armed agent's system prompt (S15d); no per-window rate cap — `rowsPerRun` × runs a day, bounded by the AI ceiling and the 20-live bucket (S15e); the cross-file commit window (the app namespace persisted, the user file not — R-52's class, F20); a two-device double apply (R-52's class). [S15, F20 — "delta"]

### Acceptance criteria (v2; AC29–AC32 replace the Spec section's PR-4 sentences — the originals stay above for history; AC32c moved to PR-5 as AC39a)

- **AC29** `app-think` + `tools:true` → `executeAppAgent` (lazy): one `runAgentTurn` over `gatedAdapter` with `data_query` (own + lazily-recorded shared tables), the scheduled `data_propose_write`, `fetch_connection`; ≤ 8 tool calls (the 9th answers the cap sentence), ≤ 10 round trips; the AI ceiling refused IN the adapter (`CAPPED` → `capped`, nothing more sent); the net ceiling asked before each fetch; the own deadline (bound − 30 s) answers with the calls spent; a late outcome's calls are folded; the system is the `scheduledAgent` assembly (never layers 45/40); F15 → `refused` uncharged; the round-trip-cap sentence; host/subscription brain or a sidecar app → the tool-free step with `tools.unavailable`; demo or keyless BYOK → `refused` + `blockedBy:'brain'` on BOTH paths; the kit's stub → `appAgent.test.ts`, `gatedAdapter.test.ts`, `scheduleExecutors.test.ts`, `scheduleAppThink.test.ts`, `scheduleQueue.test.ts` (late fold), knowledge `scheduled-agent-prompt.test.ts`, host `kitDeadLanes.test.ts`.
- **AC30** `fetch_connection`: the input keys are EXACTLY `{connection, path, query}`; `method:'POST'`/`body` in the input reach the executor as GET with no body and the scheduled gate's refused list stays empty; one-host → symbolic; multi-host with one declared API host on the APPROVED requirement → composed and host-asserted; **negatives**: `/\t/<other approved host>/x`, `/\n/…`, `//x`, a `?`/`#`/`\` in `path`, a pending requirement's host, a host outside `allowedHosts`, a sidecar slot, a LAN-class row, a network-barred app, an unapproved row — each refused before the executor; a composed-path failure carries no host; every returned string scrubbed (an injected value echoed JSON-escaped is withheld or masked); a fetch after a shared read in the turn → the taint sentence, while fetch-then-read passes; ≤ 16,000 chars; counted before; `via:'scheduled-run'` → `fetchConnectionTool.test.ts`; the prompt pinned → knowledge `fetch-connection-prompt.test.ts`.
- **AC31** The task write authority (own data, ADR-0033's shape): the tick (off on every mount, never seeded) and the editor's control are the only writers of `writeAuthority`; offered only for a one-app task; an armed run applies at the pooling point through ONE capture seat call (`requireUndo:true`, `admit` before COMMIT) journaled to `ownUndo:<appId>:<taskId>` with the claim row's `applied` written in the same tick; fresh sequential counts are `expectChanges` and the budget; `stillArmed` evaluated immediately before the seat; an aborted run applies nothing; only `ok` steps' items are armable; held reasons recorded; disarm on: steps/owner/shorter-interval edits without consent, a version change from ANY source (proactive + the walk's belt) with the `app-updated` trace, an untrusted import (intent-equal included) with the `imported` trace, the row act, the Settings act; kept across pause and off/on but nothing applies meanwhile; **negatives**: an imported armed task never applies; no proposal channel can carry an authority; an unundoable item (function, arithmetic, `snug_kv`, credential column, > 200 rows a step) is never applied under the authority; a cross-app item never applies; a full task bucket holds `full` and nothing commits → `taskWriteAuthority.test.ts`, `applyArmed.test.ts`, `scheduleQueue.test.ts`, `userdb.test.ts` (`:467+`), db `own-undo.test.ts`, protocol `schedule.test.ts` + `own-undo.test.ts`, `enableConsent.test.tsx`, `scheduleEditor.test.tsx`, `settingsSchedule.test.tsx`, `scheduleRowStates.test.tsx`, `appVersionChanged.test.ts`.
- **AC32** The words and the facts: the chooser's hints, the *let it look things up* tick and its unavailable line, the consent's tools/cost rows, `stepWords`, `schedule-propose.md`; the result page's two sections, held sentences, the one *stop*, `stepFacts` (where / saw — closing R-88 / tools with `fetched`), `blockedBy:'brain'` → *open Settings*; the schedule's changes list; `keptOnClear`; the delete warning; `resultFacts` in the feed, the history and the notification; focus and 375 px → `scheduleEditor.test.tsx`, `enableConsent.test.tsx`, `resultDetail.test.tsx`, `scheduleChanges.test.tsx`, `resultsList.test.tsx`, `scheduleRowStates.test.tsx`, `scheduleCopy.test.ts`, `scheduleFloors.test.ts`.
- **AC32a** `capToolCalls` (shared counter, the cap sentence, pass-through by identity without a cap); `approveDataWrite` characterised BEFORE D-PR4-6 changes its path (the double-click guard, one `executeApprovedWrite` call, the outcome mapping, the meta merge) → `capToolCalls.test.ts`, `chatPersistence.test.tsx`.
- **AC32b** Own writes: the capture variant — whole-batch plannability, the own column rule, `sum ≤ maxChanges`, `admit` before COMMIT in both `requireUndo` modes, the bare `{own}` up front over 200 rows, every refusal by name → db `brokered-write.test.ts`, `driver.test.ts` (new describe; `:696` unchanged); `applyOwnWrite`/`undoOwnWrite` (room before the seat, the approval bucket's eviction, `full` for a task, `stillArmed` after the last await, live re-validation on undo, the credential-named column refused, `undone` with no access line, no SQL in the entry); an approval journals `authority:'approval'` with `via`; the `inTransaction`-first order; the ring + cache invalidation; the trust belt → `writeBroker.test.ts` (`:360-411` INVERTED under the invert table — the seat input, the journal, the rings), `doorbell.test.ts`, `chatPersistence.test.tsx`, `dataWriteCard.test.tsx`.
- **AC32d** The scan parses JSX text over `schedule/`, `views/Schedule*`, `access/refresh.ts` and `agent/copy.dataWrite.ts`; the new modules listed → `scheduleCopy.test.ts`.
- **AC32e** Docs (Gate 6) — D-PR4-22 + D-PR4-23 + KB 85 (one sentence at `:21` *a schedule may make changes on its own — only the user turns that on, for its own app's data, with an undo*; one bullet under *What the Host Shows*; no new heading; pinned in `scheduled-runs-kb.test.ts`; `gen:content`) + architecture (`:843`, `:867`), code-map (`:78`, `:113`, `:115`, `:29`), glossary (*make changes on its own* / write authority, *own undo*, `:88`, `:112`, `:113` amended) + next-steps (PR-3's landed riders pruned; the 2026-10-09 "standing approvals for unattended writes" item NARROWED — DB writes of a task's own app are armable; a scheduled mutating connected call is still refused and still its own ADR; the D-PR4-1 moves; the two stale closures; Q4's armed half) + lessons; the kit measured (`check-host-kit`) and recorded.

### Module contracts (exact names the lanes share)

- **protocol** `own-undo.ts` (internal): `OWN_WRITE_AUTHORITIES`, `OwnWriteAuthority`, `OWN_UNDO_VIAS`, `ownUndoEntrySchema`, `OwnUndoEntry`, `OWN_UNDO_MAX_LIVE = 20`, `OWN_UNDO_ROW_MAX_BYTES = 256 * 1024`; `access.ts` exports `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_STEPS` and the extracted per-cell walk; `schedule.ts`: `app-think` + `tools?: true`; task + `writeAuthority?`, `disarmed?`; run + `applied?`; item + `held?` (`SCHEDULE_HELD_REASONS = ['busy','changed','cannot-undo','full','failed','over-limit','stopped']`); step result + `inOpenApp?`, `saw?`, `tools?`, `blockedBy?`; `APP_AGENT_MAX_TOOL_CALLS = 8`, `APP_AGENT_MAX_ROUND_TRIPS = 10`, `FETCH_CONNECTION_MAX_CHARS = 16_000`, `APP_AGENT_DEADLINE_MARGIN_MS = 30_000`; the `:313` comment rewritten; `index.ts` exports. `json-schemas.ts` SOURCES unchanged (pinned).
- **db** `brokered-write.ts`: the capture variant of `BrokeredApplyInput`, `ownCapturable`; `driver.ts` forwards it; `userdb/own-undo.ts`: `listOwnUndo(appId, {taskId?} | {bucket:'approval'})`, `putOwnUndo(appId, bucket, entry) → 'ok' | 'full'` (the approval bucket evicts), `markOwnUndone(appId, bucket, id, at)`, `ownUndoRoom(appId, bucket)`; `app-settings-keys.ts` `ownUndoKey(appId, bucket)`; `deleteApp` / `deleteScheduledTask` sweeps; the untrusted import drops `ownUndo:*`; `schedules.ts` reconcile (strip + trace + `disarmedTasks`), `hasScheduleRunSeatsToStrip`, `scrubScheduleRunRows` strips `applied`, `keptOnClear`.
- **playground engine** `access/writeBroker.ts`: `applyOwnWrite(db, {appId, bucket, statements, params, expectChanges, maxChanges, requireUndo, authority, via, runId?, taskId?, stillArmed?}) → {ok:true, changes, undoId?, undoable} | {ok:false, reason: 'busy' | 'changed' | 'cannot-undo' | 'full' | 'failed' | 'stopped'}`, `undoOwnWrite(db, appId, bucket, undoId, {via:'user'}) → UndoOutcome`, `applyApprovedWrite` → capture (D-PR4-6); `agent/dataTools.ts` `capToolCalls`, the `scheduled` option, `executeApprovedWrite(db, proposal, {via})`; `agent/fetchConnectionTool.ts` `buildFetchConnectionTool({appId, getDb, onNetCall?, signal?, fetchImpl?, taint})`, `fetchableConnections(db, appId)`; `agent/providerTools.ts` `renderProviderResult(result, {scrub?, maxChars?})`; `agent/gatedAdapter.ts`; `schedule/appAgent.ts` `executeAppAgent`; `schedule/proposalGate.ts`; `schedule/applyArmed.ts` `applyArmed(db, run, task, items, {now})`; `schedule/executors.ts` (gates, lazy dispatch, `finalizeOutcome`); `schedule/queue.ts` (the walk at pooling, late fold, `fitRunRow`/M17); `schedule/acts.ts` (`armTask`, `disarmTask`, `disarmAllTasks`, `updateTask`'s widening rules); `schedule/appThink.ts` (the keyless gate, `saw`, `RESPONSE_SCHEMA` by armed state, the gate helper); `schedule/appRun.ts` (`inOpenApp`); `state/appVersionChanged.ts` (disarm every source); `schedule/floors.ts` + `editorModel.ts` (costs — the UI lane).
- **playground UI** `EnableConsent.tsx`, `ScheduleEditor.tsx`/`StepsEditor.tsx`/`editorModel.ts`, `ScheduleSettingsCard.tsx`, `ScheduleRow.tsx` (chip, act, attention), `ResultDetail.tsx`, the schedule's changes list (in the history view), `ResultsList.tsx`, `views/ChatLog.tsx` + `agent/copy.dataWrite.ts`, `agent/useBuilderChat.ts` (`approveDataWrite` → `{via:'chat'}`, the belt, the card's undo), `schedule/copy*.ts`, `access/copy.ts` (`OWN_UNDO`).
- **knowledge** `prompts/tools/fetch-connection.md`, `prompts/system/<nn>-scheduled-agent.md` + the `scheduledAgent` assembly branch, amended `schedule-propose.md`, KB 85; `ToolPromptName` + the system layer name; `gen:content`.

- **Test lanes (Opus 5.5 high; failing first; disjoint files; one workflow):** **T-A host-native jobs** — `capToolCalls.test.ts`, `gatedAdapter.test.ts`, `fetchConnectionTool.test.ts`, `appAgent.test.ts`, `dataToolsScheduled.test.ts`, `proposalGate.test.ts`, `scheduleExecutors.test.ts`, `scheduleAppThink.test.ts`, knowledge `fetch-connection-prompt.test.ts` + `scheduled-agent-prompt.test.ts`, host `kitDeadLanes.test.ts`. **T-B authority + own writes** — protocol `schedule.test.ts` + `own-undo.test.ts`, db `brokered-write.test.ts` + `driver.test.ts` + `own-undo.test.ts` + `userdb.test.ts` (reconcile, strip predicate, sweeps), playground `writeBroker.test.ts` (+ the invert table), `doorbell.test.ts`, `applyArmed.test.ts`, `taskWriteAuthority.test.ts` (acts, widening, version disarm), `appVersionChanged.test.ts`, `scheduleQueue.test.ts`, `chatPersistence.test.tsx` (AC32a's approve rows + the belt). **T-C UI + words + scan** — `enableConsent.test.tsx`, `scheduleEditor.test.tsx`, `settingsSchedule.test.tsx`, `scheduleRowStates.test.tsx`, `resultDetail.test.tsx`, `scheduleChanges.test.tsx`, `resultsList.test.tsx`, `dataWriteCard.test.tsx`, `scheduleCard.test.tsx`, `scheduleFloors.test.ts`, `scheduleCopy.test.ts` (the parse upgrade + every new sentence), `accessCopy.test.ts` (`OWN_UNDO` + the shared `literalsIn` import), knowledge `scheduled-runs-kb.test.ts` (KB 85's sentence). A lane that must touch a file outside its list reports it as a deviation and moves only the pin; a question it cannot answer from this contract becomes a numbered v2.x note BEFORE the implementation lanes start.
- **Implementation lanes (Opus 5.5 xhigh):** **I-1 protocol + db** (every internal shape, the capture variant, `ownCapturable`, the own journal, the reconcile, the sweeps, the strip) AND the shared playground TYPE surface as signatures with throwing bodies (`applyOwnWrite`, `undoOwnWrite`, `executeApprovedWrite`'s `{via}` + outcome, `capToolCalls`, the `scheduled` option's type) → then in parallel **I-2a writes** (`writeBroker.ts`, `applyArmed.ts`, `queue.ts`, `acts.ts`, `appVersionChanged.ts`, `proposalGate.ts`) ∥ **I-2b agent** (`dataTools.ts`, `gatedAdapter.ts`, `fetchConnectionTool.ts`, `providerTools.ts`, `appAgent.ts`, `executors.ts`, `appThink.ts`, `appRun.ts`, ALL of `packages/knowledge` incl. KB 85 and `gen:content`, the kit stub in `apps/host`) ∥ **I-4 UI** (every surface above, `schedule/copy*.ts`, `access/copy.ts`, `agent/copy.dataWrite.ts`, `floors.ts`, `editorModel.ts`, `useBuilderChat.ts`, `ChatLog.tsx`). Shared-file rule: a lane names another lane's sentence or prompt by KEY and the owner writes it; a literal dynamic import of a sibling's not-yet-landed module is avoided (lesson 2026-10-11). Then the kit measured, the Opus high verification lane, the three-part Gate-5 review, the fold, the docs lane (D-PR4-22/23 + AC32e's docs/), Gate 6.

### PR-5 additions (the moves of D-PR4-1 — written into the Spec section's PR-5 block as AC39a–AC39e)

AC39a the chat's cross-app write (D-PR3-16 reversed) with this review's inputs: S4 (a separate `meta.accessWrite` key; an invalid `target` drops the whole card; `applyApprovedWrite` refuses a `target`; the untrusted import drops unresolved ones), F6 (a host-only `service.writeApproved` answering `ExecuteApprovedWriteOutcome` with a pre-seat `drifted`), D14 (the host line first; *open Ledger’s access* deep link `?access=history`; `failedWhy`), D15 (`userTitle(readerName, wantsWrite)`; the *— not change them* tail), D17 (`CHAT_DOOR.changes`, `oneChange`, `readOnlyTable`, the prompt sentence), v1's D-PR4-12/13 (the DDL mark for the chat caller only, the one-app rule, the rename's home re-decided — the Worker plan per D-PR3-16 or `renameDmlTarget` on raw text — `planWrite` after `recordRead` with `inTransaction` first, `access_propose` + `access`). AC39b *fetch new data first* — the chat's (a user act, never a brain tool) and the scheduler's (`refreshFirst` on the step; the refresh engine counting the run it sits in). AC39c the ⋈ *fetch now*, with the decisions on SPEC MUST 17 and a grant-less `refreshed` line. AC39d the refresh chip's *cancel* (the engine's abort seam). AC39e the owner's ONE push of the staged spec revision (PR-4's + PR-5's) and whitepaper edition 6.

### Contract v2.1 notes — decided after the three test lanes (2026-10-11; binding for the implementation lanes; every lane question answered — the lane's proposed answer is TAKEN unless a note below says otherwise)

The lanes' 60 questions (T-A Q-T-A-1…15, T-B Q1…Q21, T-C TC-Q1…Q24) are answered by their proposals, which are the binding API sketch: `executeAppAgent(step, ctx)` over the mocked `createTurnAdapter` seam and `accessService()` (T-A-1); `gatedAdapter(inner, {db, spent?, soFar, now})` with ONE mutable `soFar` shared with the fetch tool, counting only on delegation, `CAPPED` uncharged (T-A-2); `gateProposal(db, appId, {sql, summary?}) → {ok, item} | {ok:false, reason: 'invalid'|'credential'|'busy'|'failed'}` (T-A-3); `scheduled: {armed, onProposal(item)}` replacing the chat `onProposal` when present, one statement, no params, ≤ 3 staged per tool set, every *Proposed —* answer carrying D8 (T-A-4); `buildFetchConnectionTool(...)` → one tool + `FETCH_CONNECTION_TOOL_NAME`, `fetchableConnections` entries carry `slot` (T-A-5); `StepContext.boundAt?: number` (epoch ms of the run's bound) for the agent's deadline (T-A-6); the StepOutcome seats and `saw = {source display name, the source's own table}` (T-A-7); `keylessBrainGate` + `KEYLESS_BRAIN_REFUSAL` exported from `appThink.ts`, both gates applied by the PRODUCTION composition (`executeStep`) through an optional `StepExecutorDeps.brainRouteFor` — the direct `executeAppThink` keeps today's behaviour (T-A-8); armed ≡ `ctx.task.writeAuthority !== undefined` (T-A-9); `scheduledAgent` wins over the other assembly options (T-A-10); the agent's first message = the prompt + the typed SELECTs' rows in one `<query_result>` (T-A-11); the busy sentence names the asked app (T-A-12); the cap sentence's number is `tools.used` (T-A-13); the kit stub rejects with `noDataLane('appAgent.executeAppAgent')` (T-A-14); a capped agent step is `capped: true`, status `refused` (T-A-15). T-B: the capture seat refuses an unplannable batch by name under both modes and the ENGINE re-calls the bare `{own}` for an approval (Q1); `ApplyRefusal` gains `'cannot-undo'`, rules `column`/`table`/the parser's, never the words "another app"/"shared" (Q2); a bucket is the string `'approval'` or a `taskId`, `ownUndoKey(appId, bucket)` (Q3); `listOwnUndo` answers LIVE entries newest first, never throws (Q4); `ownUndoRoom → boolean` (Q5); `putOwnUndo` throws `UserDbError` on a strict-parse failure, the approval bucket evicts its oldest LIVE on the count rule and undone-then-oldest on bytes (Q6); `applyApprovedWrite(db, proposal, {via})` → `{ok:true, executed, undoable, undoId?}`, busy → `{ok:false, reason:'failed', message ∋ 'middle of a change'}` before any `scratchRun` (Q7); `applyOwnWrite`'s `changes: number[]` (Q8); `applyArmed` receives only armable items, returns `{applied?, items}` and rewrites the claim row; `stillArmed` re-reads the task FRESH (Q9); every item applied → `proposals` omitted (Q10); the seat bounds (Q11); `armTask(taskId)` / `disarmTask(taskId)` / `disarmAllTasks() → number`, the consent path = `updateTask` then `armTask` (Q13); `TaskPatch.ownerAppId?` (Q14); "shorter minimum interval" = the minimum gap between occurrences (Q15); `report.schedules.disarmedTasks` always present, `onAppVersionChanged`'s outcome unchanged (Q16); export AND import strip the new run seats (Q17); `keptOnClear` + the per-task sweep (Q18); `undoOwnWrite`'s failures through `ACCESS_SHEET.undoFailed` (Q19); a late outcome folds into the day's counters only (Q20); `metaToDataWrite`'s drops (Q21). T-C: `EnableConsent` props `presetOnItsOwn?`, `connectionsByApp?`, `onEnable({onItsOwn})` and the test ids (TC-Q1); the three consent call-row inversions are D-PR4-20 inversions, kept as written (TC-Q2); the tools row and the cost row (TC-Q3/4); the editor's ids and `STEPS.kindHint(kind, appName)` (TC-Q6); the brain line whenever `chatDoorOpen(chatBrainRoute(db, appId))` is false (TC-Q7); the query fields stay visible under the tools line (TC-Q8); the Settings act's row and its button, no confirm (TC-Q10); the chip and the attention precedence paused > disarmed > imported > needs-you (TC-Q11); the result page's ids and the history link (TC-Q14); the in-session settled line stays beside the persisted entry (TC-Q16); the `stepFacts`/`resultFacts` strings (TC-Q17/18); the card's `dataWriteUndoable`, `onUndoDataWrite`, `appName` props (TC-Q19); `OWN_UNDO.arm`'s singular (TC-Q20); the changes list in the history (TC-Q21); a needed machine token goes through a protocol constant (TC-Q22); KB 85's two pins (TC-Q24).

Notes that DECIDE or CHANGE something:

- **N1 — the user's approves get their own seat (TC-Q15).** `applied` stays the TASK's one group (T-B's object pins hold). The run gains `approved?: [{appId, undoId?, undoable?: false, summary?, changes}] ≤ 3` (omit-default; one entry per user-approved item; no SQL) — so a walk that applied a prefix and a later user approve of a held item sit on one run. `fitRunRow` and the M17 row keep it with `applied`; `hasScheduleRunSeatsToStrip` matches `"approved"` too; the strip, `keptOnClear` (a live undo) and `resultFacts` cover it. The result page's *you applied this — 2 rows changed · undo* reads from `approved`. The orchestrator moved `resultDetail.test.tsx`'s PR-4 approve row to `approved` (the cross-lane pin).
- **N2 — the import report widens.** `userdb.test.ts:478`'s exact `report.schedules` pin gained `disarmedTasks: 0` (orchestrator; a widening, not a weakening).
- **N3 — the `disarmed` trace clears on ANY save through `updateTask`** (the user reviewed it — TC-Q12) and on `armTask` (T-B Q12); the disarms the user performs write no trace.
- **N4 — "one app" (TC-Q5 over T-B Q13's wording):** the tick is offered — and `armTask` admits — iff at least one `app-think` step exists AND every non-notify step names the same app; `armedVersion` is that app's current version.
- **N5 — the missing pin (TC-Q23):** I-2a writes, FIRST, the `appDrift.test.ts` pin for `updatePausesSentence(titles, {armed: true})` (the tail *and stops it making changes on its own* before *until you turn it back on…*); `keptOnClear` is T-B's (done).
- **N6 — build order:** the playground resolves `@snugprotocol/*` to `dist` — I-1 rebuilds protocol and db (and I-2b rebuilds knowledge after `gen:content`) before the playground suites can see the new exports.
