### Contract v2.1 notes — decided after the four test lanes (2026-10-12; binding for the implementation lanes; every lane question answered — the lane's proposed answer is TAKEN unless a note below says otherwise)

The lanes' 71 questions (T-A TA-Q1…Q14, T-B TB-Q1…Q23, T-C TC-Q1…Q16, T-D Q1…Q18) are answered by their proposals, which are the binding API sketch. **db + protocol (T-A):** `findHeadMeta(html, name)` returns the DECODED content of exactly one matching head meta (attribute order free; the name value and the stop tokens ASCII-case-insensitive; `&apos` without `;` is a bare `&`; the unit cap inclusive) (TA-Q5); `renameDmlTarget(sql, from, to) → {ok: true, sql} | {ok: false, rule}` replaces ONLY the IDENT span by `"to"` (inner `"` doubled), keeping the keywords' spelling and all whitespace; `qualified` is checked BEFORE the double re-parse; non-ASCII inside a string literal is admitted (TA-Q6, TC-Q6); `readAppProvenance(undefined) → undefined`, a valid row → `{imported: true, at}`, any other present value → `{imported: true}` (TA-Q7); `carryAccessHistory(target = the WINNER, snapshot = the LOSER, {since})` — `since` strict (`at > since`), `carried` counts lines ADDED, a natural-key match merges (max count/rows, the winner's other fields) uncounted, `dropped` counts candidates not added; `applyTombstones(target, revoked)` takes `snapshotLocalAccessGrants(sql).revoked` whose values become the FULL revoked grant (TA-Q8); `report.history` appears ONLY when `carryHistorySince` is passed (null counts) and tombstones apply only then — the recovery restore keeps the pulled row exactly as today (TA-Q9); `carryFromImage` answers `{carried: 0, restopped: 0}` for any image it cannot open (TA-Q10); `SyncableUserDb.carryFromImage(bytes, {since})` REQUIRED; `importUserDb` options `{trustedOrigin?, carryHistorySince?}` (TA-Q11); a card is unresolved when a `schedule` card has no `resolution` key and a `dataWrite`/`accessWrite` card no `outcome` key — a RESOLVED schedule card now STAYS on an untrusted import (today's LIKE dropped it — a stated behaviour change) (TA-Q12); `FETCHED_OUTCOMES = ['fresh','recent','failed','skipped','none']`, `fetched` 1..8, `refreshFirst` inside `canonicalScheduleIntent`, the shared proposal schema still PARSES it and each channel strips it (TA-Q13); the index exports `extractAccessWants`, `findHeadMeta`, `ACCESS_WANTS_SCAN_MAX_UNITS`, `appProvenanceKey`, `appIdFromAppProvenanceKey`, `readAppProvenance`, `renameDmlTarget`, `SYNC_UNANCHORED_HASH` (TA-Q14). **Discovery UI (T-B):** `STRIP_OUTCOME.allowed(reader, source, tables, duration, write = [], opts?: {notChange?})` (TB-Q2, TC-Q14); `ACCESS_WANTS.preview(appName, names, more)` / `forWhat(appName)` (TB-Q3); the test ids `access-pairs`, `access-pair-row`, `access-pair-review`, `access-pair`, `access-act-line`, `tile-could-read`, `access-wants-note` (TB-Q5); `registryTables(schema)` in `relevance.ts` (TB-Q6); `rankPairs({readers, sources})` returns EVERY admitted pair, Settings renders the first `PAIRS_MAX` (TB-Q7/Q8); `startUserAsk` writes the session's not-offered memory, cleared on `bumpLibraryRevision()` (TB-Q9); PINNED = candidates offer only that source and `preselect.appId = source` (TB-Q10); a gone source → `{kind: 'not-offered', reason: 'gone'}` (TB-Q11); `UserAskAnswer = 'opened' | 'answer-other-first' | 'no-app' | {kind: 'not-offered', reason} | {kind: 'answer-other-first'}` — the bare form keeps its string (TB-Q12); `readerProvenanceKind(app, {imported?})` / `provenanceLine(app, {…, imported?})` (TB-Q13); `AccessWantsNote` props `{appName, html}` (TB-Q14); the pending's `askedIn?: 'chat' | 'declared'`, `offeredBy?: 'source'` (TB-Q15); pinned with no table-tier match → `preselect {appId: source, tables: []}` (TB-Q16); `CHAT_FETCH.first(names)` joins two with *and* (TB-Q17, T-D Q11); the widened ⋈ needs no tables on the other app (TB-Q20); a not-offered pair act keeps the sheet up with its act line (TB-Q21); `accessCountsByApp` in `grants.ts`, absent ≡ `{0, 0}` (TB-Q23). **The chat's write (T-C):** writable-first is per GRANT; a read-only table of a write grant and `<alias>__snug_kv` answer `readOnlyTable(source, table)` (TC-Q1); the CHAT_DOOR keys' shapes and `Error: <sentence>` framing (TC-Q2); `heading(…, writable?)` / `tableLine(…, write?)` / `renderSharedDdl = [tables, closing].filter(Boolean).join('\n\n')` (TC-Q3); `SharedTablesSeat.recordRead(grantIds, sql, {counting?})`, `planWrite(grantId, sql)`, the counting line's table = `dmlTargetTable` of the renamed statement (TC-Q4); `BuildDataToolsOptions.onAccessWriteProposal?(card) → boolean | void`, `grantedAt` the live grant's (TC-Q5); the brain's answer (TC-Q7); `writeChatApproved` shapes, a plan of 0 at apply → `refused` (TC-Q8); `brokeredWriteCore` options and tokens (TC-Q9); `approveCrossAppWrite(db, card, {threadId}) → AccessWriteCardState` — `busy`/`waiting` set `failedWhy` with NO `outcome` (TC-Q10); the card's props and DOM (TC-Q11); `approveAccessWrite`/`declineAccessWrite`, `ChatMessage.accessWrite` (TC-Q12); the prompt sentences (TC-Q15); the kit header *ten modules are swapped by resolved path* (TC-Q16). **Fetch (T-D):** `refreshRuns`' full surface (`beginRefreshRun`, `setRefreshPlacement`, `joinRefreshRun → leave`, `endRefreshRun`, `listRefreshRuns`, `cancelRefresh → boolean`, `abortAllRefreshes('reset')`, `abortRefreshesFor`, `useRefreshRuns`) (Q1); the entry's `requestedBy` is the REQUESTING app's id (Q2); a 4th argument to `refreshSource` marks a host door (Q3); `service.refreshFor(caller, grantId, {deps, signal, run?}) → {outcome, calls}`, policy first (Q4); the outcome mapping (Q5); `when` already worded (Q6); `DelegatedRun.cancelled: true` (Q7); `EnableConsent.fetchFirstByApp` (Q8); the editor's ids (Q10); `ChatFetchToggle {appId, pressed, onPress, disabled?}` (Q11); `send(text, wire?, {fetchFirst: true, onConsumed?})` (Q12); `ChatMessage.fetchNote` persisted as `meta.fetchNote` on the user's row (Q13); the dialog reads the touched record's posture from `runPlacement`, the queue tag unchanged (Q14); `StepOutcome.fetched`, `none` for an empty `asked` (Q15); the chip's DOM and the 830 px rule in `theme/schedule-bits.css` (Q16 — I-2c owns that file); `stepCost` + `REFRESH_FIRST_MAX_SOURCES × stepCost(app-run)` (Q17); a cancel's line written before the waiters' answer (Q18).

Notes that DECIDE or CHANGE something:

- **N1 — KB 87's example (TA-Q4 vs T-B):** the purpose is *to show spending by category* (T-B's pin; T-A's row parses whatever KB 87 carries): `<meta name="snug:access-wants" content='[{"purpose":"to show spending by category","hints":{"tables":["transactions","accounts"],"words":["spending","budget"]}}]'>` in a ```html fence, right after the viewport meta, before any script.
- **N2 — `userdb.test.ts:1546` (TA-Q1):** its TRUSTED wants line INVERTED in place by the orchestrator under D-PR5-2 (a trusted pull re-derives; READER's HTML declares nothing → no row). The S9 inversion (an unresolved own `dataWrite` card dropped by an untrusted import) is CONFIRMED (D-PR5-10's rider).
- **N3 — the lone-surrogate rule (TA-Q2):** D-PR5-2's `JSON.stringify(value).isWellFormed()` cannot catch one (stringify re-escapes it); the rule is: EVERY parsed string, keys and values, satisfies `String.prototype.isWellFormed()`.
- **N4 — a BLOB html cell (TA-Q3):** the reader admits only `typeof(html) = 'text'` (`CASE WHEN typeof(v.html) = 'text' THEN substr(v.html, 1, 65536) END`); AC33's "a BLOB cell derives nothing" wins over v2's `CAST`.
- **N5 — `wontAskAgain` (TB-Q1):** today's bytes KEPT ("Budget won't ask this again — allow it any time from Budget's access"); the act line uses the key.
- **N6 — the *ended* words (TC-Q13):** typographic, like the rest of `agent/copy.dataWrite.ts` — *Budget’s access to Ledger ended — nothing was changed; allow it again from Budget’s access* (the orchestrator moved that one literal in `chatCrossAppCard.test.tsx`).
- **N7 — `EnableConsent` is not a setter of `refreshFirst` (T-D Q9):** the editor's tick is its only setter; the consent shows the row for a step that carries it. D-PR5-19's "or `EnableConsent`" is struck.
- **N8 — the sanctioned in-place test edits beyond v2's list (each marked in the file):** `runningChip.test.tsx` — the WHOLE PR-3 tests at `:294` and `:312` (the chip drawn from the delegated record); `accessProposeTool.test.ts:77` (the input keys gain `access`); `access-doors-prompts.test.ts` (the PR-2 data-propose-write sentence → the amended two); the KB 87 heading-tree pin AMENDED (the new heading filtered, every old one still pinned in order); `runHeaderIcons.test.tsx`'s "a pending ask alone" row moved to a one-app file (fixture only).
- **N9 — the kit stub drift (T-C):** `apps/host/src/stubs/accessCard.ts` lacks `resolution.write` since PR-3 — I-2b aligns it with the real type while adding `access` (the new type-mirror row fails `tsc` until then).
- **N10 — build order:** the playground resolves `@snugprotocol/*` to `dist` — I-1 rebuilds protocol and db before the parallel lanes; I-2b rebuilds knowledge after `gen:content`; no lane builds while another lane's tests run (one shared tree).

## Decisions & surprises

- **D1 (2026-10-10)** The owner's framing "rather than letting app A directly read from app B" describes a design that never existed: the host has always brokered the read. The plan therefore does not move the broker; it gives the broker two more doors (chat, scheduler) and three more verbs (write, refresh, discover). Said plainly in the Spec so the fresh session does not look for app-to-app code to remove.
- **D2** "Without instantiating the app" is literal only for host-native work (read, think, fetch, bounded write — PR-2/PR-4). App-authored handlers need the app's runtime; the fix for the owner's complaint is ONE instance with placement and handover (PR-1), not the end of the headless frame.
- **D3** PR-2 cannot record `via` or `provenance: 'chat'` without a strict-record change; it records today's shapes and PR-3 adds the fields. The history line for a chat read therefore reads like an app read for the life of PR-2 alone — acceptable because PR-3 follows and the sheet's own copy says where the ask came from. *(PR-2 Gate 2: any `via` or `provenance: 'chat'` key written in PR-2 is a strict-parse REFUSAL — `appendAccessLog` throws and the read is withheld — so none is ever written.)*
- **D4** The refresh cooldown is in-memory per host (a cost control), not a settings row (a security record would be). Two hosts on one file can each refresh once per window — bounded to one extra run, counted on each.
- **D5** `provenance`'s strict enum, the log entry's `via`, the grant's new fields and the undo record are the ONLY reasons PR-3 touches records; every behaviour that could ship off-wire was moved to PR-2/PR-4/PR-5 so the wire PR stays as small as a 1.2 can be.
- **D6** The kit page had 26 KiB of headroom after PR #198; PR-3 adds a write planner to the Worker and a schema or two. The plan names the ceiling conversation as a possible STOP, not a silent bump.
- **D7** Sidecar-fact sources stay deferred (Q17) with the taint design named; the owner can flip it with one word and PR-5 absorbs it (the egress note and a connected-call refusal while the grant is live).

### PR-1 — Gate 4 decisions & surprises (2026-10-10, from the implementation lane's report; each verified against the contract)

- **D-PR1-12 `DelegatedRun.grantedHost?: string`** (additive): `closedAfterChange(appName, host)` needs the host of the allowed call; the gate records it on a grant.
- **D-PR1-13 `DispatchEnd` gains `{ kind: 'blocked'; message }`**: an app whose html vanished mid-run keeps today's `blocked` (`appMissing`).
- **D-PR1-14 `Dispatch` carries `spent` and `afterHandover`**: the hidden path's ceiling questions count the step's earlier attempt (summed tallies); `afterHandover` drives the announce bound.
- **D-PR1-15 The D-PR1-6 pre-check runs before each LIVE dispatch only** (first or after a handover), never before the hidden path — the kept pin `appRunHandshake.test.tsx:658` requires a day at the net ceiling to still mount the hidden frame and refuse per call, and D-PR1-6's own title says "before a delegated dispatch".
- **D-PR1-16 A re-dispatch's announce wait is bounded by the deadline alone**; the first dispatch keeps `min(announceTimeoutMs, remaining)`.
- **D-PR1-17 `outcomeOf` reads the refusals of the attempt that ENDED the step** (the cut attempt's record belongs to an abandoned handler run); tallies are still summed across attempts.
- **D-PR1-18 The gate records nothing when the run's own signal withdraws a parked ask** (the record was already returned to the executor); D-PR1-9 names only the withdrawal.
- **D-PR1-19 `state/net.ts`'s belt learns the retracted generation from a `delegatedRunStore` subscription**, because the registry has dropped its entry by the time a retraction is announced; the posture also dies structurally through the v2.1 token match.
- **D-PR1-20 `list`/`release` keep the frame's own posture during a delegated run** — the contract closes `request`/`query` only; said in the handler's header. (The security diff review is asked whether that is safe.)
- **Surprise S-PR1-1:** `appRuntime.test.tsx:120` pinned the attended transport by IDENTITY (`toBe(probe)`); the contract wraps it. Routed to the test lane as a behavioural pin (a send reaches the probe; a delegated run counts).
- **Surprise S-PR1-2:** `resultDetail.test.tsx:439/:459` pin the old needs-you card text; the `needsYouTitle` card change waits for the test lane's updated pins (tests first), then lands.
- **Surprise S-PR1-3:** `appRunHandshake.test.tsx:893` was fragile on `announceTimeoutMs: 40` vs `vi.waitFor`'s 50 ms poll — hardened by the test lane with `announceTimeoutMs: 2_000`.
- **Measured:** playground 3643/3644 (the one red is S-PR1-1), host 681/681, desktop 231/231, e2e `schedule-flow.spec.ts` 5/5 (36.7 s, Node 22, port 8797), `check-threat-model` green (ledger `1031a017416a`), kit page 2,733,171 B (16,829 B under the ceiling). Diff: 23 files, +1,355 / −301.

### PR-1 — Gate 5 fold (2026-10-10; two fresh-context diff reviews on Fable xhigh — security 7 findings, maintainability 9; no blocker; both "merge with the folds listed"). Every fold below is taken unless marked otherwise; the code goes to one Fable xhigh fold lane, the pins to one Opus lane, the docs to the orchestrator.

- **F-1 (security MAJOR) The open-url seat closes during a delegated run.** `state/openUrl.ts` `createOpenUrlHandlerFor(appId).open` answers `'declined'` while `delegatedRunFor(appId) !== undefined` — hidden-frame parity (a hidden frame composes no open-url at all). Pin in the open-url suite. Delta: the "ONLY runtime difference" sentence and R-82 name the seat.
- **F-2 (maintainability MAJOR) `docs/code-map.md:113-114`:** the row's tail still says "opening the app does not interrupt it", "the gate follows the trigger", "an unattended run uses its own hidden frame whether or not the app is open", "the key cleared on every exit", and `:113` "dispatched to the hidden-frame seams" — rewritten to the ADR-0077 model (orchestrator).
- **F-3 (both MINOR) Retraction during the live kv write hands over** instead of `failed 'the open app could not be reached'`: after `await kvSet`, re-check the retraction before the hint — unattended → `handover → hidden`, manual → `APP_CLOSED_SUMMARY`; the hidden twin re-checks the registration before `host.notifyEvent` so a hidden handler never starts after its handover is decided. Pin both.
- **F-4 (security MINOR) The dialog resolves ITS OWN entry**, never the store head: `NetConfirmDialog` calls `pending.resolve(decision)` (withdrawals now move the head without a user act). Pin: a head that moved under the click never receives the decision.
- **F-5 (security MINOR) The app name is delimited in the scheduled dialog's body** — `“<appName>”`, like the schedule title — in `body` and `afterRunBody`. Pin.
- **F-6 (security MINOR) After a remount mid-run the new generation inherits the sticky posture:** `endDelegatedRun` refreshes the touched generation to the LIVE one when the registration token still matches (the instance that could have read the kv input is the one that stays ask-only). Pin: remount during a run → after the run, a POST from the new generation parks an `afterRun` ask. R-84 gains the sentence.
- **F-7 (security MINOR) Never hand over after the user ANSWERED** — a grant (already) or a DECISIVE refusal: on retraction with a decisive refusal the attempt ends `failed APP_CLOSED_SUMMARY` and `outcomeOf`'s record-first rule reads the refusal (`refused`, the user's own sentence) — the hidden re-dispatch would otherwise re-run the handler and overwrite "you said don't send" with the generic line. Pin.
- **F-8 (security MINOR) `HIDDEN_UNMOUNT_WAIT_MS` is bounded by the remaining deadline** (`Math.min`). No pin needed beyond D-PR1-10's.
- **F-9 (maintainability MINOR) StrictMode's simulated cleanup must not fire `onUnmounted`:** `ScheduledRunHost`'s cleanup calls `owner.onUnmounted()` only when `hiddenMountStore.get() !== owner`. Pin: a cleanup re-run while the store still holds the mount fires nothing; a clear fires once.
- **F-10 (maintainability MINOR) `list` filters on `present()`** so it never advertises a grant `query` will refuse during the run. Pin.
- **F-11 (maintainability MINOR) Two sentences move into `copy.ts`:** `runPlacement.ts`'s "<app> is already running another schedule" and `appRun.ts`'s "the open app could not be reached" (`alreadyRunning(appName)`, `APP_UNREACHABLE_SUMMARY`), pinned by `scheduleCopy`.
- **F-12 (maintainability MINOR) The sticky belt has ONE home:** the `subscribeAppHosts` listener that calls `clearTouchedGeneration` moves from `state/net.ts` into `schedule/runPlacement.ts` (which already imports `state/appHosts.ts`); `net.ts`'s `generationTouchedByRun` map is deleted. The token match stays the structural rule.
- **F-13 (maintainability MINOR) One refusal type:** `ScheduledConfirmGate.refused` is typed `ScheduledRefusal`-compatible (`why?`), `RefusalRecord` deleted.
- **F-14 (maintainability MINOR) The e2e spec header says Node 22** (`better-sqlite3` fails under Node 24) and `SNUG_SERVER_PORT=8797`.
- **Declined:** maintainability MINOR-8 (keep `StepContext.interrupt`, honestly commented); security F-5's alternative "clear the kv per attempt" (the handshake pins "written per dispatch, cleared once" and F-6 closes the hole without it).
- **Verified clean by the reviewers (no fold):** the gate bypass traces (a)–(h), the confirm queue, the access door (`request`/`query`), handover ordering, counting and the pre-check, the registry, C1 surfaces, the manual and hidden paths, the threat docs line-for-line except R-82/R-84 (folded above).

### PR-2 — Gate 4 decisions & surprises (2026-10-11, from the four implementation lanes' reports; each verified against contract v2 + v2.1; the PR-1 twin is the model)

- **D1-1** `scopeCopy` takes the OPENED handle (steps 2–5); each entry point opens and closes in its own try/finally (the throwaway-copy pins count one open and one close). **D1-2** The dump's set budget re-weighs the kept rows (`jsonUtf8Weight`) — the runner answers no kept weight; bounded by the 2 MiB per-table cap. **D1-3** The dump's `columns` is the scope's own list; `types` looked up by exact name from `table_xinfo` after the drift check passed. **D1-4** An attach collision is checked against ANY `sqlite_master` object (lower-cased) and earlier attaches; any CREATE/INSERT failure throws `SCRATCH_UNAVAILABLE` naming the table, before any statement. **D1-5** `scratchRun`'s empty-batch early return stays (no copy, no attach). **D1-6** `coalesced()` compares `tables` as ordered sequences (the service writes scope order on every line).
- **D2a-engine-1** The `SessionAccessGrant`/`AnyAccessGrant` aliases moved into the leaf with the three facts (the leaf may import the protocol only); grants.ts re-exports them type-only. **-2** `policyContextFor(db, now)` exported from service.ts — one home for binding the policy's context. **-3** `accessService()` is a stable facade resolving overrides at every call; `__setAccessServiceForTests(over?)` replaces the map, no argument clears it (the lanes take the module first, then set the spy). **-4** Rule-2..6 refusals carry `grant: found`; rule-1 refusals carry nothing (an unknown id, another reader's grant and an unbound session grant stay byte-identical). **-5** The ok verdict for `ask`/`list` carries `attended`; ignored there. **-6** `service.list` filters by `authorise(materialise).ok` alone — `live` IS rules 2, 3 and 5. **-7** An admitted read without a carried grant threw → `HOST_ERROR` (retired at Gate 5 by the `Admitted` overload, M-1). **-8** The service fetches its own db per op through `accessDeps().getDb()`; the handler only for `request`. *Deviation:* `AccessService.list` is async (no synchronous db accessor exists; the frame's answers are byte-identical); the `refused` line's reader name is resolved in the service.
- **D2a-ask-1** `renewSeedOf` kept as a one-line alias for `accessSheet.test.tsx` (the orchestrator moved that row and deleted the alias the same hour). **-2** `ACCESS_CARD.stale` ('this app is gone — nothing to ask for') added — the `no-app` state had no sentence and copy.ts is the only home. **-3** The builder set offers `access_propose` at build time under surface + the Settings switch + a wired stager (`buildByokTools` is synchronous; `agentTools.test.ts` pins the seven-tool set); the app-level rungs are the tool's at the call (the ownership rung added at Gate 5, SEC-2). **-4** `startUserAsk` without `ask` answers `'answer-other-first'` when `openReview` is false and `'no-app'` for a missing reader. **-5** The settle mapping: allowed → the source/tables/duration; not-now → not-now; declined → declined; failed → `CONSENT_UI.nothingAllowed`; dismissed and muted → NO resolution (B-Q3). **-6** The card reads the thread row ONCE on mount — the pin names the title and is what *review* asks for; a card whose `appId` ≠ the pin renders stale with no acts (S10). **-7** `waiting` while the sheet its *review* opened is up; a dismissal returns the acts. **-8** `egressFor` names the reader from the library row; an unowned id gets no chat line whatever the route. **-9** `STEP_LABELS.access_propose`; the rehydrate's pin is `row?.appId ?? pinnedAppId`. **-10** The hook's `onAccessProposal` closure is passed to the builder's handlers only; D2b passed it to `laneToolsFor`.
- **D2b-1** `materialiseGrants` performs no verdict — the service admits (`live && authorise(materialise).ok`) and performs the one effect a dump names (`drift` → `suspendAccess('source-changed')`). **-2** `takenNamesFor` adds every registry object (indexes and triggers too) — and, since Gate 5, every runtime object through `listAppObjectNames` (SEC-3). **-3** Only a SUCCESSFUL dump is cached. **-4** The cache is checked after the dequeue `stillLive` re-check; a stopped grant answers `ended` even with a cached result. **-5** A bytes thunk that throws → `too-large` over the source cap, else `failed`. **-6** `recordRead` iterates the set's distinct ids in set order filtered by the named ids; an id absent from the set is ignored; a `getDb` failure refuses every named id `failed`; rows summed per grant, tables = the scope (A-Q7/8). **-7** The data tools' refusals are prefixed `Error:`; the attach is `toAttach(set, recorded)`. **-8** The offer rungs in laneTools are read at the call through lazy imports in one private `accessProposeToolFor`. **-9** The engine is reached through ONE lazy edge (service.js + grants.js); the gate (`appMayUseAccess`, `chatDoorOpen`/`chatBrainRoute`) statically. **-10** One caller object per turn, reused by the seat's `recordRead`. **-11** `defaultSharedFor` folds refused grants into `skipped` and recomputes `readOnlyTables`. **-12** `renderSharedDdl` joins blocks with a blank line; a shared fence shows the columns line even at zero rows, with the cut said. **-13** No second `maskRows` pass on a dump — the copy's own withhold and mask are the rule (the read's belt predates PR-2 and stays). *Deviation:* a prefixed alias slug may reach 36 characters (no pin).
- **Surprises:** `ReturnType<typeof vi.spyOn>` resolves generically under vitest 3.2.7 and refuses a real spy under `strictFunctionTypes` — the one line the orchestrator changed in a test (`MockInstance<Db['scratchRun']>`); the KB edits moved five knowledge snapshot goldens (updated); no eslint is installed in the repo. The kit after D2b: **2,763,638 B — 13,638 B over the ceiling** (the owner's conversation, D6).

### PR-2 — Gate 5 fold (2026-10-11; three fresh-context diff reviews on Fable xhigh — security 5 findings (1 MAJOR) · maintainability-engine 10 · maintainability-doors 5; no blocker; all three "merge with folds". Every fold taken; the code to one Fable xhigh lane, the pins to one Opus lane, the docs to the orchestrator.)

- **SEC-1 (MAJOR)** A source app's display name (trim + 80-cap only; a model-authored title, an HTML `<title>`, a shared bundle's name — line breaks included) was rendered as the HOST's heading OUTSIDE the `<query_result>` delimiter in the chat's and the scheduler's prompts — a cross-app framing-text channel of the class the delimiter exists to close. Fold: `oneLine()` in `agent/sharedDdl.ts` (the one renderer) applied to every source name a prompt or a tool answer carries; four negative pins; the delta's row renamed and extended. **SEC-2 = DOORS-1** The builder set's `access_propose` had no ownership rung at the call though tools.ts said so: `appMayUseAccess` in the tool, `NOT_OWNED` sentence, a pin. **SEC-3** `takenNamesFor` missed a runtime-created VIEW/INDEX/TRIGGER of the alias name (the attach refused, failing closed, after the line was written): the proper fold — `listAppObjectNames(appId)` on the db, every runtime object taken. **SEC-4** `ACCESS_CARD.stale` pinned; a stored `dismissed` resolution is no longer admitted (no card). **SEC-5 = DOORS-2** The dismissal's `settle` could erase the card's *answer the open question first* note by one microtask: the phase resets only from `waiting`; a macrotask-`getDb` pin.
- **M-1** `Admitted` + overloads on `authorise`; the can't-happen throw gone. **M-2** `stillReadable` in policy.ts — the three spellings of "still this caller's, active, unexpired" become one. **M-3** One `admitted()` for `list` and `materialise`; `row.live &&` dropped (the policy is the rule). **M-4** No runtime export when there is nothing to materialise. **M-5** The dump cache stamped with the bytes' window (never served past 10 s). **M-6** `kind` is the Worker's discriminant. **M-7** `attachScratchTables` beside `scratchRun`; `as BindParams`. **M-8** The code-map storage row's coalescing sentence. **M-9** service.ts's header names the one invert. **M-10** The test lanes' local types replaced by the real exports. **DOORS-3** The abort-during-materialise pin. **DOORS-4** The vocabulary plant rows no longer write to disk (an in-memory proof over the real bytes). **DOORS-5** This section (the Gate-4 record was in the journal only).
- **Declined:** none. **Verified clean by the reviewers (no fold):** the policy's cell-for-cell equality with main's handler; service.read's byte-identity; the dump/attach/alias/`namesTable` belts (no statement reaches an attached table without naming it); the lazy edges; the data lane's sequencing; the card and sheet states; the scheduler door; the prompts; the docs against the code.

### PR-3 — Gate 4 decisions & surprises (2026-10-11, from the seven implementation lanes' reports and the fixes lane; each verified against contract v2 + v2.1; the PR-2 twin is the model)

- **D-IW-1** `ACCESS_LEVELS = ['read', 'write']` + `AccessLevel` exported (one spelling for the record's `access`, the ask's `access?` and `AccessRequestSemantics.access`). **-2** The response union's eight variants in `ACCESS_OPS` order. **-3** `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_STEPS` 64 and the undo ops enum are module-private. **-4** `withheld: []` parses as given; the normalisation to absent lives in `canonicalAccessGrantIntent`. **-5** The withheld/columns disjointness is case-sensitive like 1.1's duplicate rule. **-6** The undo entry's superRefine returns on the byte-cap issue before the walk; the per-cell walk refuses an auth-like column NAME too. **-7** `grantIdSchema` → `uuidV4Schema` (one regex for every uuid seat). **-8** Undo step `columns` carry the contract's bounds only. **-9** The `ACCESS_MATERIALISE_*` values copied unchanged. (I-W-rest) **-1'** KB 85's "A fetch another app asked for" is a bold-led paragraph (the heading tree is pinned at seven). **-2'** KB 00's clause sits beside the pinned phrase with a voice gloss. **-3'** `AccessDiscoverResult.candidates` is the protocol's `AccessCandidateClass`; `AccessDataChange { table }` new. **-4'** `host.ts` cases in `ACCESS_OPS` order; `refresh` spreads `reason` only when defined. **-5'** The fixture's three new helper stubs keep the KB's placeholder bodies. **-6'** The helper's three arms mirror the 1.1 arm byte for byte. **-7'** The write/refresh teaching lives as bold-led paragraphs inside the pinned sections (the heading tree is pinned at eleven). **-8'** The skill's sentence says "data" so "change it" has a referent. **-9'** The runner was built beside the sdk and knowledge.
- **D-ID-1** A new leaf `packages/db/src/statement-rules.ts` (the six statement rules + `KV_TABLE_DDL` moved verbatim out of `driver.ts`, which re-exports them) breaks the driver → brokered-write → scoped-read → driver cycle. **-2** The per-namespace transaction record is a STACK of frames (BEGIN/SAVEPOINT/RELEASE/ROLLBACK TO/COMMIT/END), cleared on every reopen (`persist`, `runExport`, `runImport`) and on a COMMIT/END/ROLLBACK failing "no transaction is active". **-3** Every `refused` refusal carries a `rule` token (`statement` · `no-where` · `table` · `column` · `computed` · `value`) mapping 1:1 onto `writeRefused`'s — the engine never parses prose. **-4** The grammar admits `NOT IN`/`NOT LIKE`; refuses `LIKE … ESCAPE`, a column named twice; `NULL`/`TRUE`/`FALSE` admitted values; `-` glued to a number is one literal. **-5** Two more fail-closed facts: a user column named `rowid`/`oid`/`_rowid_`, and a TEMP shadow of the table's name. **-6** An UPDATE's capture is recorded under `new.rowid` (a moved INTEGER PRIMARY KEY stays undoable). **-7** The undo re-validates the SHAPE rules (not the two reader-oracle rules — a reader's INSERT on a table with a credential-named column must stay undoable); every inverse is built and belted BEFORE `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. **-8** The plan seat applies D-Q15's cell rule early (`unsafe`/`too-large` at the plan). **-9** `BrokeredPlan.table` is the scope's spelling; the apply resolves `setColumns`/`insertColumns` to the live `table_xinfo` spelling. **-10** The `brokered` signal rings ONCE per apply (`table` when every plan names the same table, `changes` the sum); a throwing listener is swallowed. **-11** The facade refuses `brokeredWrite` through `hostKvRefusal` (tombstone AND an unheld app); its listener set is wired into every inner driver inside `makeInnerDriver`. **-12** `putAccessUndo` drops unreadable elements beside expired ones; `markAccessUndone` validates `at`; an unknown id is a no-op; `listAllAccessUndo` answers every readable entry. **-13** Under `kv`, `scopeCopy` runs `KV_TABLE_DDL` first (an honest empty table for a source that never used kv). **-14** The brokered-write unions are exported by name.
- **D-IU-1** `writeRefused` takes a token OR that rule's own sentence (the engine passes tokens, the copy suite sentences). **-2** `undoFailed(reason, sourceName = 'the other app')`: the four tokens worded with the source's name; any other reason said as given. **-3** `historyLine(entry, now, readerName?, sourceName?)` keeps the two optional names; a refreshed reason's words go BEFORE the time, a wrote line's via clause AFTER. **-4** `ConsentDecision.allow.kv`/`.refresh` optional (absent ≡ false); the sheet always emits both. **-5** `ParkAccessRequestInput.renew` stays names; the old scope rides the `SheetSeed`. **-6** The renewal seed carries `tables` only when the old scope is WIDENED, `kv`/`refresh` only when true (a plain read renewal seeds exactly `{duration, unattended}`). **-7** "choose at least one column" is the writer's sayable refusal when every readable column is withheld. **-8** `renewalBase` drops the renewed record's kv/refresh before the spread. **-9** `StripOutcome.stopped.write` REQUIRED. **-10** `STRIP_OUTCOME.allowed`'s two forms (every table / some). **-11** `REFRESH_FAILED_WORDS` one record over the five reasons; `refreshNoHandler` is its `no-handler` sentence by reference. **-12** `TableChoice` lives in grants.ts; `EgressOptions` in egress.ts. **-13** The GRANT's provenance is `chat` when the pending's `askedIn === 'chat'` while the pending keeps `user`. (I-U-ui) **-1'** A renewal's seeded table starts with every readable column ticked minus the seed's withheld (a column the source gained since is offered ticked). **-2'** The failed-undo outcome line is `undoFailed(answer.reason, sourceName)` from the engine's token. **-3'** `readResolution` admits `write` only as a list ⊆ the resolution's tables. **-4'** The column chooser and the write toggle render only on a TICKED row; unticking forgets an open chooser. **-5'** Both disclosures are native `<details>`; the sheet owns `optionsOpen`. **-6'** `kv`/`refresh` are kept PER SOURCE APP on the sheet. **-7'** The ⋈ sheet bumps the revision itself after the undo. **-8'** `AccessHistory` props: `sourceName` required, `undoable`/`onUndo` optional; `UndoOutcome` exported. **-9'** The one undo outcome line is component state keyed by `undoId`. **-10'** The chooser's cut reuses `CHIPS_MAX`. **-11'** The write badge sits in `.access-row-state`; no second act.
- **D-IE1-1** The write act's `table` is optional: the service names it off the statement's verb (`dmlTargetTable`); a statement naming none passes rule 7 vacuously and the broker refuses it `statement`. **-2** The discover cache lives in service.ts keyed by the reader's app id, stamped with `readerFrameEnds` (a reset moves it). **-3** *(retired by the fixes: own mode now uses the seat's `own` variant — no plan is built from text.)* **-4** The undo's schema re-validation reads `pragma_table_xinfo` through the driver's read seat (never an export, which would clear the transaction record). **-5** A strict write's SQL `failed`/`copy-failed` answers the bare `ACCESS_QUERY_FAILED` sentence (a constraint's name is a source fact). **-6** The per-app minute counts reads and writes only. **-7** `loggedSql`/`appendLine` exported from writeBroker.ts, imported by service.ts. **-8** `ringDataChanged(target, undefined)` rings `data-changed {}` when the table is unknown; own mode rings once per distinct table. **-9** `armDoorbell` in `attemptOpen`'s ok branch and `recoverFresh`; `importUserDb` needs no re-arm (the facade's set survives the swap). **-10** A plan evicts the grant's cached dump with its bytes; the three answer mappers are exhaustive. **-11** `executeApprovedWrite` reaches `applyApprovedWrite` through a lazy import (F13). **-12** The `undone` line's readerName is the reader's current library name, falling back to the entry's. **-13** The kv alias is de-collided like every full name. **-14** `endedWrite`: a grant still live but no longer writable for the table answers `writeNotGranted`; an expiry marks once.
- **D-IE2-1** `DispatchEnd` gains `{ kind: 'busy' }` (beginDelegatedRun's refusal, a second hidden mount); `outcomeOf` reads it `failed`; refresh.ts maps it to `ACCESS_BUSY` and puts the cooldown stamp back. **-2** `countersSoFar(db, spent, soFar, nowIso)` / `foldDailyCalls(db, calls, nowIso)` take the instant (the queue folds on its injected clock). **-3** `Dispatch.spent` = the run's earlier steps + the step's earlier attempts. **-4** A record's refusals are judged before the end kind. **-5** `clearSourceReadCaches` runs whenever the source's code ran and reported ok, even when refusals turn the answer `refused`. **-6** A source whose library row is gone answers `refreshFailed('failed')` with no line. **-7** The refreshed line is written best-effort (`logQuietly`). **-8** Two clocks as in appRun.ts (the engine's decisions on `accessDeps().now()`; the deadline on `Date.now()`). **-9** `net.ts` keeps the PR-1 `scheduledRun` seam beside `scheduledVia` (netState pins it). **-10** `scheduledDbDriver` forwards `subscribeWrites` (and, after the fixes, `inTransaction`) and refuses `brokeredWrite` as data. **-11** The refresh chip is its own small component with a modifier class. **-12** `refusalSentence` maps `refused` to the generic needsYou sentence (unreachable for a schedule). **-13** A refresh's refusals and calls are summed across attempts.
- **The fixes (D-FIX)** after the lanes: **-1** `SnugDbDriver.inTransaction(namespace)` — a READ of the record, no handle touch; the facade and the scheduled binding forward it; the broker asks it BEFORE the plan's fresh export (sql.js `export()` would discard the app's transaction and clear the record — the seat's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` stays the belt). **-2** The seat's OWN variant `{ own: {sql, params?}[], caps, expectChanges? }`: the belts (`forbiddenStatementReason`, the DML class, one statement), `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, each statement, the drift halt, `COMMIT`, `undoable: false`, no facts, no capture; `applyApprovedWrite` uses it and the text-extraction planner is gone; `caps.maxChanges` is NOT read in own mode (the approved counts are the rule). **-3** The planner's cell rule is a pure predicate (`JOURNAL_TEXT_MAX_CHARS` 4096 = `ACCESS_PARAM_STRING_MAX_CHARS`, pinned by a test): importing the protocol's `accessParamSchema` had pulled zod and the whole access module into the inline Worker (−79,223 B). **-4** `markAccessUndone` with three arguments everywhere. **-5** The reader fixture's header says its helper block IS KB 87 v2's render.
- **Surprises:** `scopedReadWorker`/`materialise` rows moved their cap imports to the protocol (the test lanes' doing — D-PR3-26). The knowledge content regeneration moved six snapshot goldens (updated after reading each diff). `accessDrift.test.ts:262` is a CLOCK BOMB in a PR-2 test, not a PR-3 defect: a one-day access seeded at `2026-10-10T09:00Z` against the wall clock the recheck reads — green at the session's start, red after 09:00Z (the pins lane fixes it with a fake clock). `driver.test.ts` "an import clears it" exported a never-written namespace (0 bytes → `BAD_IMPORT`) — a fixture defect. vite resolves a LITERAL dynamic import at transform time, so `AccessSheet.tsx`'s lazy `import('./writeBroker.js')` made the whole sheet suite fail to load until the module existed (the lanes landed in parallel; green once both were in). Root `pnpm test` refuses on the branch (`check-host-kit` red by design; `server#test` fails on a better-sqlite3 native-ABI mismatch under Node 24, unrelated). `check-threat-model` is 498/498 and `check-whitepaper` 110/110 after the docs second pass; the ledger re-pinned twice (`051f72ad3911` → `98c86ecdeaaa`).
- **Measured — the kit:** baseline 2,742,366 B; after Gate 4 **2,897,704 B**; after the zod cut **2,818,481 B — 68,481 B OVER `KIT_SIZE_CEILING_BYTES` (2,750,000), +76,115 B over `main`**. The per-suspect deltas (one stub each): writeBroker.ts 7,096 · refresh.ts 2,723 · doorbell.ts 1,078 · the apply half in main 9,999 · the planner in the Worker 15,071 → the new code ≈ 36 KB, inside the +35–47 KB estimate; the rest is growth across modified modules (`protocol/access.ts` +19.6 KB of source, `access/copy.ts` +14.5 KB, the KB content +10.2 KB, `userdb/access.ts` +7.1 KB, `service.ts` +7.0 KB, the sheet/chooser/history/policy ≈ 5–6 KB each) — no single further cut. **D-PR3-28 / D6: the owner's ceiling conversation, held in the PR body — the one-line decision is `scripts/check-host-kit.mjs:30`; the optional bytes are the refresh engine (≈ 2.7 KB) and `discover` (< 1 KB); CI's `check-host-kit` is red on the branch until the owner decides.**

### PR-3 — Gate 5 fold (2026-10-11; three fresh-context diff reviews on Fable xhigh — security 6 findings (1 BLOCKER) · maintainability-wire+db 10 (3 major) · maintainability+product-engine+UI+docs 9 (1 major); all three "merge with the folds listed". Every fold taken; the code to one Fable xhigh lane, the docs to one Fable lane, the pins to one Opus lane, the task file to the orchestrator.)

- **SEC-1 (BLOCKER)** The apply measured the journal's STEPS against `ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES` while the entry schema measures the WHOLE entry (ids, instants, the names ≈ 300–500 B more): a reader could land a write in the window below the cap, `journal()`'s safeParse refused the entry after the COMMIT and the `wrote` line carried no `undoId` — a reader-craftable change the user cannot undo (verified: 200 × 300-char inserts). Fold: `STRICT_CAPS.maxUndoBytes = ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES − ACCESS_UNDO_ENTRY_RESERVE_BYTES` (1 KiB for the host-written seats) shared by the plan and the apply, so the dry run refuses `too-large` before any live handle; a near-cap pin; R-97 says so.
- **SEC-2** `putAccessUndo` counted the reader's live entries over the LOOSE view while the engine pre-checked over the STRICT read — a planted loosely-valid row could cost a committed write its undo or refuse a reader forever. Fold: the accessor builds `kept` from the strict read (a strictly-unparseable element is worthless bytes); one rule. **SEC-3** `discover` cached only after the gather resolved — a burst of eight ran eight library gathers. Fold: the in-flight promise is cached before the await. The orchestrator's re-read added the stale case: a caller that awaited a STALE hit then started its own gather beside the one a concurrent caller had just parked (one per caller again, after the cache expired or the frame reset) — now it joins the parked gather (`64fcc55`); pinned with the empty-cache burst. **SEC-4** The undo admitted a credential-named column that a genuine entry can never name. Fold: refused `column` at phase 1 (a planted row can move values, never into a key). **SEC-5** A table-less `data-changed` (own mode, the table unknown) was dropped by the SDK's reducer. Fold: `AccessDataChange { table?: string }` — `{}` reaches the listener. **SEC-6** Residuals the delta lacked: the FK CHILD-side oracle under `foreign_keys = 1` (one bit about a parent key's existence — now REFUSED symmetrically with the parent-side rule, free when the pragma is off), a UNIQUE over an unshared column with a non-NULL DEFAULT, the busy answer as a mid-change signal (R-92), a refresh chain across open frames under away-ticked accesses (R-91; a refresh is admitted as a READ), R-96's burst bound; the delta and the fold re-pinned.
- **W-1 (major)** A write matching no row answered `undoable: true` with an empty step: a no-op journal entry offering *undo* of nothing, counting against the reader's twenty. Fold: `undoable` means there is something to undo (the empty steps filtered); the protocol's step `rows.min(1)` as the belt. **W-2 (major)** `caps` was a required seat on the `own` and `undo` variants that neither apply read — a bound the type promised and the code ignored. Fold: dropped from both variants; the engine's dead caps gone. **W-3 (major)** The comment-stripping rule was spelled seven times in the db package. Fold: `stripSqlComments` in `statement-rules.ts`, the one home. **W-4** `ScopeCopyReport` gains `ok: true` (the house discriminant). **W-5** The db index no longer exports the raw-handle seats (`applyBrokeredBatch`, `buildUndoStatement`, `parseBrokeredStatement`, `tableFacts`, `scopeCopy`) — the apply is the driver's seat (D-PR3-11 read literally). **W-6** `BUSY_REFUSAL` one home. **W-7** One `grantHint(event)` helper in the SDK. Sanctioned test edit: the knowledge KB pins that grepped the SDK's three literal `onHostEvent(<CONSTANT>,` calls now read the `grantHint(<CONSTANT>)` shape (the intent — the SDK subscribes with the constants the helper hears — unchanged; the data-changed arm stays literal, SEC-5). **W-8** KB 87 said a refresh runs "only when the user is present" — the landed policy admits it under a read's presence rule (E1-Q5); the sentence corrected. **W-9** The module contract's `PlanRefusal` gains `'unsafe'` and the S12 fold sentence reads "the plan's walk is the early answer" (edited above). **W-10** One `tableColumns` helper over `PRAGMA table_xinfo` (four hand-written filters become one).
- **ENG-1 (major)** An APP's renewal ask (`request { renew }`) parked names only and never seeded the sheet, so the sheet started wider than the grant it renewed (withheld columns re-ticked) and a write grant renewed as read-only under one tap — D-PR3-23 promised the scope travels on a renewal; only `startUserAsk` seeded it. Fold: `renewalSeedOf(grant, duration, unattended)` exported from userAsk.ts and called by BOTH doors (the app path keeps its session/away defaults; only the scope travels); two pins. **ENG-2** The write's table was derived twice and the policy's write act carried it as optional (rule 7 skippable by shape). Fold: the service refuses a nameless statement BEFORE the verdict; `table` required on the act and on `BrokeredWriteRequest`. **ENG-3** After a live→hidden handover the refresh's second attempt asked the ceiling without the first attempt's calls. Fold: each attempt's Dispatch carries the summed tally. **ENG-4** Three copies of the source-bytes thunk + `SourceTooLarge`, `readerName`/`iso` triplicated, a double revision bump on undo. Fold: one home each (materialise.ts, grants.ts); the engine's bump stands. **UI-1** `undoArm(table, 1, …)` disagreed in number ("put 1 row … back as they were"). Fold: the sentence agrees for n = 1. **UI-2** After *yes, undo* focus fell to `<body>` inside the modal sheet (the outcome line was not focusable); and the per-row write toggles sit above the *more options…* summary that reveals them. Fold: the outcome line takes focus on the next frame (the OutcomeLine recipe); the summary gains an `aria-describedby` sentence. **DOC-1** SPEC §24.5's own-app sentence named `authority: 'approval'`, which nothing records at 1.2 — reworded (reserved for the own-app undo, §25); the glossary likewise. **DOC-2** Code-map named the deleted `limits.ts`, `undoArm`'s old arity and the retired `scheduledRun` seam; architecture the same seam — corrected. **DOC-3** The delta quoted an undo sentence the copy does not carry — corrected to the `gone` token's words.
- **Declined:** none — every finding folded (W-3's optional `errorMessage` consolidation taken only if cheap). **Verified clean by the reviewers (no fold):** the parser against 66 smuggling attempts (every subquery, function, CTE, RETURNING, conflict clause, qualifier, second statement, comment trick, double-quoted string, bare column, arithmetic refused by name); the three column oracles and the masked set decided before any dry run; the table facts at both seats incl. the TEMP trigger, the CHECK and the partly-shared DELETE on the live handle; a failed apply leaving no TEMP object and no open transaction; the UPDATE that moved an INTEGER PRIMARY KEY captured under `new.rowid` and undone; the transaction record across BEGIN/SAVEPOINT/RELEASE/ROLLBACK TO and every reopen; the write signal raised only in `handle()`; the refresh gates/cooldown/stamp synchronous before the first await, the refusing branch first, the sticky write unconditional; every event one id or one table; the SDK settlers; the runner's enumeration; a strict `failed` answering the generic sentence; the schemas a byte-exact regeneration; `sideEffects: false` with no top-level side effect added; the zod-free Worker; the eleven methods / seven ops / fifteen codes consistent across protocol, runner, SDK and KB; the consent sheet, the history, the rows and the strip against D-PR3-23/24; the CSS tokens; every spec MUST 18–30 traced to code; the delta, the fold, ADR-0076, next-steps, the glossary and product-vision against the code.

### PR-4 — Gate 4 decisions & surprises (2026-10-12, from the four implementation lanes' reports on Opus 5.5 xhigh; each checked against contract v2 + v2.1)

- **I-1 (protocol + db + shared surface + copy):** `OwnWriteRefusal = Exclude<ScheduleHeldReason, 'over-limit'>` — tied to the held table (D-I1-1); `ownUndoRoom` reserves one FULL entry (`ACCESS_UNDO_MAX_BYTES`) beside the live ones — ~1 KiB stricter than the contract's "64 KiB − reserve", so a put after a passing room check can never answer `full` after the commit (D-I1-2); `putOwnUndo` admits an entry only into its own bucket (`approval` ⇔ authority `approval` with no `taskId`; a task bucket ⇔ authority `task` with `taskId` = the bucket) — a task whose id spells `approval` can never reach the bucket that never refuses (D-I1-3); journal parse failures reuse `ACCESS_INVALID` (D-I1-4); the own column rule judges the columns the UNDO would write (SET / the INSERT list), never the WHERE (D-I1-5); the variant runs its own cell/byte check before `admit`, and a call that captured no rows commits `undoable:false` without asking (D-I1-6); `clearScheduleHistory` writes the kept runs newest first by `dueAt` (D-I1-7); bounds the contract left open — `armedVersion ≥ 1`, `applied.items` 1–3, `approved` 1–3, `tools.fetched` 1–8, `saw` names 1–64 (D-I1-11); `executeApprovedWrite`'s `{via}` defaults to `'chat'` (the PR-3 callers) and the result page passes `'user'` (D-I1-12); every PR-4 sentence single-homed by key (D-I1-13); the vocabulary scan reads string literals in TYPE positions too — a `Pick<ScheduleRun, 'proposals'>` under `schedule/` trips it, so the lanes use the protocol's types and constants (D-I1-18 — a lesson).
- **I-2a (writes):** `OwnWriteInput.onCommitted?(outcome)` runs synchronously after the bucket put and before the doorbells — the walk rewrites the claim row inside it (F20's same tick, D-I2a-1); the seat's drift answers `changed` with `current` (D-I2a-2); the approval reaches the seat through an internal `seatOwnWrite` after its own `inTransaction` + dry run (D-I2a-3); seat refusals map `too-many` → `changed`, `too-large`/`unsafe`/unplannable → `cannot-undo` (a task) or the whole-batch bare re-call (an approval) (D-I2a-5); own and strict undo share ONE core, `reverseEntry` (D-I2a-7); **armed is decided on the task captured at claim** — a change proposed while the model was told "it waits for your OK" is never made on its own even if the schedule was armed mid-run — and the walk also asks `stillArmed` at its start (D-I2a-8); a failed re-dry-run holds that item and the rest `failed`, the prefix may apply (D-I2a-9); `applyArmed` never throws (D-I2a-10); the queue slices to 3 before the walk (D-I2a-11); an aborted armed run holds only the armed app's ok-step items `stopped` (D-I2a-12); `fitRunRow` sheds summaries, then the waiting list, then the step host facts, never `applied`/`approved` (D-I2a-13); the walk runs after the bound timer and is not cancellable — the seat is atomic and `stillArmed` re-reads the global pause just before it (D-I2a-14); the notification body is the words + `· <resultFacts>` with the words cut to keep the facts inside 120 chars (D-I2a-15); `disarmSchedulesForAppVersion` disarms only when the new version differs from `armedVersion` (D-I2a-16); `armTask` refuses a not-one-app schedule `NOT_ONE_APP` (D-I2a-17); `armableAppOf` (N4) lives once in `applyArmed.ts` (D-I2a-18); the steps compare is `scheduleStepSchema`-parsed + `canonicalJson`, "shorter interval" via `floors.ts` `minIntervalMs` with a one-off as +∞ (D-I2a-19); the plural armed tail agrees in number (D-I2a-20); the authority literal reaches `applyArmed.ts` by destructuring `OWN_WRITE_AUTHORITIES` (D-I2a-21); the late fold attaches only on the lost race (D-I2a-22).
- **I-2b (agent):** `renderProviderResult` keeps the positional scrub by overload (D-I2b-1); `FETCH_CONNECTION_MAX_CHARS` bounds the WHOLE answer, frame included (D-I2b-2); the fetch tool's deps strip `lanFetch` and the private-http `transportPolicy` as well as `sidecarFetch` — the LAN exclusion is structural too (D-I2b-3); `onNetCall(slot)` and `tools.fetched` tallied at admission (D-I2b-4); `fetchableConnections` → `{slot, name}` and leaves out rows with no single address (D-I2b-5); symbolic-path failures answer the scrubbed executor message, composed-path failures a fixed host-free sentence (D-I2b-6); `gatedAdapter` counts before awaiting and un-counts `CONSENT_REQUIRED` (D-I2b-7); the sidecar degrade uses `appHasSidecarFact` over ANY row status — R-9's own trigger, since the tool loop bypasses `guardWireForApp` (D-I2b-8); `saw` on the tool-free step rides every outcome after the set was obtained, on the agent only the recorded tables (D-I2b-9); the agent's first message carries the shared tables by NAME only (`withSharedRows: false`) — rows only through `data_query`'s recorded seat (D-I2b-11); the deadline answers `failed` with the calls spent (D-I2b-12); a mid-turn net-ceiling refusal keeps the answer `ok` + `capped` (D-PR1-6's precedent), an AI `CAPPED` → `refused` + `capped` (D-I2b-13); staged proposals ride every outcome — the walk arms only ok steps' (D-I2b-14); the prompt platform is read through a new `promptPlatform()` in `agent/transport.ts` (the availability scan's one allow-listed read, D-I2b-15); `schedule_propose` passes `tools: true` through (D-I2b-16); the scheduled-agent layer is `50`, with `{{appAgentMaxToolCalls}}`/`{{appAgentMaxRoundTrips}}` placeholders (D-I2b-17); `inOpenApp` on every outcome a live attempt settled, manual *run now* included (D-I2b-18); `KEYLESS_BRAIN_REFUSAL = brainBlocked.text` and a composition without `brainRouteFor` (the unit seam) runs every ask step tool-free and ungated as before PR-4 (D-I2b-20). Files touched outside the list, owned by no lane: `agent/transport.ts` (`promptPlatform`), `agent/scheduleProposeTool.ts` (the passthrough).
- **I-4 (UI):** the consent answers `onEnable({onItsOwn})`; each host arms through `armIfTicked` only after the one writer created the schedule, and a refused arming leaves the schedule unarmed and SAYS so (D-I4-1); the brain line shows under a TICKED tools step only (D-I4-2); one shared undo component `access/OwnUndoAct.tsx` for the result page, the changes list and the chat card, focus to *keep* on arm and to the outcome line on answer (D-I4-4); after a reload an undone-earlier entry reads *undo ended after seven days* (no accessor exposes undone entries — named in the next-steps) (D-I4-5); the changes list reads every app's bucket for the task (the delete sweep's rule) (D-I4-6); the result-to-changes link `/schedule?history=<taskId>` (D-I4-7); attention order paused > disarmed > imported > needs-you (D-I4-8); every cost surface counts a tools step 10 AI calls, the missed card included (D-I4-9); `CONSENT_ROWS.lookUpBound(perDay, each)` and `lookUps([])` reads *none* (D-I4-10); the approve's `approved` entry and its cap (D-I4-11); a pre-PR-4 applied card claims nothing about undo (D-I4-12); the editor reads armed from the task as loaded and never re-arms without the consent (D-I4-13); the Settings act has no confirm — the row disappearing is the answer (D-I4-14). Touched beyond the list where the surfaces required it: `schedule/pageModel.ts`, `MissedCard.tsx`, `routes.ts`, `run/RunView.tsx`, two theme CSS files, the new `access/OwnUndoAct.tsx` and `schedule/ScheduleChanges.tsx`.
- **Surprises:** the T-B characterisation proved the live bug D-PR4-5 names (an approved write under the app's open `BEGIN` applied, because `scratchRun`'s export discarded the transaction) — fixed by `inTransaction` first; the vocabulary scan reads type-position literals (D-I1-18); the kit check reads whatever `apps/host/dist` holds — it measured `main`'s stale 2,565,276 B until the orchestrator rebuilt it (**2,624,707 B after Gate 4, +59,431 B, 125,293 B under the ceiling**) — measure only after `pnpm --filter host build` (a lesson).
- **v2.2 notes — the sanctioned test edits (Gate 4 → the pins lane):** (a) `dataTools.test.ts:514` widened to D-PR4-6's `{ok, executed, undoable:true, undoId}`; (b)/(c) `writeBroker.test.ts:416`/`:1101` — "no line" means no line FROM THE WRITE: the setup's own `granted`/`read` lines stay; (d) `scheduleExecutors.test.ts:372` gains `saw`; (e) five `appRunHandshake.test.tsx` manual rows gain `inOpenApp: true`; (f) `scheduleRowStates.test.tsx:365` — the attention assertion before the navigation; (g) `resultDetail.test.tsx:922` — a fixture the connection registry admits; (h) the own-undo fixture helpers typed `OwnUndoEntry`; (i) a NEW pin for `UpdatePausesNote`'s armed tail.

### PR-4 — Gate 5 fold (2026-10-12; the Opus high verification lane all green — protocol 622 · db 964 · knowledge 358 · sdk 110 · runner 184 · auth 946 · adapters 130 · playground 5,998 + 3 skipped · host 731 · desktop 231 · the kit 2,624,726 B · `check-threat-model` 498 · website-sync 25 pages · sandbox-guard 4 · whitepaper 110 · e2e playground 16/16 (access 11 · schedule-flow 5) and host 13/13 (schedule 10 · local-schedule 3); four fresh-context diff reviews on Opus 5.5 xhigh — security 7 findings (2 major) · jobs 11 (2 major) · authority 12 (3 major) · UI 13 (3 major); no blocker; all four "merge with the folds listed". Every finding folded but two, each declined with its reason; the code to three Opus xhigh lanes (jobs ∥ authority, then UI), the pins to two Opus high lanes, the docs to the Gate-6 docs lane. Ids: SEC-n · JOBS-n · AUTH-n · UI-n.)

**Jobs (code lane C-J; pins lane P-E):**
- **J1** [JOBS-F1, major] `proposalGate.ts` refuses an UNBOUND placeholder (`?`, `?N`, `:v`, `@v`, `$v` outside quoted text — blank `'…'`/`"…"` first, then `/(?<![\w$])(?:\?\d*|[:@$][A-Za-z_]\w*)/`) as `{ok:false, reason:'placeholder'}` BEFORE any dry run; the scheduled `data_propose_write` answers it with the no-params sentence (*write the values into the statement itself*); the tool-free step drops such a candidate. `OWN_CAPTURE_RULE` → *literal values, a WHERE on UPDATE/DELETE, no functions, arithmetic or conflict clause* (no "or ?" anywhere — D-PR4-16's quoted answer amended with it).
- **J2** [JOBS-F2, major] the scheduled tool gets its OWN definition: `packages/knowledge/prompts/tools/data-propose-write-scheduled.md` (`ToolPromptName` `'data-propose-write-scheduled'`; ONE INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on this app's own tables, values written in, no params, never a *From <Source>* table, the answer says whether it waits for the user's OK or this schedule may make it on its own, never claim it was made); `inputSchema` = `statements` (`minItems:1, maxItems:1`) + `summary`, NO `params` property (the runtime refusal stays); the chat's def byte-identical to `main`; `50-scheduled-agent.md`'s blast-radius header rewritten for the armed case; `gen:content`.
- **J3** [JOBS-F3] `executeAppAgent` re-reads `chatBrainRoute(ctx.db, step.appId)` immediately before building its adapter: `kind === 'demo'` → `keylessBrainGate`'s outcome; `!chatDoorOpen(route)` → `NO_DIRECT_BRAIN` with `tools:{unavailable:'brain'}`; the adapter config is built FROM that route; a mismatch of `adapterKindFor` with the route throws loudly. The duplicated branch logic goes.
- **J4** [JOBS-F4] the stored answer is the FINAL round's text (the round with no tool calls), never the concatenation.
- **J5** [JOBS-F5] the fetch carries the turn's signal (`AbortSignal.any` over the executor's own); the tool's `requestTimeoutMs` 30 s → 20 s so the 30 s margin holds.
- **J6** [JOBS-F6] `StepOutcome.capped?: boolean | 'ai' | 'net'` — `appAgent` sets `'net'`/`'ai'`, `appRun`'s `withCap` returns the kind it found; the queue's run reason becomes `capped(<the kind's words>)` (C-A); the result page reads the kind (C-U).
- **J7** [JOBS-F7] `StepContext.proposalsSoFar?: () => number` (the queue sets it to the pooled count — C-A); the agent's `onProposal` answers `false` (→ `RUN_FULL`) once pooled + its own reach 3; the queue appends `surplusNote` when its slice drops any (C-A).
- **J8** [JOBS-F8] `dryRunCandidates` counts a `busy` refusal apart from `dropped`; the summary's note reads *<n> suggested change(s) not kept — <appMidChange(app)>* (one home in `schedule/copy.ts`), never *not safe to keep*.
- **J9** [JOBS-F9, SEC-F6] ONE helper `runContextQueries(db, appId, appName, sqls, attach)` (asks `inTransaction` once when there are queries; busy → each answers `{error: appMidChange(appName)}`), used by BOTH `executeAppThink` and `executeAppAgent`.
- **J10** [JOBS-F10] the gate's `failed` carries SQLite's detail; the scheduled answer reads `Error: that change failed on a copy of the data — <detail scrubbed 'deliver'>; it was not proposed`; `invalid` names the 2,048-character cap.
- **J11** [JOBS-F11] `WITHHELD_SUMMARY` → `schedule/scrub.ts`; one `ZERO_CALLS` in `protection.ts`; `DEMO_BRAIN_REFUSAL` deleted — `executeAppThink`'s `transport === undefined` arm returns `keylessBrainGate`'s outcome (`scheduleExecutors.test.ts:446`'s "its own sentence" row INVERTED as a named inversion); `appAgent.ts` imports nothing from `./executors.js` (a static-import pin).
- **J12** [SEC-F4] the agent's net check reads the net counter directly (`day.net + 1 > SCHEDULE_DAILY_CEILINGS.net`) — never through `wouldExceedCeiling`'s AI-first answer.

**Authority (code lane C-A; pins lane P-E):**
- **A1** [SEC-F1 major, AUTH-F4, AUTH-F10] `ownUndoBelongsIn(bucket, {authority, taskId})` in `packages/db/src/userdb/own-undo.ts` (approval ⇔ bucket `approval` and no `taskId`; task ⇔ `taskId === bucket` and bucket ≠ `approval`), used by `putOwnUndo`, as a FILTER in `listOwnUndo`, and FIRST in `seatOwnWrite` (not belonging → `cannot-undo` under `requireUndo`, else `failed` — before any seat); `admit` = belongs ∧ the schema; `armTask` refuses a task whose id is `OWN_UNDO_APPROVAL_BUCKET` (`RESERVED_ID`); `ownUndoEntrySchema` gains a refinement — authority `task` ⇔ `taskId` present; protocol exports `TASK_WRITE_AUTHORITY` / `APPROVAL_WRITE_AUTHORITY` (named, `satisfies OwnWriteAuthority`) and `applyArmed.ts` imports by NAME, never by tuple position. (AUTH-F4's typed-bucket keys DECLINED for A1: the same protection without an accessor rewrite across every suite — reversal word "keys".)
- **A2** [SEC-F2 major, AUTH-F1 major, AUTH-F12] the proactive disarm is WIRED: `onAppVersionChanged(db, appId, version, 'own', nowIso)` after `saveAppVersion` in `agent/artifactSink.ts` (an existing app), after `revertApp`/`resetToFactory` in `run/VersionsPanel.tsx`, and `'starter'` after `saveAppVersion` in `starter/starterUpdate.ts`; `accessDrift.test.ts`'s call-site scan extended so every `saveAppVersion(`/`revertApp(`/`resetToFactory(` site reaches `onAppVersionChanged`; `disarmSchedulesForAppVersion` wraps each task's put in try/catch (never thrown at the update — the reader-access pause still runs).
- **A3** [AUTH-F2 major] the run's abort seat stays live THROUGH the walk: the steps loop's `finally` clears only the timer; `current = undefined` after `settleChanges`; `applyArmed(db, run, task, items, {now, signal})`; `stillArmed` adds `!signal.aborted` (at the walk's start and immediately before the seat); the pre-seat awaits (`inTransaction`, `scratchRun`) race the signal; an aborted walk holds every armed item `stopped` and the run folds `interrupted`.
- **A4** [AUTH-F3 major] the walk partitions by `ownCapturable(item.sql)` BEFORE the dry run: refused items `held:'cannot-undo'`; only the capturable ones are dry-run, budgeted and applied, in order; `applyArmed` answers ONE verdict per input index (`{made:true} | {held:reason} | {untouched:true}`) and the queue's `afterWalk` maps by index — never "the first N of the armed app".
- **A5** [SEC-F3, AUTH-F5] once the seat answered ok, nothing turns the outcome into a refusal: `seatOwnWrite`'s `onCommitted`, cache clear and rings — and `undoOwnWrite`'s `retire()`, caches and rings — each in try/catch with a `console.warn` (PR-3's post-commit posture).
- **A6** [SEC-F7] deleting an app disarms every task naming it; `stillArmed` and `armTask` also refuse when the app's `createdAt` is after `writeAuthority.armedAt` (a reinstall under the same id never inherits the consent).
- **A7** [AUTH-F7] `applied`/`approved` are stripped ONLY by the untrusted import pass (`scrubScheduleRunRows(…, {stripOwnSeats: !trustedOrigin})`) — an export, a sync push and a trusted pull keep them (the device keeps its disclosure across a pull; a foreign `undoId` already resolves to nothing once the untrusted import drops `ownUndo:*`). The T-B Q17 export-strip pins are INVERTED as named inversions.
- **A8** [AUTH-F9] a prefix whose fresh counts are all 0 never reaches the seat: no `applied` group, the items leave the waiting list.
- **A9** [AUTH-F11] `planOwnCapture` re-words a parser refusal by rule in own-mode words (e.g. `no-where` → *a change to every row can’t be undone — it needs a WHERE*); the pin's reader-words regex widens to `/other app|another app|isn.t shared|shared/i`.
- **A10** [JOBS-F6/F7 queue side] the queue's capped reason from the kind; `proposalsSoFar`; the surplus note.
- **A11** [UI-12 engine side] `schedule/taskShape.ts` (pure) holds `armableAppOf` and `widens` (schema-parsed); `acts.ts` and `applyArmed.ts` import from it.
- **A12** [UI-2 accessor side] `listOwnUndo(appId, which, {includeUndone?: boolean})` — undone-but-unexpired entries reported with their `undoneAt`.
- **A13** [AUTH-F6] `ownUndoRoom` stays the gate; the result page words `full` with the bucket's real live count (C-U).

**UI (code lane C-U, after C-J ∥ C-A; pins lane P-U):**
- **U1** [UI-1 major] the editor's brain read is tri-state `'tools' | 'no-tools' | 'blocked'` (`blocked` ⇔ `keylessBrainGate(db, appId, chatBrainRoute(db, appId)) !== undefined`): `blocked` shows `brainBlocked.text` + a link to Settings under EVERY ask step, ticked or not, and on the consent (`blockedApps`); `no-tools` keeps `STEPS.toolsUnavailable`.
- **U2** [UI-2 major] `liveUndoIds` → a `Map<id, 'live' | 'undone'>` from A12; an undone entry reads *undone* after a reload; a gone id reads *can no longer be undone* (ONE home shared with `DATA_WRITE_CARD`'s ended) unless the run finished ≥ `ACCESS_UNDO_TTL_MS` ago, when it reads *undo ended after seven days* (`resultDetail.test.tsx:720` rebased onto an 8-day-old run).
- **U3** [UI-3 major — the orchestrator's decision, under the owner's delegation of copy and defaults; reversible by the word "read-only"] the consent and the editor stop promising what ADR-0076 §9 says is false: `CONSENT_ROWS.lookUps` → *may look things up through (it only asks, never posts — but what it asks can carry your data; it can use them without asking you each time): <names>*; `STEPS.toolsLine` → *… and ask <App>’s connections (it only asks, never posts) · up to 10 AI calls each time*; a negative pin: neither says *read only* or *can’t send*.
- **U4** [UI-4] pins only (P-U): the strip and the sheet — the tick mounts off, off arms nothing, on arms, a refused arming is said.
- **U5** [UI-5] an armed schedule edited out of one-app shows `CHANGES_CONTROL.armedLost` in the warning slot.
- **U6** [UI-6] arriving at `/schedule?history=<id>` scrolls to and focuses the history (`tabIndex={-1}`); the result page's link shows only when the schedule is armed or the run has `applied`; `CHANGES_LINK` → *this schedule’s changes you can still undo*.
- **U7** [UI-7] the result page's *stop* leaves a focused `role="status"` line (`ROW.stoppedOnItsOwn`); Settings' act leaves `SETTINGS.stoppedAll(n)` focused and catches a failure into words.
- **U8** [UI-8] the editor opens a disarmed task with the disarm line + `CHANGES_CONTROL.disarmedHint` (*save to keep it waiting for your OK, or choose make them on its own*).
- **U9** [UI-9] an existing schedule whose ask step GAINS *let it look things up* saves through the consent (the connections named, the cost confirmed).
- **U10** [UI-10] `resultFacts(run, appName, now)` counts a waiting batch only before its `expiresAt` (threaded through `resultSummary`, `historyLine`, the notification).
- **U11** [UI-11] `aria-describedby` from each new checkbox/select to the lines that explain it.
- **U12** [UI-12] `editorModel` re-exports `armableAppOf as onItsOwnApp` and `widens` from `taskShape.ts` (identity pin); the false "F13" comments deleted.
- **U13** [UI-13] the look-up bound counts only tools steps; `historyLine` drops an empty words segment; an arming refused after a create reads `ACTIONS.savedButWaits(refusal)`.
- **U14** [AUTH-F6, JOBS-F6 page side] `HELD_WHY.full(appName, live)` names the live count read at render; the capped line names the kind.

**Docs (Gate 6) — SEC-F5, AUTH-F8:** the delta adds (a) the per-turn taint does not stop an ARMED change copying shared rows into the reader's own tables, from which a later untainted fetch can carry them — and the first message's shared-table NAMES reach the brain before any read is recorded (the optional code fold making post-taint items unarmable is DECLINED: copying derived values into the reader's own tables is the use the read grant and the authority exist for; the channel is named — reversal word "taint-arm"); (b) the own journal holds before/after images of the user's own rows for seven days and rides every export and sync push; (c) an own-journal entry planted through the TRUSTED origin is an own-table write on one *undo* click, bounded by the bucket's namespace, `tableShape`, the live column re-check and the after-image match — no more than that origin's power to rewrite the file; (d) the stop window — a stop that lands between `stillArmed` and the seat still commits one run's ≤ `rowsPerRun`; (e) forward compatibility restated (AUTH-F8): a run that ran in the open app or read another app's data, and a task carrying `writeAuthority`, `disarmed` or a `tools` step, is refused by a pre-PR-4 reader — dropped from its history, deleted by its untrusted import (P-E pins the direction with a hand-copied pre-PR-4 step schema).

The invariant: every kit binding — artifact, artifact-static, file and the local runner's page (the MCP binding) — pins a host brain composed `tools: false` or the demo brain; `resolveBrain` honours the pin over the WebLLM flag; the classifier is skipped under the host brain and has no wire under the demo brain, so a kit chat turn never gets a lane and the tool-free builder never builds `access_propose`. Majors folded: the scoped copy's `query_only` would have refused the dry run; the copy drops triggers before the facts were read (now from the RAW copy and again on the live handle, with TEMP triggers, FK actions and `ON CONFLICT REPLACE`); sql.js `export()` closes and reopens the connection so `busy` was unobservable (now a per-namespace `inTransaction` flag; the read-side rollback named as pre-existing); the plan ran on 10 s-old cached bytes (now a fresh export); the refresh engine ignored `globalPause`, `allows('schedule')` and the absent state row (now three gates) and dropped the sticky posture D-PR1-3 built (kept for both postures); a reader could evict its harmful write's undo with twenty trivial ones (now a per-reader live cap that refuses); `requestedBy` and the refresh `summary` were app text crossing into another app (`oneLine` at the one place; `summary` cut from the wire); explicit defaults on the v2 record would have suspended every pre-upgrade backup on restore (now omitted, absent ≡ default); the write signal sat inside `runKvSet`, which the host's handshake shares (now in `handle()`); `accessOps` was permanent wire surface the `MALFORMED` fallback already covers (cut); a 25-function expression grammar against AC19's "literal-valued" (now literals, placeholders and simple comparisons); the sheet grew a checkbox grid on every table (now chips by default behind *choose columns…*); the egress block mis-filed the write/refresh lines (now the note under the toggle); the title and the allowed line never said "change" (now they do); *fetch now* broke the one-act rule (deferred with the other two *fetch* affordances); the kit estimate is +35–47 KB against 7,634 B — the ceiling conversation is certain and opens in the PR body.
